<result># WORKFLOWS and BUILD STAGES — flightcrew

## 1. What a "workflow" is

A **workflow** is a plain JavaScript file under `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/` (e.g. `fc-implement.js`) that runs inside Claude Code's own workflow runtime — not a shell script, not something `fc` executes. It is distributed by `fc distribute --apply` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/distribute.mjs:53-65`, `plannedCopies()`), which copies every `*.js` in `flightcrew/workflows/` into `&lt;target&gt;/workflows/` (default `$REPO/.claude/workflows/`). Once there, Claude Code runs it as a slash command, `/fc-implement`, `/fc-review`, `/fc-explore`, invoked with a structured `args` object.

Every script (`flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/README.md:11`) opens with `export const meta = { name, description, phases }` as its literal first statement — `name` must equal the filename — and ends with `export default result`. Inside, it can only call the runtime primitives it's handed as ambient bindings: `agent(prompt, options)` to dispatch one subagent (with `label`, `phase`, `schema`, `agentType`, `isolation`, `model`), `parallel([...])` to fan several out at once, `phase(name)`/`log(msg)` for reporting, and `args`/`budget` for input. A script is explicitly barred from doing real work itself: **it reads no file, runs no command, touches no git state, and has no clock or randomness** (`Date.now`, `new Date`, `Math.random` are all unavailable — enforced by testbench suite T29, see §2 below). Any timestamp has to arrive via `args.timestamp`.

Crucially: **a workflow only dispatches agents and returns a payload.** Nothing it returns is stored, and nothing counts as evidence, until the orchestrator (a human-supervised Claude Code session, `flightcrew/crew/orchestrator.md`) takes that payload, writes it to a file, and runs the matching `fc return &lt;kind&gt;` command, then lands green units with `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;` (README.md:9).

### The three scripts

| Script | Purpose | Key args | Returns |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fc-implement.js` | Dispatches one launch's implementer agents, wave by wave: pilot units first, then the rest of the wave in chunks of `implementers_concurrent` | `{ launch, units, implementers_concurrent, timestamp, agent_type?, model? }` | `{ workflow, launch, timestamp, dispatched, returns, halt, stopped_on }` |
| `fc-review.js` | Runs the whole critic → fix → re-verify loop for one launch, up to `critic_passes` passes | `{ launch, spec_path, critic_prompt_path, units, critic_passes, timestamp, model? }` | `{ workflow, launch, timestamp, passes, fixes, verifications, unrouted, observations, escalation, trigger }` |
| `fc-explore.js` | Fans one read-only explorer agent per open question, concurrently | `{ questions: [{id, question, stage, scope_paths}], timestamp?, model? }` | `{ workflow, timestamp, asked, answers, rejected }` |

### The "workflow shape" — when a launch actually uses a script here

Per `workflows/README.md:3`: a launch's kickoff fixes one of three "shapes":
- **`shape-session`** — the orchestrator dispatches subagents itself, directly, from its own session (no workflow script involved).
- **`shape-workflow`** — the orchestrator hands a whole wave to one of these scripts. This shape is chosen specifically **when a wave holds more units than the `implementers_concurrent` ceiling**.
- **`shape-sessions`** — the run is split across multiple sessions entirely.

So these three `.js` files are only invoked at all when a launch's kickoff picked the `shape-workflow` template — they are an optional acceleration path for large waves, not the only way flightcrew runs.

## 2. The build stages — the actual sequence from plan to green testbench

The canonical phase sequence lives in code as a literal array:

```js
// flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs:28
export const PHASES = ['targets', 'plan', 'contracts', 'implement', 'verify', 'review', 'report', 'ended'];
```

Walking it with what happens in each stage (per `flightcrew/crew/orchestrator.md:16-25` and `bin/cmd/launch.mjs`):

1. **targets** — the launch is opened (`fc launch new &lt;spec-path&gt;`) and its spec/tests-map targets are pinned.
2. **plan** — the orchestrator dispatches the `planner` agent, stores its returned plan with `fc plan write --stdin`, reads back `plan.md`. **Gate G1**: a human must `fc launch gate G1 approve` before any dispatch happens.
3. **contracts** — the plan's one `kind: "contracts"` unit (wave `W0`, no dependencies — e.g. `U0` in the real example) is rendered (`fc worker render &lt;id&gt;`), dispatched to one `implementer`, its return stored (`fc return worker … --unit`), and merged (`fc worker merge`). **Gate G2** halts here — a human approves before real implementation begins.
4. **implement** — this is the "seven implementation units in parallel, each refuted and fixed once" stage the `flightcrew-build` skill describes. Units are grouped into **waves** by `depends_on` depth (`fc-implement.js:164-180`, function `waves()`: "a unit runs one wave after the deepest unit it depends on"). Within a wave: units flagged `pilot: true` run **first**, and the rest of the wave is dispatched only if the pilots came back green; the rest run in **chunks of `implementers_concurrent`** (from `launch.json.ceilings`), the next chunk starting only once the current one has fully returned (`fc-implement.js:233-243`, function `run()`). In the real example (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/plan.json`): `U0` (contracts, wave 0) → `U1..U6` (kind `feature`, wave 1, all depending only on `U0`, with `U2` marked `pilot: true`) → `U7` (kind `integration`, wave 2, depending on `U1`..`U6`). Every implementer return is validated against the inlined worker-return schema; a return that's missing or malformed becomes a synthesized `status: "halt"` with `halt.kind: "budget"` (`fc-implement.js:199-213`, `haltReturn()`). Dispatch stops on the first `halt` from any unit, or the first `red` pilot return (`stopped_on` names the unit; `halt` is only filled for an actual halt).
5. **verify** — `fc launch phase verify`, then `fc verify`, which runs **checks, boundary, locked-paths and budget, in that order** (`bin/cmd/verify.mjs:61-66`): all four must be clean for exit 0. Where the plan calls for it, a dedicated `verifier` re-runs checks independently.
6. **review** — the refute-and-fix loop: `fc critic render` seals a critic dispatch, a critic pass runs, findings are routed by kind, fixes are dispatched, a verifier re-verifies, and — if findings remain — another pass runs, up to `ceilings.critic_passes`. This is exactly what `fc-review.js` automates (see below).
7. **report** — `fc launch phase report`, **Gate G3** halts for human sign-off.
8. **ended** — a human closes the run: `fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt;` (`accepted`, `accepted-with-reservations`, `abandoned`, `partial`).

So the shape described by the `flightcrew-build` skill — *"contracts first, seven implementation units in parallel each refuted and fixed once, then integration until the testbench is green"* — maps onto real phases **contracts → implement (waves, pilot-then-chunks) → review (the refute/fix/re-verify loop, capped at `critic_passes`) → verify**, with `U7` (`kind: integration`) as the final wave that only runs once every feature unit (`U1`-`U6`) is merged.

## 3. What a "worker" is

A "worker" is an `implementer` subagent (`flightcrew/crew/implementer.md`) building exactly one plan unit inside its own git worktree (`isolation: worktree`, `permissionMode: acceptEdits`, `maxTurns: 25`, `model: opus`).

**Rendering the dispatch.** `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` (`bin/cmd/worker.mjs:73-103`) refuses unless the launch is in phase `implement` and no abandon trigger has fired, then calls `workerPrompt()` (`bin/worker/render.mjs:181-203`). This builds a **sealed prompt**: the unit's intent, scope, constraints, interfaces, decisions (all pulled live from the pinned spec via `specBlocks()`), the exact `spec_refs` the unit must satisfy, its checks by **id only** (never the command text — `checkLines()`, `render.mjs:118-129`), its allowed `paths` and `depends_on`. The template used is `flightcrew/templates/worker-dispatch.template.md` (falls back to a built-in string `WORKER_BUILT_IN` if absent). The rendered prompt is **both printed and written to disk** at `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/returns/&lt;unit id&gt;.prompt.md` (`worker.mjs:96-100`) — this matters because a subagent's worktree branches from HEAD, so an uncommitted prompt file would be invisible to the worker; it must be committed on the run branch first.

**What the worker is told** (from the template, `templates/worker-dispatch.template.md`): build this unit and nothing else; auto-loaded project instructions that ask it to read other files don't apply to this role; create branch `git switch -c &lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit name&gt;`; run checks via `./flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc check &lt;ids&gt; --launch &lt;launch&gt;` (leading `./` matters — runs the worktree's own copy); stage only the unit's paths by name (never `git add -A`, never anything under `flightdeck/launch/`); commit with a quoted heredoc delimiter.

**What it must return** — the `worker-return` shape (`flightcrew/schemas/worker-return.schema.json`), required fields: `unit`, `status` (`green|red|halt`), `branch`, `worktree`, `spec_refs`, `checks` (`[{id: "T&lt;n&gt;", exit}]`), `artefacts`, `commits`, `iterations`, `halt` (`null` or `{kind, detail}` with `kind` ∈ `test-contradicts-spec, unsatisfiable, blocked, budget, boundary`), `notes`. `additionalProperties: false` everywhere — nothing extra is tolerated.

**Validation and recording.** The return is stored with `fc return worker &lt;file&gt; --unit &lt;id&gt;` (or `fc worker return &lt;unit&gt; &lt;file&gt;`, same code path — `worker.mjs:224-228` delegates to `return.mjs`'s `store()`). `storeReturn()` (`bin/worker/return.mjs:91-110`) validates against the schema (`loadSchema('worker-return')` + `validate()`); **an invalid return is never written and no event is appended** — it throws `ReturnError({blocking:true})`. Only on success does it write `launch/&lt;L&gt;/returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json` and append a `return` event with `{kind:"worker", status, unit}`.

**Merging.** `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;` (`worker.mjs:134-213`) is the landing step: it refuses if the stored return isn't `status: "green"`, if a dependency isn't merged yet, if the repo isn't on the run branch, or if the merge conflicts. On success it `git merge --no-ff --no-commit`, re-runs the unit's own checks against the *merged* tree (not just the worker's isolated worktree), commits with `--no-verify`, appends a `unit_merged` event with `{unit, branch, commit}`, then tears down the worktree/branch (best-effort, reporting any leftovers).

Real example (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/returns/U0.json`): `{"unit":"U0","status":"green","branch":"flightcrew-buildout-v1","worktree":".","spec_refs":["I2","I3",...],"checks":[{"id":"T26","exit":0}],"artefacts":[...16 files...],"commits":[],"iterations":1,"halt":null,"notes":"The nine new schemas and the seven check libraries every later unit imports. Units ran in the repository working tree rather than in per-unit worktrees, so the return carries no branch commits."}` — note this real run didn't actually use isolated worktrees (`worktree: "."`), which the note calls out explicitly.

An `explorer` worker (read-only, `model: haiku`, `maxTurns: 12`) works the same way for a question rather than a unit: dispatched via `fc-explore.js`, must return the `explorer-return` schema (`id` matching `X&lt;n&gt;`, `question`, `stage`, `answer`, `confidence` ∈ `certain|probable|guess`, `pointers`, `candidates`), stored with `fc return explorer &lt;file&gt; --id X&lt;n&gt;`.

## 4. Budgets

`fc budget` (`bin/cmd/budget.mjs`) counts what a run has spent against ceilings declared in `launch.json.ceilings`, writes `evidence/budget.json`, and fires an **abandon trigger** when any counter passes its ceiling.

Counters and how each is derived (`budget.mjs:1-107`, `countBudget()`):

| Counter | Source | Ceiling field |
|---|---|---|
| `agents` | count of `SubagentStart` events | `ceilings.agents` |
| `stop_blocks` | count of `stop_block` events | `ceilings.stop_blocks` |
| `consecutive_stop_blocks` | `stop_block` events since the newest `escalation`, `gate`, `phase`, or passing `check_run` event (`lastClearIndex()`) | `ceilings.gate_iterations` |
| `critic_passes` | number of `review/pass-*.json` files on disk | `ceilings.critic_passes` |
| `minutes` | span between the first and last non-`usage` event timestamp (`minutesSpanned()`) | `ceilings.minutes` |
| `tokens` | summed `input_tokens`+`output_tokens` from `usage` events, `'unobserved'` if none were ever recorded | `ceilings.tokens` |
| `tool_failures` | `PostToolUseFailure` events | (reported, not enforced) |
| `permission_denials` | `PermissionDenied` events | (reported, not enforced) |
| `compactions` | `PreCompact` events | (reported, not enforced) |

A counter whose ceiling is `null` is only reported, never enforced, and `'unobserved'` tokens are never compared against anything (`exceededCeilings()`, `budget.mjs:110-117`). When a ceiling is exceeded, `fc budget` appends a `trigger` event with `{name:"budget", detail: "&lt;name&gt; &lt;count&gt; above ceiling &lt;ceiling&gt;; ..."}` and exits with `EXIT.blocked`. Default ceilings baked in as `BUILT_IN_DEFAULTS` in `launch.mjs` include `agents: 12`, `implementers_concurrent: 4`, `turns_per_agent: 25`, `gate_iterations: 3`, etc. — so `implementers_concurrent` (the fan-out width) is itself one of the budget ceilings.

`fc verify` (§2/§5) always runs `fc budget` as its fourth and final stage (`verify.mjs:20-35`, spawning it as its own child process "so the budget counters stay in one place"), meaning a launch can't pass verification while over budget.

## 5. Parallelism, fan-out, and how results are merged/gated

**Waves are the top-level fan-out unit.** `fc-implement.js`'s `waves()` (lines 164-180) computes, for every plan unit, `depth = 1 + max(depth of every dependency)`; units sharing a depth form one wave. Waves run **strictly in order** (the `for (const wave of waves(units))` loop, lines 245-264) — a later wave never starts before an earlier one finishes (or halts).

**Within a wave, two levels of fan-out:**
1. **Pilots first.** `wave.filter(pilot === true)` runs via `parallel()` before the rest of the wave; if any pilot comes back `status: "red"`, the whole wave (and run) stops right there (`fc-implement.js:239-240`, checked only when `group === 'Pilots'`) — this is the "prove the risky bit works before committing the fleet" gate.
2. **Chunked concurrency for the rest.** The remaining units are split via `chunks(list, implementers_concurrent)` (lines 182-188) and each chunk is dispatched together with `await parallel(part.map(unit =&gt; () =&gt; build(unit, group)))` — genuine concurrent `agent()` calls — but **the next chunk only starts once the whole current chunk has returned** (line 234's `for (const part of chunks(...))` is sequential across chunks, parallel within one).

**Merging/gating results.** Every individual return is schema-validated in-line (`violations()`, a hand-rolled JSON-Schema subset checker duplicated verbatim in each script — types, enum, pattern, minLength, minimum, items, required, additionalProperties). A `null`/`undefined` or invalid return is converted into a synthetic `halt` (`haltReturn()`) rather than silently dropped, so a stuck subagent still produces a well-formed record. After each chunk, the aggregate `returns` array is scanned for the first `halt` (stops everything) or, for the pilot group only, the first `red` (stops the wave). The whole run's outcome is two independent fields: `halt` (non-null only for an actual halt return) and `stopped_on` (the unit id of *whatever* stopped it, halt or red) — the README stresses "test `stopped_on`, not `halt`, to tell a stopped run from a completed one."

The **critic/review loop** (`fc-review.js`) fans out differently: it's not unit-parallel by wave, it's *finding*-parallel within a pass. One critic runs per pass (not parallel), its `open` findings are bucketed by which unit's `paths` glob matches the finding's `file` (`owner()`, `covers()` — a small hand-written glob-to-regex translator supporting `**`, `*`, `?`, anchoring), and then **all units with routable findings are fixed concurrently** via `parallel(groupList.map(group =&gt; () =&gt; agent(fixPrompt(...))))` (line 461). Any fix agent that halts, or a `spec-conflict` finding, or a re-verification that `refuted: true`, immediately produces an `escalation` and stops the loop; reaching `critic_passes` with blocking findings still open produces a `trigger` instead. Findings owned by no unit land in `unrouted` for the human orchestrator to place by hand.

**Explorer fan-out** (`fc-explore.js`) is the simplest: every question is dispatched in one single `parallel()` call, all at once, no chunking, no waves, no halting logic — a bad/missing answer is simply moved to `rejected` and the run continues (`fc-explore.js:144-153`).

**The orchestrator's own gating** sits above all of this: it stores nothing itself (`fc plan write`, `fc launch note`, `fc return &lt;kind&gt;` are the only writers), and three hard human gates — G1 (post-plan), G2 (post-contracts), G3 (post-report) — plus any `halt` return or fired abandon trigger (`fc launch escalate &lt;kind&gt;`) force an immediate stop regardless of what a workflow script itself would have done next.</result>

# Flightcrew Isolation, Permissions and Security — Findings

All paths below are relative to `~/Projects/flightdeck/flightdeck/` unless given in full. All six hooks live in `flightcrew/hooks/`.

## 1. The six hooks

All six are wired in `flightcrew/hooks/settings.fragment.json` and share one runner in `flightcrew/hooks/lib.mjs` (`runHook`/`runGuard`, lib.mjs:198-260). That shared wrapper is what makes every hook behave the same way at the edges: it reads stdin, resolves the active launch from `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, hands the parsed envelope to the hook's own logic, and forces the exit code to either 0 or 2 — nothing else is ever returned, and every thrown error is swallowed and logged rather than allowed to crash the session (lib.mjs:230-249).

**event-log.mjs** — binds to `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop`, `TaskCreated`, `TaskCompleted`, `PostToolUseFailure`, `PermissionDenied`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `Stop`, `WorktreeRemove` (event-log.mjs:7-10). It never blocks anything — it only appends one JSON line to `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/events.jsonl` per recorded event, carrying `source: 'hook'` and a `detail` object limited to the keys that event's schema allows (event-log.mjs:26-56). On `SessionStart` it also prints a `systemMessage`: *"flightcrew: launch \&lt;name\&gt; is active in phase \&lt;phase\&gt;; hooks are enforcing its locks and boundary. If this session is not that run, run fc launch end or set FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=none."* (event-log.mjs:63-64). Always exits 0.

**lock-guard.mjs** — binds to `PreToolUse`, matcher `Edit|Write|NotebookEdit`. Full body (14 lines):
```js
await runGuard('lock-guard', (ctx, relative) =&gt; {
  const launch = ctx.launch.json ?? {};
  if (launch.phase === 'targets') return 0;
  const locked = launch.paths?.locked ?? [];
  if (!matchAny(locked, relative)) return 0;
  record(ctx, 'lock_denied', { path: relative });
  decide('deny', `${relative} is locked for launch ${ctx.launch.name}; report a wrong or unsatisfiable check instead of editing it`);
  return 0;
});
```
In every phase except `targets`, an edit whose repository-relative target matches one of the launch's `paths.locked` globs is denied, with a `lock_denied` event recorded first. It always exits 0 — the refusal itself travels on stdout as a JSON decision, not via the process exit code.

**boundary-guard.mjs** — binds to the same `PreToolUse` event/matcher. It computes the set of paths the current phase permits (`permitted()`, boundary-guard.mjs:10-19):
- in phase `targets`: `paths.locked` + the launch's own folder (`flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/**`) + the spec's canonical home (`flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;spec&gt;/**`)
- in phases `contracts`, `implement`, `verify`, `review` (`BOUNDARY_PHASES`, lib.mjs:19): `paths.allowed` + the launch's own folder
- any other phase: no boundary (`null`, meaning nothing is checked)

This only runs at all while `paths.enforce_boundary` is true. A target outside the permitted set is denied and a `boundary_denied` event recorded (boundary-guard.mjs:21-29). Deny message: *"`&lt;path&gt;` is outside the boundary of launch `&lt;name&gt;` in phase `&lt;phase&gt;`; work inside the unit's paths, or escalate if the boundary is wrong."*

Both guards share one decision shape emitted by `decide()` (lib.mjs:59-63):
```json
{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"&lt;text&gt;"}}
```
`permissionDecision` is `deny`, `ask`, or absent (silence = allow). `ask` is used when the launch is ambiguous (two `active` launches, or an unreadable `launch.json`) — the guard can't tell which run it's speaking for, so it hands the decision to a human rather than guessing (lib.mjs:239-243). Both guards are registered on the identical matcher and run in parallel; per the README, "a target that is both locked and outside the boundary draws a decision from each, and Claude Code takes the strictest."

**structural-check.mjs** — binds to `PostToolUse`, matcher `Edit|Write`. After an edit, it looks up `launch.json`'s `structural` map by the edited file's extension, substitutes the shell-quoted absolute path for `{file}`, and runs the command via `/bin/sh -c` with cwd at the git toplevel (structural-check.mjs:20-38). Exit 0 and silent on a zero exit; on non-zero it prints the last 20 combined stdout+stderr lines to stderr and **exits 2** — this is the one guard-adjacent hook that blocks by exit code rather than by a `deny` decision. No command configured for that extension → silent exit 0. Runs in every phase.

**stop-gate.mjs** — binds to `Stop`, with `"timeout": 600` (settings.fragment.json). It is the only hook whose timeout is raised above Claude Code's default, "because it runs real checks." It maps phase → gate module: `{ verify: 'acceptance-gate', contracts: 'contracts-gate' }` (stop-gate.mjs:11); every other phase is a no-op. Logic (stop-gate.mjs:66-141):
- an open `escalation.json` releases it immediately: records `stop_release`, exit 0
- no tests map pinned, or the gate module missing/throwing → logged to `hooks.log`, silent, exit 0 (never a block)
- runs the phase's gate, which returns `{ ran, checks: [...], extra: [...] }`
- if nothing is `blocking`: records one `check_run` event per check, exit 0
- if something blocks: counts consecutive `stop_block` events since the last passing `check_run`/`gate`/`escalation`/`phase` event (`consecutiveBlocks`, stop-gate.mjs:30-42); if the new count reaches `min(ceilings.stop_blocks, 8)` it instead records a `stall` then a `trigger` event, prints `stall: ... the gate is releasing the turn. Escalate with fc launch escalate, or end the launch.` to stderr, and exits **0** (handing control back to a human); otherwise it records `stop_block` (with the running count and failing check ids), prints each blocking item as `&lt;id&gt; exit &lt;code&gt;` plus its last 20 output lines, and exits **2**.

**session-end.mjs** — binds to `SessionEnd`. Best-effort, budgeted at 1500ms total (`BUDGET_MS`, session-end.mjs:10): it shells out to `fc evidence` then `fc report` with a shrinking timeout each, so the launch's evidence page and report survive the ending session. Failures only produce a `hooks.log` line, never a nonzero exit. Always exits 0.

## 2. Boundaries

A "boundary" is the set of repository paths a launch's active phase permits a worker to touch, computed by `boundary-guard.mjs`'s `permitted()` function from `launch.json`'s `paths` object:

```json
"paths": {
  "allowed": ["&lt;glob a unit may write, repository-relative&gt;"],
  "locked": ["&lt;glob no edit may touch while the launch is past phase targets&gt;"],
  "enforce_boundary": "&lt;boolean, true from the tests-map pin onwards&gt;"
}
```
(from `flightcrew/templates/launch.template.json`)

It is declared per-launch in `launch.json`, expressed as glob patterns in the dialect implemented by `flightcrew/checks/lib/glob-lib.mjs` (`**` any depth, `*` within a segment, a leading `/` anchors at repo root, a trailing `/` covers the directory and everything under it, no negation). Violations are detected two ways:

- **Live, per-edit:** `boundary-guard.mjs` denies the tool call before it happens (see §1).
- **Retrospectively, over the whole change set:** `fc boundary` (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/boundary.mjs`) computes every file changed since `lock_commit` (via `changedSince()` in git-lib.mjs — commits after the base, plus staged/unstaged/untracked files, excluding `.claude/worktrees/**` and `flightdeck/testbench/runs/**`) and flags any not matching `paths.allowed`, `paths.locked`, or the launch's own folder as `outside`. It writes `evidence/boundary.json` and exits 2 if anything is outside. The real example at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/evidence/boundary.json` shows this exact shape:
```json
{
  "base": "49aff47990c6b7df60c2da3f8a57fb20cb366e01",
  "changed": [ {"path": "...", "added": 128, "removed": 0}, ... ],
  "outside": [".claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js"]
}
```
Here one file (`.claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js`) was flagged outside the boundary even though everything else in the changed set matched an allowed/locked pattern.
- `fc boundary` is also run automatically inside the contracts-phase stop gate (`contracts-gate.mjs`'s `runFcBoundary()`) — a red boundary there is reported as a synthetic blocking item `{ id: 'boundary', verdict: 'fail', blocking: true }` in the gate's `extra` array, so a boundary breach can hold the turn just like a failing check.

The README calls out the one gap the live guard cannot see: a shell command (`sed -i`, a redirect, `git checkout` of a boundary/locked file) writes without going through `Edit`/`Write`/`NotebookEdit`, so no `PreToolUse` hook fires. Three backstops are meant to cover it together: `permissions.deny` rules on the locked paths (applies to file tools directly), a `sandbox.filesystem.deny` list (applies to Bash and children — shown as `_sandbox_example` in the fragment), and `fc locked`/`fc boundary` running inside `fc verify` so a shell-level write is still caught before a run is accepted.

## 3. Locked files/suites

"Locked" is a subset of paths (`paths.locked` in `launch.json`) that no edit tool may touch once the launch is past its `targets` phase — this typically protects the frozen spec, the pinned tests map, and the test suite files themselves, so an implementer cannot rewrite the tests it is being judged against.

- **Live enforcement:** `lock-guard.mjs` denies any `Edit`/`Write`/`NotebookEdit` whose repository-relative target matches a `paths.locked` glob, in every phase except `targets` (lock-guard.mjs:8, quoted in full above). It records a `lock_denied` event and replies with the deny message *"`&lt;path&gt;` is locked for launch `&lt;name&gt;`; report a wrong or unsatisfiable check instead of editing it."* The deny message is deliberate: it tells the agent the correct response to a bad lock is to escalate (`fc launch escalate wrong-check`), not to edit around it.
- **Retrospective detection:** `fc locked` (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/locked.mjs`) reuses the same `changeSet()` as `fc boundary`, filters it to paths matching a `paths.locked` glob, writes `evidence/locked.json`, and exits 2 if any locked path changed by any means (commit, staged, unstaged, or untracked). The real example at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/evidence/locked.json` shows `"locked": []` — a clean run where nothing locked was touched, over the same 150+ file change set as the boundary example. Per the README, `fc locked` "runs inside `fc verify`, so a shell write is caught before a run can be accepted" — this is the backstop for the guard's blind spot (a shell command bypassing Edit/Write).
- The word also appears for git worktrees: `git worktree unlock` is called during `fc worker merge` cleanup (worker.mjs:195) as ordinary git worktree locking, unrelated to `paths.locked`.

## 4. Worktree isolation

Yes — the system uses git worktrees to isolate parallel implementer subagents, one per plan unit, each on its own branch:

- `worktree.baseRef: "head"` is set in the settings fragment. Per the README: "Keep `worktree.baseRef` at `head`, or worker subagents branch from the remote default branch and never see the run's branch." This is a harness-level `WorktreeCreate` setting Claude Code itself consumes — it is *not* hookable. The README explains why: "A `WorktreeCreate` command hook does not observe worktree creation — it replaces it, and must print the directory it created. Any non-zero exit aborts the creation. A logging hook attached there would break every isolated subagent." So `WorktreeCreate` is deliberately left unhooked, while `WorktreeRemove` is recorded normally by `event-log.mjs`.
- Worktrees live under `.claude/worktrees/&lt;name&gt;/`, which is required to be in `.gitignore` (README installation step 3) and is excluded from every changed-path computation (`git-lib.mjs`'s `EXCLUDED = ['.claude/worktrees/**', 'flightdeck/testbench/runs/**']`).
- The guards resolve a target file to a repository-relative path using `toplevel()`/`repoRelative()` in `git-lib.mjs`, which specifically special-cases worktrees: if `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails (as for a not-yet-created subdirectory) but the path sits under `&lt;projectDir&gt;/.claude/worktrees/&lt;name&gt;/`, that worktree directory is treated as the root (git-lib.mjs:108-121). This is why the guards work correctly for edits made *inside* a worktree — the hooks-guards testbench suite has explicit cases (`lock-worktree-target-relative-to-worktree-root`, `boundary-worktree-target-relative-to-worktree-root`) proving a locked/boundary path denies correctly whether the edit is made from the worktree root or a subdirectory of it, with relative paths resolved against the worktree.
- **Dispatch:** `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` (worker.mjs:73-103) checks the phase is `implement` and no abandon trigger has fired, then writes the unit's sealed prompt to `returns/&lt;id&gt;.prompt.md` in the launch folder before printing it — the comment explains: "a subagent worktree branches from HEAD, so the prompt file has to exist in the run branch before the dispatch names it by repository path."
- **Merge and teardown:** `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;` (worker.mjs:134-213) enforces a strict sequence: no fired trigger, a stored `green` return, all dependencies already merged, repo currently on the run branch, unit branch exists — then `git merge --no-ff --no-commit`, re-runs the unit's own checks against the merged tree, aborts on conflict or red checks, commits with `--no-verify` only once green, records a `unit_merged` event, then tears down: `git worktree unlock`, `git worktree remove --force`, `git worktree prune`, `git branch -D &lt;unit-branch&gt;`. Failures to clean up are reported as warnings rather than failing the merge, and `fc launch end` prints the leftover `git worktree remove`/cleanup commands for a human (launch.mjs:769-779).

## 5. The stop gate

The stop gate (`stop-gate.mjs`) fires on the `Stop` hook event and decides whether the turn is allowed to end. Only two phases are gated at all:

- **Phase `verify`** runs `acceptance-gate.mjs`: it looks up the acceptance check id (map's own `acceptance` field, else the launch's, else falls back to `T1` — `acceptanceId()`), runs it through `fc check`, and blocks if its verdict is `fail` or `error`.
- **Phase `contracts`** runs `contracts-gate.mjs`: it runs the checks belonging to the plan unit whose `kind` is `contracts` (or the acceptance check as fallback if the plan declares `no_contracts`), applying spec B40's rule — a check with verdict `error` always blocks; a check that `fail`s blocks only if its baseline's `expect` starts with `pass` (an expected failure does not block) — and additionally runs `fc boundary`, reporting a red boundary as a synthetic blocking item `{ id: 'boundary', ... }`.
- Every other phase (`targets`, `plan`, `implement`, `review`, `report`, `ended`) is a complete no-op: exit 0, nothing printed, nothing recorded.

Before running any gate it checks for an open `escalation.json` — if present, it records `stop_release` and returns 0 unconditionally, meaning a human's escalation always wins over a red gate.

If the gate is red, it must be true that the consecutive-block count hasn't reached the ceiling. That count is `min(launch.ceilings.stop_blocks, 8)` (`MAX_BLOCKS = 8` is a hard ceiling regardless of what the launch config asks for). Below the ceiling: record `stop_block` with the running count, print `&lt;id&gt; exit &lt;code&gt;` plus the check's last 20 combined output lines to stderr, exit 2 (blocking the turn from ending). At the ceiling: record `stall` then `trigger`, print a message pointing at `fc launch escalate` or ending the launch, and exit 0 — releasing the agent rather than looping it forever. The block count resets whenever a `gate`, `phase`, or `escalation` event, or a passing `check_run`, is newer than the run of blocks (`consecutiveBlocks()`, stop-gate.mjs:30-42).

## 6. The constitution fragment

`flightcrew/templates/constitution-fragment.md` is short (7 lines) and states the rules an agent operating in this repo must follow, as plain prose rather than code:

- Runs live under `flightdeck/launch/`; only one launch is `active` at a time; `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;` pins a session to one launch regardless of status, and `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=none` turns off enforcement for that session.
- The runner is invoked by path (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc`), never installed on PATH — `fc launch status` reports the active launch and phase.
- It tells the agent where to read next before acting: which manual to read at which stage, and to read `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` before planning any run.
- It states the lock/boundary rule in prose form matching the guards' actual behavior: locked paths are refused past the `targets` phase; paths outside `paths.allowed` are refused from the point the tests map is pinned. Where an edit is refused, the correct response is `fc launch escalate wrong-check --detail "&lt;text&gt;"`, not editing the refused file anyway.
- It forbids hand-editing generated artifacts (plans, kickoffs, reports, the evidence page) — those are only ever produced by `fc plan render`, `fc launch kickoff`, `fc report`, `fc evidence`.

## 7. The overall security story

Each layer defends against a distinct failure mode a semi-autonomous coding agent can produce. `lock-guard` and `boundary-guard` (PreToolUse, deny/ask) stop a worker from silently rewriting the tests it's being judged against or wandering outside the files a unit was scoped to touch — the two most direct ways an agent could "cheat" its own acceptance criteria. `structural-check` (PostToolUse, exit 2) catches a syntactically broken file the instant it's written, before the mistake compounds across further edits. `stop-gate` (Stop, exit 2/0, with an escalation/stall escape valve) prevents an agent from declaring a phase done while its acceptance or contracts checks are still red, while a hard ceiling (`min(ceilings.stop_blocks, 8)`) stops it from being trapped in an infinite retry loop — that failure is handed back to a human via a `trigger` event instead. `event-log` and `session-end` exist not to block anything but to guarantee an audit trail and a rendered evidence page survive the session regardless of how it ends, so a run's history can't be silently lost. Underneath all of this, the "no-op rule" (silence whenever a launch can't be confidently resolved) and the retrospective `fc boundary`/`fc locked` commands (run inside `fc verify`) form the failure-safe backstop: hooks never crash or hang a session, and the harness-level `permissions.deny` plus `sandbox.filesystem.deny` rules close the one gap the guards structurally can't see — a shell command that writes a locked or out-of-boundary file without ever calling `Edit`/`Write`/`NotebookEdit`.</result>

# 1. Kickoff: how a run is started

**"Kickoff"** is the standing instruction sheet an orchestrator (a Claude Code session, or set of sessions) follows for the whole duration of one run. It lives at `flightdeck/launch/&lt;launch-name&gt;/kickoff.md`. Nothing else in the system reads it — not the verifier, not the critic — only the orchestrator conducting the run. Per the library README (`flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/README.md:3`):

&gt; "the spec says what must be true, the plan says how this run is cut, the project constitution ... says how the project always works, and the kickoff says only how to conduct a run of this shape."

**Composition.** `kickoff.md` is never hand-written — it is *rendered* by `fc launch new` / `fc launch kickoff` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/launch.mjs:161-183`, function `renderKickoff`). It is assembled from three kinds of library part, always in this order:

1. **A header block**, generated code (not a file), carrying: the launch path, the spec path with its pinned commit, the tests-map path with its commit (or `(none)`), the assembled `kickoff version` string, a `read first` pointer to `RUNLOG.md`, a `prior reports` list (other launches of the same spec that already have a `report.md`), and `write plan with: fc plan write` / `evidence: &lt;path&gt;/evidence.html`.
2. **`base.md`** — what every run shares regardless of shape or task: the 7-step Conduct sequence with its three halting gates, the Escalate rule, Roles, Communication, Budgets, Outputs, and Never lists.
3. **Exactly one `shape-*.md`** — how work is dispatched.
4. **Exactly one `task-*.md`** — how the work is cut.

Each part file's very first line must be `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;` (nothing else on that line). The assembled kickoff's version string is the `+`-joined `part@version` list, e.g. `base@1+shape-workflow@1+task-feature@1` (seen literally in `flightcrew-buildout/launch.json:21` and in the rendered `kickoff.md:3`). This version is stored in three places: `launch.json.kickoff.version`, `plan.json.kickoff_version`, and the run-log entry — so any run can be traced back to exactly which conduct rules it followed. Parts are selected on the command line as `--kickoff base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;`; the default is `base+shape-session+task-feature` (`launch.mjs:45`).

A rule in the README: "`kickoff.md` is rendered, never hand-edited. A conduct change is an edit to a part here, with that part's version bumped; the next render picks it up" — and `validate-kickoff.mjs` checks a rendered kickoff for a valid header, resolvable pointers, and that every backticked name under `## Roles` has a matching `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/&lt;name&gt;.md` file.

**The three shapes** (`shape-session.md`, `shape-sessions.md`, `shape-workflow.md`) answer "how are units dispatched, what counts as progress, how does the run stop":

- **`shape-session`** (default): one Claude Code session dispatches subagents directly, one per unit, worktree-isolated. Used when every parallel wave fits inside the `implementers_concurrent` ceiling. Progress = stored returns + `events.jsonl`, summarised by `fc events summary`; "the session's own scrollback is not evidence." The three gates halt the session's own turn.
- **`shape-sessions`**: the run is split across *multiple* Claude Code sessions against the *same* launch — one conducting session owning the gates, plus one further session per named "stream" of units (assigned via `fc launch note`, opened with `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;launch&gt;` set). Only the conducting session stores returns, merges units, records gates, and calls `fc launch end`. Used when a set of units needs its own context window across hours rather than turns.
- **`shape-workflow`**: used when a wave holds *more* units than the `implementers_concurrent` ceiling. The orchestrator session keeps the gates and every `fc` command, but hands the fan-out to a dynamic workflow script (`/fc-implement`, `/fc-review`, `/fc-explore`), copies of which `fc distribute --apply` places in the harness's workflows folder. This is what both real examples used (`kickoff version: base@1+shape-workflow@1+task-feature@1`).

**The four task types** (`task-feature.md`, `task-migration.md`, `task-audit.md`, `task-agent.md`) answer "how are units cut, what does wave 0 owe, what risks are worth naming":

- **`task-feature`**: cut vertically, one testable behaviour per unit; wave 0 is the single `kind: contracts` unit; the last wave holds one `kind: proof` unit carrying the acceptance check `T1`.
- **`task-migration`**: a mechanical sweep; wave 0 lands the new form plus a compatibility surface; the last wave deletes the compatibility surface and proves the old form is gone; "a unit that finds a defect on the way converts the call site as it stands and names the defect in its return notes" — never fixes it inline.
- **`task-audit`**: no code changes — cut by question, each unit needing a check id from the pinned tests map that *reproduces* its finding; wave 0 builds a shared harness under `audit/harness/`; later units write only to `audit/findings/&lt;unit&gt;/`.
- **`task-agent`**: for building agent-shaped products (prompts, agents, workflows) whose output varies between runs; behaviours are tagged `[deterministic]`, `[property]`, `[statistical]`, `[judged]`, each with different evidentiary obligations, and a judged verdict is appended via `fc events append &lt;json&gt; --stated`.

**Conduct sequence (base.md), concretely, in 7 steps:** (1) `fc launch status`, read spec/tests-map/run-log, dispatch explorers for facts the plan needs, store answers via `fc return explorer &lt;file&gt; --id X&lt;n&gt;`; (2) dispatch planner, `fc plan write` → **HALT: Gate 1** (human approves/exits plan.md against spec); (3) Wave 0 contracts unit, dispatch one implementer, `fc worker merge` → **HALT: Gate 2** (stop-gate hook runs checks + boundary); (4) Waves 1..n, pilot units first then chunks capped by `implementers_concurrent`; (5) Verify: `fc launch phase verify`, `fc verify`, optional verifier dispatch; (6) Review: `fc critic render --pass &lt;n&gt;`, dispatch critic, route findings back to owning implementer or escalate spec conflicts; (7) Report: `fc launch phase report` → **HALT: Gate 3** (human reads ledger/findings/cost, runs `fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt;`).

# 2. The launch folder anatomy

Every run lives at `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/`, created by `fc launch new &lt;spec-path&gt;` (default name `&lt;spec.name&gt;-&lt;n&gt;`, e.g. `flightcrew-v1-1`, or overridden with `--name`, as in the real examples `flightcrew-buildout` / `flightcrew-buildout-2`). Per `flightdeck/launch/README.md:13-23`, the anatomy is:

```
&lt;L&gt;/
  launch.json      state: status, phase, pins, paths, ceilings, gates, outcome
  kickoff.md       the conduct rules, rendered by fc launch kickoff
  specs/&lt;S&gt;/       pinned copies of the spec and the tests map
  plan.json plan.md   the plan (fc plan write); plan.md is rendered
  events.jsonl hooks.log   what the hooks and fc recorded
  evidence/ evidence.html  check results, boundary, locked, budget; the evidence page
  returns/ review/ notes.md   stored returns, critic passes, orchestrator notes
  report.md        the run report, final at fc launch end
```

Confirmed on disk in `flightcrew-buildout/` and `flightcrew-buildout-2/`: `events.jsonl`, `evidence/` (with `boundary.json`, `budget.json`, `locked.json`, `summary.json`, one `T&lt;n&gt;.json` per check), `evidence.html`, `kickoff.md`, `launch.json`, `notes.md`, `plan.json`, `plan.md`, `report.md`, `returns/`, `review/`, `specs/&lt;spec-name&gt;/spec.v1.json` + `tests-map.v&lt;n&gt;.json`.

**`launch.json` fields** (schema at `flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/launch.schema.json`; template at `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/launch.template.json`):

- `schema_version`, `name` (folder name).
- `status`: `draft | active | accepted | accepted-with-reservations | abandoned | partial` — only one launch may be `active` at a time (enforced by `checkLaunches` in `doctor.mjs:128`).
- `phase`: `targets → plan → contracts → implement → verify → review → report → ended`, strictly ordered; `fc launch phase &lt;p&gt;` only accepts the immediately next phase unless `--force`.
- `created` (date launch opened).
- `spec: {name, version, commit, file_commit, path}` — `commit` is the spec file's own frozen commit header, null while draft; `file_commit` is `git log -1` for the file, informational.
- `tests_map: {version, commit, path}` or `null` before pinning.
- `kickoff: {path, version}` — the assembled `part@version+...` string.
- `base_commit` (HEAD when opened), `lock_commit` (HEAD when the tests map was pinned — the base for boundary/locked/critic-diff calculations, null until pinned).
- `branch` (integration branch, `run/&lt;name&gt;` by default — though both real examples instead used `flightcrew-buildout-v1` as a shared branch, a deviation the buildout-2 report notes as an observation).
- `previous_launch` (newest other launch of the same spec, or null — e.g. `flightcrew-buildout-2.launch.json` has `"previous_launch": "flightcrew-buildout"`).
- `allow_draft` (true when a human accepted a draft spec/map with `--allow-draft`).
- `paths: {allowed: [globs], locked: [globs], enforce_boundary: bool}` — `enforce_boundary` flips true only at the tests-map pin.
- `acceptance` (the acceptance check id, e.g. `"T1"`).
- `structural: {".mjs": "node --check {file}", ...}` — one lint-style command per extension, run after every edit.
- `ceilings: {agents, implementers_concurrent, turns_per_agent, gate_iterations, stop_blocks (max 8), critic_passes, minutes, tokens, expected_tokens}` — defaults from `BUILT_IN_DEFAULTS` in `launch.mjs:48-67` (agents:12, implementers_concurrent:4, turns_per_agent:25, gate_iterations:3, stop_blocks:8, critic_passes:2, minutes:240).
- `gates: {G1, G2, G3}` each `{status: pending|approved|exited, at, note?}`.
- `outcome` (set only by `fc launch end`): `accepted | accepted-with-reservations | abandoned | partial`.
- `ended` (ISO timestamp, null until ended).
- `accepted_units` / `abandoned_units` (only on a `partial` ending).
- `landed: {commit, pr, integration_check}` (written by `fc launch land`).

**Gates in practice** (from `flightcrew-buildout/launch.json:100-114`): G1 approved with a human-written `note` explaining what was read; G2 approved similarly after wave 0 landed; G3 left `pending` because the run was abandoned before reaching it.

**Pinning tests-map (`fc launch pin tests-map`, `launch.mjs:412-457`)** is the moment `paths.allowed`/`locked` are populated from the map's `allowed_paths`/`locked_paths` (plus two glob locks the pin always adds: the launch's own `specs/**` and the canonical spec folder's `specs/&lt;S&gt;/**`), `enforce_boundary` flips true, and `lock_commit` is set to current HEAD — this is the freeze point past which the critic's diff and the boundary/locked checks are all measured.

# 3. System outputs

**`events.jsonl`** — one JSON object per line, the immutable factual record (schema at `event.schema.json`). Required fields: `ts, event, launch, phase, source, detail`; optional `session_id, agent_id, agent_type`. `source` is one of `hook` (a Claude Code hook fired it), `fc` (an `fc` command wrote it), or `stated` (a human recorded something that happened outside the hooks, via `fc events append --stated`). Recognized hook events: `SessionStart, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentStop, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted, PostToolUseFailure, PermissionDenied, PreCompact, PostCompact, Stop, WorktreeRemove`. Synthetic events written by `fc` itself: `gate, phase, stop_block, stop_release, stall, trigger, escalation, lock_denied, boundary_denied, check_run, return, unit_merged, usage, launch_end`. Real lines from `flightcrew-buildout/events.jsonl`:

```
{"ts":"...","event":"phase","launch":"flightcrew-buildout","phase":"plan","source":"fc","detail":{"from":"targets","to":"plan","forced":false}}
{"ts":"...","event":"gate","...","detail":{"gate":"G1","decision":"approve","note":"Plan read against the spec: ..."}}
{"ts":"...","event":"return","...","detail":{"kind":"worker","status":"halt","unit":"U2"}}
{"ts":"...","event":"escalation","...","detail":{"kind":"wrong-check","detail":"Two locked suite cases cannot be satisfied..."}}
{"ts":"...","event":"launch_end","phase":"ended","detail":{"outcome":"abandoned","at":"verify"}}
```
`fc events summary` tallies by event type and by agent; `fc events append/usage` write new lines. A sibling `hooks.log` carries raw `&lt;iso ts&gt; &lt;hook name&gt; &lt;message&gt;` lines (not seen populated in these examples since the buildout sessions ran without hooks installed — a fact recorded by hand in `notes.md`).

**`evidence/&lt;T&gt;.json`** — one file per check id, schema `check-result.schema.json`: `{id, command, cwd, exit, verdict (pass|fail|error|skipped), stdout_tail, stderr_tail (last 40 lines each), duration_ms, ran_at, commit, covers (spec node ids), phase}`. Real example, `T1.json`: `"command": "node flightdeck/testbench/suites/e2e/run.mjs"`, `"verdict": "pass"`, `"covers": ["B34"]`.

**`evidence/summary.json`** — rebuilt from every evidence file present: `{ran_at, commit, counts: {pass,fail,error,skipped}, checks: [{id, verdict, covers}], unverified, quarantined, uncovered}`. Real example shows 27 pass / 2 fail (`T11`, `T24` both `"verdict": "fail"`).

**`evidence/boundary.json`, `locked.json`, `budget.json`** — additional evidence artifacts: boundary lists every changed path with added/removed line counts and whether it's `outside` the allowed globs; locked lists paths matching a locked glob; budget snapshots every ceiling against its count (`agents 9/12`, `stop_blocks 0/8`, `minutes 6/240`, etc.) and an `exceeded: []` array.

**`evidence.html`** — one self-contained, no-network HTML page rendered by `fc evidence` (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/evidence.mjs`), rewritten "at every check, gate, phase change, return and ending." Fixed section order (`SECTIONS` array, `evidence.mjs:12-22`): `Unverified, Quarantined, Test-file changes, Boundary, Changed since lock, Findings, Phases, Agents, Failures`, followed by a `Checks` section with every check's raw stdout/stderr tail. It exists so a human reviewer never has to read raw JSON — it's the canonical "what happened" display; per `shape-session.md`: "the evidence page is the display, and the session's own scrollback is not evidence."

**`report.md`** — the run report, rendered by `fc launch end` (and standalone by `fc report`) from `report.template.md`'s 8 fixed headings, each carrying a provenance tag telling the reader what kind of claim it is:
```
## Ledger [checked · reviewed · stated]
## Verification [checked]
## Review [reviewed]
## Phases [recorded · stated]
## Agents [recorded · stated]
## Failures and interventions [recorded]
## Orchestrator notes [stated]
```
Critically: "The report never states an acceptance verdict: it records what was checked, what was reviewed and what was merely stated, and the human decides." The Ledger is one row per plan unit (unit/kind/checks+verdicts/branch/merge-commit/return-status), e.g. from the real example: `U2 | feature | T1 pass, ... T11 fail, ... | flightcrew-buildout-v1 | — | halt`. It also lists `merged`, `open`, `unverified`, and `attempted and discarded` (worktrees removed without a green return or merge). Verification pulls straight from `evidence/summary.json`'s counts. Review joins every critic finding with its resolution state from `review/resolutions.json`. Orchestrator notes is literally the contents of `notes.md` verbatim.

**`RUNLOG.md`** — the cross-run history at `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md`, one entry per ended run inserted newest-first right after the `# Run log` heading, by `fc launch end` → `fc runlog stub` (`runlog.mjs`). Mechanical fields (spec, kickoff version, outcome, cost) are auto-filled; diagnosis fields arrive as literal `&lt;fill&gt;` for a human to complete: `kept`/`reservation` for accepted runs, or `seen on`/`cause`/`fixed on`/`change`/`watch`/`kept`/`promote` (plus `landed`/`abandoned` unit lists) for abandoned/partial runs. `symptom` is pre-filled from the recorded ending event, never hand-written. Real entries in `RUNLOG.md` show this exact shape, e.g.:
```
## 2026-09-03 · flightcrew-v1 · flightcrew-buildout
...
symptom: abandoned at verify — escalation wrong-check: Two locked suite cases cannot be satisfied by any faithful implementation. ...
```
The planner reads this file (via `fc runlog show --spec &lt;S&gt;`) *before* writing a new plan for the same spec, which is why the header block's `read first:` field points at it. It closes with the newest critic pass's `observation`-kind findings as bullet points.

**`notes.md`** — free-text orchestrator notes appended by `fc launch note &lt;text&gt;` (`launch.mjs:670-679`), printed verbatim into the report's last section. Real example explains hook-installation gaps and why no unit branches were created.

**`returns/`** — every agent's stored return, one JSON file per unit/explorer/verifier, written via `fc return worker|explorer|verifier &lt;file&gt; --unit/--id/--pass &lt;n&gt;`. Real `U2.json`: `{unit, status: "halt", branch, spec_refs, checks: [{id, exit}], artefacts, commits: [], iterations, halt: {kind: "test-contradicts-spec", detail: "..."}, notes}`.

**`review/`** — critic pass files `pass-&lt;n&gt;.json` (schema-shaped `{verdict, pass, findings: [{id, kind, severity, spec_ref, file, line, text, state, resolved_commit, dispute}]}`) plus `resolutions.json` layering resolution state on top. Real `pass-1.json` from buildout-2 shows findings of kind `scope-violation`, `correctness-gap`, `spec-conflict`, `observation`, with severities `blocking`/`non-blocking` and states `open`/`disputed`/`resolved`.

**Who reads/writes what, summary:**
| artifact | writer | reader |
|---|---|---|
| `events.jsonl` | hooks + `fc` commands + `fc events append --stated` | `fc events summary`, `report.mjs`, `evidence.mjs`, `runlog.mjs` (symptom line) |
| `evidence/*.json`, `summary.json` | `fc check`/`fc verify` | `fc launch phase review` blockers, `report.mjs`, `evidence.mjs`, `fc launch end`/`land` (staleness/fail checks) |
| `evidence.html` | `fc evidence` (called by every state-changing command via `bestEffortRender`) | human reviewer at every gate |
| `report.md` | `fc launch end` / `fc report` | human at Gate 3, and anyone auditing the run afterward |
| `RUNLOG.md` | `fc launch end` → `fc runlog stub`; human fills `&lt;fill&gt;` | planner (before writing the next plan for the same spec) |
| `notes.md` | `fc launch note` (orchestrator) | `report.md`'s last section |
| `returns/` | `fc return worker/explorer/verifier` | `report.md` ledger, `fc launch end partial` blockers |
| `review/pass-*.json`, `resolutions.json` | `fc return critic` | `report.md` Review section, `RUNLOG.md` observations, `fc launch end partial` blocking-finding check |

# 4. Lifecycle: launch → ended

1. **`fc launch new &lt;spec-path&gt;`** — validates the spec has a name, picks/validates a folder name, renders `kickoff.md` *before* the folder is created (so a broken kickoff part refuses with nothing written), copies the spec into `specs/&lt;S&gt;/`, creates `evidence/`, `returns/`, `review/`, an empty `events.jsonl`, and writes `launch.json` with `status: draft, phase: targets`. Prints `export FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;`.
2. **`fc launch activate [--allow-draft]`** — refuses if the spec/tests-map isn't frozen (unless `--allow-draft`), refuses if another launch is already `active`. Flips `status: active`.
3. **`fc launch pin tests-map &lt;path&gt;`** — freezes `paths.allowed/locked`, sets `enforce_boundary: true`, records `lock_commit = HEAD`, re-renders the kickoff.
4. **Phase progression `targets → plan → contracts → implement → verify → review → report → ended`** via `fc launch phase &lt;p&gt;`, each move gated by precondition checks (`planBlockers`, `reviewBlockers` in `launch.mjs:498-544`) and appending a `phase` event; an unresolved abandon trigger blocks every move except to `ended`.
5. **Gates G1/G2/G3** via `fc launch gate &lt;G&gt; approve|exit`, each writes a `gate` event (and a `phase` event when it advances the phase); G2-approve is additionally blocked (`gate2Blockers`) until wave 0's checks are green and post-lock.
6. **Escalation** (`fc launch escalate &lt;kind&gt; --detail "…"`) — writes an `escalation` event and an escalation marker file, cleared on the next phase/gate move; kinds: `spec-gap, wrong-check, blocked, trigger, budget, halt`.
7. **`fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt;`** — the terminal act. For the accepted family it first demands a matching, non-stale `evidence/summary.json` and a clean working tree under the allowed paths (excluding the launch's own folder); for `partial` it demands `plan.json` and validates dependency/merge/finding constraints (`partialBlockers`). It then sets `status = outcome`, `phase = "ended"`, `ended = &lt;now&gt;`, writes a `launch_end` event, and — best-effort but tracked for failure — re-renders `evidence.html`, re-renders `report.md`, and inserts the `RUNLOG.md` stub. Finally it prints worktree-removal and branch-deletion commands for a human to run by hand (cleanup is never automatic).
8. **`fc launch land --commit &lt;sha&gt;`** — optional, separate from ending: records where the run's changes actually landed (`landed.commit`, `.pr`, `.integration_check`), guarded by requiring fresh, all-green evidence at that commit.

There is no automatic archival step visible in this scope — `flightdeck/launch/README.md` describes only the `&lt;L&gt;/` folder persisting after `ended`, with cleanup lines printed for the human, and a top-level `runs.keep` placeholder that simply keeps the directory tracked in git. (A `flightdeck/blackbox/` directory exists with an `archive.keep` placeholder file, suggesting a separate archival mechanism, but that is outside this assignment's read boundary/scope and I did not explore it further.)

**Key file:line references for follow-up reading:**
- Kickoff rendering: `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/launch.mjs:117-197` (`partVersion`, `loadParts`, `priorReports`, `renderKickoff`, `writeKickoff`)
- Phase/gate logic: `launch.mjs:498-653`
- Ending logic: `launch.mjs:786-861`
- Kickoff validation rules 1–5: `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-kickoff.mjs:5-11,169-220`
- Launch validation rules 1–5: `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-launch.mjs:5-10,94-173`
- Report section builders: `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/report.mjs:110-260`
- Evidence page builders: `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/evidence.mjs:12-247`
- RUNLOG stub logic: `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/runlog.mjs:90-149`</result>
