<result># Report: the `fc` command-line tool

Note on path: the assignment says `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc`, but in this checkout the repo root itself is named `flightdeck` and it contains a second `flightdeck/` subfolder, so the real path is `~/Projects/flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc`. All paths below are given relative to that inner `flightdeck/` folder (i.e. `flightcrew/bin/fc.mjs` means `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc.mjs`).

## 1. What `fc` is, and how the launch root/active launch resolve

`fc` is the command-line front end for "flightcrew," a small orchestration system that runs a multi-agent build against a frozen spec: it opens a "launch" (a run folder), pins a spec and a tests map to it, dispatches implementer/critic/verifier agents, runs checks, and produces evidence/report documents. Every piece of state lives on disk under `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/`.

**Invocation — shim vs entry point.**
- `flightcrew/bin/fc` (`bin/fc:1-4`) is a one-line POSIX shell shim:
  ```sh
  #!/bin/sh
  exec node "$(dirname "$0")/fc.mjs" "$@"
  ```
  It exists purely so the tool can be run as `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc &lt;command&gt;` from anywhere without a package install step.
- `flightcrew/bin/fc.mjs` (`bin/fc.mjs:1-378`) is the real entry point. It is only invoked directly (`invokedDirectly()`, `fc.mjs:364-370`, checks that `process.argv[1]` really is this file) — when imported as a module (e.g. by other commands calling `renderThrough`) it does not call `main()`.

**Global flags.** Two flags can appear anywhere in the argument list and are stripped out before the command name is read: `--json` (turns on JSON output mode via `setJson`) and `--launch &lt;name&gt;` / `--launch=&lt;name&gt;` (forces which launch folder to act on by setting `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH` for this process). This parsing happens in `takeGlobals()` (`fc.mjs:257-276`).

**Resolving the launch root** — `resolveRoot()` in `checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs:100-112`, called from `fc.mjs:312`. It checks, in this fixed precedence order (`ROOT_SOURCES`, `launch-lib.mjs:31`):
1. `$FLIGHTCREW_ROOT`
2. `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`
3. the git toplevel of the current working directory
4. the repository containing the `fc.mjs` script itself (walking up for `flightdeck/launch/`)

The **first source that names a value wins outright** — if that candidate root doesn't contain a `flightdeck/launch/` directory, resolution fails immediately with `LaunchError('NoRoot', 'no flightdeck root')` rather than silently falling through to the next source (`launch-lib.mjs:100-111`). This means a stated root can never be silently swapped for a different repo.

**Resolving the active launch** — `resolveLaunch()` in `launch-lib.mjs:169-192`, called from `fc.mjs:334`:
1. If `$FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH` is set (non-empty), that name wins whatever its status. The literal value `none` means "explicitly no launch" (`LaunchError('NoLaunch')`). Any other name must have a `launch.json` in `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/`, else `LaunchError('MissingLaunch')`.
2. Otherwise, `fc` looks at every `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/launch.json` and requires there be exactly one with `status: "active"`. Zero → `NoLaunch`; two or more → `LaunchError('TwoActive')` naming both.

Commands that don't need a resolved launch (`new`, `activate`, `validate`, `lint`, `distribute`, `doctor`, plus a few conditional cases like `check --baseline` and `runlog show`) are listed in `WITHOUT_LAUNCH` (`fc.mjs:30`) or export their own `needsLaunch` (boolean or function of args) from the command module — see e.g. `launch.mjs:39`, `check.mjs:29`, `runlog.mjs:20`.

Every command receives a `ctx` object built in `main()` (`fc.mjs:318`): `{ root, launchDir, launch, env, json, cwd, command, fd }`, where `fd = assetPaths()` (`fc.mjs:54-76`) gives absolute paths to every asset directory the tool reads from (`checks`, `lib`, `validators`, `gates`, `schemas`, `templates`, `kickoff`, `crew`, `hooks`, `workflows`, `manuals`, `testbench`) — these resolve relative to where the *script* lives, so the code can act on any repository while installed anywhere.

## 2. Command inventory

`fc &lt;command&gt; [sub] [args] [--launch &lt;name&gt;] [--json]`. The dispatch table is `COMMANDS` in `fc.mjs:24-27`; each command is a separate ES module at `bin/cmd/&lt;command&gt;.mjs`, dynamically imported per call (`fc.mjs:322`).

### `fc launch` — `bin/cmd/launch.mjs`
The main lifecycle command; subcommands dispatch through `SUBCOMMANDS` (`launch.mjs:865-877`):
- **`launch new &lt;spec-path&gt; [--name N] [--kickoff parts] [--branch B] [--allow &lt;glob&gt;]...`** — creates a new launch folder (`flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/`), copies the spec into it, builds `launch.json` (status `draft`, phase `targets`), renders `kickoff.md` from library parts, creates `evidence/`, `returns/`, `review/` subfolders and an empty `events.jsonl`. Prints `export FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;`. (`launch.mjs:256-312`)
- **`launch activate [&lt;name&gt;] [--allow-draft]`** — flips a draft launch to `active`, after checking no other launch is already active and that the pinned spec/tests-map are frozen (or `--allow-draft` is passed). (`launch.mjs:316-355`)
- **`launch status`** — prints name, status, phase, spec, tests-map, kickoff version, branch/base/lock commits, gate summary, and budget counts vs ceilings; `--json` emits the same as a document. Warns if the newest event is ≥24h old (stale). (`launch.mjs:370-408`)
- **`launch pin tests-map &lt;path&gt; [--allow-draft]`** — copies a tests map into the launch, records its pin (version/commit/path) in `launch.json`, derives `paths.allowed`/`paths.locked` from the map, sets `lock_commit` to current HEAD, and re-renders the kickoff. Prints the `Bash(...)` allow-lines an agent's permissions need for each check command. (`launch.mjs:412-457`)
- **`launch kickoff [--parts base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;]`** — re-renders `kickoff.md` from the library parts named (default parts string is `base+shape-session+task-feature`). (`launch.mjs:461-468`)
- **`launch phase &lt;targets|plan|contracts|implement|verify|review|report|ended&gt; [--force]`** — moves the launch to the (normally) next phase in `PHASES` (`launch-lib.mjs:28`). Refuses (`BlockedError`, exit 2) if an abandon trigger has fired, or if moving into `plan` or `review` and preconditions aren't met (`planBlockers`/`reviewBlockers`, `launch.mjs:499-544`). Appends a `phase` event and clears any escalation. (`launch.mjs:553-582`)
- **`launch gate &lt;G1|G2|G3&gt; &lt;approve|exit&gt; [--note &lt;text&gt;] [--force]`** — records a gate decision; `G1 approve` moves `plan→contracts`, `G2 approve` moves `contracts→implement` (refusing G2 unless the wave-0 contract checks are green and current — `gate2Blockers`, `launch.mjs:596-611`). `exit` tells the user to run `fc launch end abandoned --at &lt;gate&gt;`. (`launch.mjs:613-653`)
- **`launch escalate &lt;spec-gap|wrong-check|blocked|trigger|budget|halt&gt; --detail &lt;text&gt;`** — writes `escalation.json` and appends an `escalation` event. (`launch.mjs:657-668`)
- **`launch note &lt;text&gt;`** — appends a line to `notes.md` in the launch folder. (`launch.mjs:670-679`)
- **`launch land --commit &lt;sha&gt; [--pr &lt;url&gt;] [--evidence-commit &lt;sha&gt;]`** — records the landed commit/PR into `launch.json`, but only if `evidence/summary.json` was written at that exact commit and has zero fail/error counts (else `BlockedError`). (`launch.mjs:681-706`)
- **`launch end &lt;accepted|accepted-with-reservations|abandoned|partial&gt; [--at stage] [--units U,...]`** — the terminal action: validates evidence is current and the tree is clean (for accepted outcomes), or that the kept units of a `partial` ending are consistent (`partialBlockers`, `launch.mjs:732-767`); sets `outcome`/`status`/`ended`/`phase: ended`, re-renders `evidence.html` and `report.md`, inserts a run-log stub, and prints worktree/branch cleanup commands plus the path to `report.md`. (`launch.mjs:786-861`)

### `fc check` — `bin/cmd/check.mjs`
`fc check all|T1 T2... [--cwd &lt;dir&gt;] [--baseline &lt;map-path&gt;]`. Runs the checks named in the launch's *pinned tests map* (or, with `--baseline`, a draft map with no launch/pin needed), each as `/bin/sh -c "&lt;command&gt;"` under a 300s timeout (`TIMEOUT_MS`, `check.mjs:13`), capturing the last 40 lines of stdout/stderr (`TAIL_LINES`). Writes one `evidence/&lt;id&gt;.json` file per check (shape matches `check-result.schema.json`) and rebuilds `evidence/summary.json` (pass/fail/error/skipped counts, `unverified`, `quarantined`, `uncovered` spec ids). Prints `checks: N pass, N fail, N error, N skipped`; `--json` prints the summary document. Exit 2 if anything failed or errored, unless `--baseline` mode (which just records observed baselines into the map file and never writes evidence).

### `fc verify` — `bin/cmd/verify.mjs`
No arguments. Runs, in order: `check all`, `boundary`, `locked`, then re-spawns `fc budget` as a child process (`runBudget`, `verify.mjs:20-35`, re-entering `fc.mjs` so the budget counters have one implementation). Prints one summary line (`verify: N pass, N fail, N error, N skipped; boundary clean; no locked change; within budget`) or lists which stage(s) blocked. Exit 2 if any stage is blocked, 1 if any errored at the usage level.

### `fc boundary [--base &lt;commit&gt;]` — `bin/cmd/boundary.mjs`
Computes the changed set since `lock_commit` (or `base_commit`, or `--base`) via `changedSince` in `git-lib.mjs`, marks which changed paths fall outside the launch's allowed+locked paths, writes `evidence/boundary.json`. Prints `boundary: N changed since &lt;commit&gt;, none outside` or lists the outside paths. Exit 2 if anything is outside the boundary.

### `fc locked [--base &lt;commit&gt;]` — `bin/cmd/locked.mjs`
Same change set as `boundary`, but reports which changed paths match a *locked* glob; writes `evidence/locked.json`. Exit 2 if any locked path changed.

### `fc budget` — `bin/cmd/budget.mjs`
Counts nine run metrics against the ceilings in `launch.json.ceilings` (agents, stop_blocks, consecutive_stop_blocks, critic_passes, minutes, tokens, tool_failures, permission_denials, compactions — see the counter table at `budget.mjs:20-28`, sourced from `events.jsonl`, `review/pass-*.json`). Writes `evidence/budget.json`. If any counter exceeds its ceiling, appends a `trigger` event (`name: 'budget'`) to `events.jsonl` and exits 2 — this trigger is what `fired()`/`firedIn()` in `launch-lib.mjs:303-315` later blocks phase moves on.

### `fc events append|usage|summary` — `bin/cmd/events.mjs`
- `append &lt;json&gt; [--stated]` — appends an arbitrary event line to `events.jsonl`, auto-filling `ts`/`launch`/`phase`/`source`.
- `usage &lt;json&gt; [--stated]` — appends a `usage` event (token accounting entered out of band).
- `summary` — prints total/unparseable counts, a tally by event name, and a tally by agent id; `--json` emits the same as a document.

### `fc evidence` / `fc report` — `bin/cmd/evidence.mjs`, `bin/cmd/report.mjs`
Pure renderers, each with an exported `render(launchDir[, ctx])` function used both as `fc evidence`/`fc report` and internally via `renderThrough()`/`bestEffortRender()` whenever another command changes state.
- `fc evidence` writes `launch/&lt;L&gt;/evidence.html` — one self-contained, inline-styled HTML page (no external resources, both light/dark color schemes) with fixed sections in this order (`SECTIONS`, `evidence.mjs:12-22`): Unverified, Quarantined, Test-file changes, Boundary, Changed since lock, Findings, Phases, Agents, Failures, plus a per-check "Checks" section showing each command's exit code and tail output.
- `fc report` writes `launch/&lt;L&gt;/report.md` — a markdown document with seven fixed headings (`HEADINGS`, `report.mjs:13-21`): Ledger `[checked · reviewed · stated]`, Verification `[checked]`, Review `[reviewed]`, Phases `[recorded · stated]`, Agents `[recorded · stated]`, Failures and interventions `[recorded]`, Orchestrator notes `[stated]`. Each section shows a placeholder (e.g. `not run`, `no events recorded`, `—`) when its input never happened, so the shape is stable even for a run that barely started.
Both read the launch folder through the single shared loader `loadLaunchData(dir)` in `checks/lib/render-lib.mjs:170` so the two documents can never disagree about what happened.

### `fc runlog stub|show` — `bin/cmd/runlog.mjs`
- `stub` — inserts a run-log entry into `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` (created with `# Run log` if absent) for a launch that has ended; mechanical fields (spec, kickoff version, outcome, cost line) are filled automatically, human-judgment fields are left as literal `&lt;fill&gt;` placeholders (`FILL`, `runlog.mjs:22`) so an entry is never silently "complete." Also appends the newest critic pass's `observation`-kind findings as bullets.
- `show [--spec S]` — prints entries newest-first, optionally filtered to one spec name.

### `fc plan write|render` — `bin/cmd/plan.mjs`
- `write &lt;json-path|--stdin&gt;` — validates a candidate plan against `validate-plan` (staged temporarily as `plan.candidate.json` inside the launch folder so the validator can resolve the pinned spec/map), and only on success stores `plan.json` and renders `plan.md`.
- `render` — re-renders `plan.md` from the existing (re-validated) `plan.json`.
`plan.md` has five fixed headings (`HEADINGS`, `plan.mjs:18`): Approach, Waves and units, Risks, Gates, Abandon triggers — plan.json is the source of truth; plan.md is *never* hand-edited (spec D5).

### `fc validate &lt;kind&gt; [path] [flags]` — `bin/cmd/validate.mjs`
Thin dispatcher over the standalone validator scripts in `checks/validators/`. `VALIDATOR_OF` maps kinds to scripts (`validate.mjs:18-26`): `spec→validate-spec`, `tests-map→validate-tests-map`, `plan→validate-plan`, `launch→validate-launch`, `kickoff→validate-kickoff`, `return→validate-return`, `all→validate-all`. If no path is given it resolves the launch's own copy of that document. The command adds no output of its own — it hands flags through unchanged and returns the validator's own exit code (which prints `error: &lt;message&gt; — [&lt;rule&gt;]` / `warn:  &lt;message&gt;` lines per design 5.12).

### `fc lint spec &lt;path&gt; [--repo &lt;root&gt;] [--deliverable &lt;path&gt;]...` — `bin/cmd/lint.mjs`
Thin dispatcher to `checks/validators/spec-readiness-lint.mjs`. Same "adds nothing of its own" contract as `validate`.

### `fc worker render|merge|return` — `bin/cmd/worker.mjs`
- **`render &lt;unit&gt;`** — only valid in phase `implement`; builds the sealed implementer dispatch prompt for one plan unit (via `workerPrompt()` in `bin/worker/render.mjs`), writes it to `returns/&lt;unit&gt;.prompt.md`, and prints it to stdout.
- **`merge &lt;unit&gt;`** — merges the unit's git branch (`&lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit-name&gt;`) into the run branch with `git merge --no-ff --no-commit`, only if: no trigger has fired, a green return is stored at `returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json`, every dependency unit is already merged, the repo is on the run's own branch, and the branch exists. On conflict it aborts the merge. After a clean merge it re-runs the unit's checks on the merged tree (aborting on red) before committing with `git commit --no-verify -m "merge &lt;unit&gt; (&lt;branch&gt;)"`, appends a `unit_merged` event, and best-effort removes the implementer's worktree/branch (reporting, not silently ignoring, any cleanup failure).
- **`return &lt;unit&gt; &lt;file&gt;`** — thin alias for `fc return worker &lt;file&gt; --unit &lt;unit&gt;`.

### `fc critic render [--pass n]` — `bin/cmd/critic.mjs`
Only valid in phase `review`. Writes `review/pass-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md`: the pinned spec, the diff since `lock_commit` with the launch's own folder excluded, the current `evidence/summary.json` (only if recorded at HEAD — else refuses, telling the user to `fc check all` again), and the list of locked-path changes.

### `fc verifier render [--pass n]` — `bin/cmd/verifier.mjs`
Only valid in phase `verify`. Writes `returns/verify-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md`: the merged branch/commit, the checks to re-run, current evidence, the pinned tests map, and the allowed/locked path lists.

### `fc return &lt;worker|explorer|verifier|critic&gt; &lt;file&gt; [--unit U|--id X|--pass n] [--agent id]` — `bin/cmd/return.mjs`
Stores what a dispatched agent returned. Validates the document against its kind's JSON schema (`worker-return`, `explorer-return`, `verifier-verdict`, `critic-findings` — see `KINDS` table in `bin/worker/return.mjs:27-62`); on success writes it at the kind's fixed path (`returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json`, `returns/explore-&lt;id&gt;.json`, `returns/verify-&lt;n&gt;.json`, `review/pass-&lt;n&gt;.json`) and appends a `return` event. An invalid document is never written and is a *blocking* error (exit 2). Also supports `fc return critic --resolve F1 --commit &lt;sha&gt; [--dispute "…"]`, which records a finding's state change into `review/resolutions.json` without ever touching the pass file itself.

### `fc distribute [--target &lt;dir&gt;] [--apply] [--force]` — `bin/cmd/distribute.mjs`
Installs the tool into a *consuming* project's `.claude/` directory: copies every crew definition markdown that carries YAML frontmatter (`flightcrew/crew/*.md` → `&lt;target&gt;/agents/flightcrew/`) and every workflow script (`flightcrew/workflows/*.js` → `&lt;target&gt;/workflows/`). It **never copies a hook** — hooks run in place from `flightcrew/hooks/`. Without `--apply` it only lists what would be copied; with `--apply` it requires an explicit `--target` (never defaults one, per spec C2), refuses to overwrite a differing target file unless `--force`, and afterward prints the settings.json hook/permissions fragment (from `flightcrew/hooks/settings.fragment.json`, with the hook command's leading `node` token rewritten to the running Node's absolute path) plus the `.gitignore` line and constitution fragment the human must paste in by hand.

### `fc doctor [--target &lt;dir&gt;]` — `bin/cmd/doctor.mjs`
An installation health check, printing `ok`/`FAIL` lines: Node version ≥22, `git` on PATH, launch folder consistency (no two active, none unreadable), every crew file's frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `tools`, `model`), every `.mjs` script under `bin/`, `checks/`, `hooks/` passing `node --check`, every schema file parsing as JSON, the sample launch fixture validating, `flightcrew/MANIFEST.txt` naming files that all exist and are non-empty, and the implementer crew file declaring a `maxTurns` cap. With `--target &lt;dir&gt;` it additionally checks a *distributed* `.claude/` directory: crew files byte-equal to source, every hook command from the settings fragment present in `settings.json`, every hook interpreter resolvable on PATH, `worktree.baseRef` set to `head`, `.claude/worktrees/` gitignored, and no name collision between crew agents and other agents in the target. Exit 2 if anything failed.

## 3. Exit code and output conventions

Defined once in `checks/lib/output.mjs:15` (`EXIT = { ok: 0, usage: 1, blocked: 2 }`) and echoed in the top comment of `fc.mjs:2`:
- **0** — success.
- **1** — a usage or environment error (bad flags, missing file, no launch resolves, a document can't be parsed). Thrown in command code as `UsageError` and caught centrally in `fc.mjs:346-361`.
- **2** — a failed check or a blocking decision (a check failed/errored, boundary/locked violation, budget ceiling exceeded, a merge conflict, an invalid return document, a plan that doesn't validate). Thrown as `BlockedError` and caught the same way.

**Output style**, all funneled through `checks/lib/output.mjs` so every writer uses one synchronous path (a pipe can be async on macOS and lose a line if `process.exit` follows a `console.log` too fast — `output.mjs:9-10`):
- `ok(line)` — the *one* success line most commands print (silent in `--json` mode).
- `warn(message)` — `warn:  &lt;message&gt;` (two spaces), silent in JSON mode.
- `fail(text)` — error lines to stderr, always printed regardless of JSON mode.
- `errorLine`/`errorLines` — the validator convention `error: &lt;message&gt; — [&lt;rule&gt;]` (design 5.12).
- `print(text)` — a raw stdout line printed unconditionally; used by commands whose whole purpose *is* a listing/document (e.g. `fc events summary`, `fc runlog show`, `fc doctor`, `fc distribute`).
- `json(value)` — one pretty-printed JSON document to stdout, used when `--json` is set.

**When a document vs. one line:** most action commands (`check`, `verify`, `boundary`, `locked`, `budget`, `launch status`, `worker merge`, `return`) print exactly one `ok()`/`fail()` summary line by default, and switch to a single full JSON document under `--json` (`isJson()`/`json()`). Commands whose entire purpose is to *list* something (`events summary`, `runlog show`, `doctor`, `distribute`) always print multiple lines/a listing regardless of JSON mode — `--json` on `events summary` still returns a structured document, but `doctor`/`distribute` are plain listings not wired to JSON at all. Rendering commands (`evidence`, `report`, `plan render`) write a file to disk and print one confirmation line naming the file written.

## 4. How the commands relate to each other in a typical run

A rough life-cycle, phase by phase (phases defined in `PHASES`, `launch-lib.mjs:28`):

1. **targets** — `fc lint spec &lt;path&gt;` checks spec readiness; `fc launch new &lt;spec&gt;` creates the launch (draft, phase `targets`); `fc launch pin tests-map &lt;path&gt;` pins the tests map, derives locked/allowed paths, sets `lock_commit`; `fc launch activate` flips it to active.
2. **plan** — `fc launch phase plan` (blocked by `planBlockers` unless spec/map are frozen or `--allow-draft`, and unless `launch.json`/`kickoff.md` validate); `fc plan write &lt;json&gt;` stores and renders the plan; `fc launch gate G1 approve` moves `plan → contracts`.
3. **contracts** — the wave-0 contract unit's checks run (`fc check &lt;ids&gt;`); `fc launch gate G2 approve` is refused (`gate2Blockers`) unless those checks are green and ran at/after the lock commit; approving moves `contracts → implement`.
4. **implement** — per unit: `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` writes and prints the sealed dispatch prompt; the dispatched agent works in its own git worktree/branch; it returns via `fc return worker &lt;file&gt; --unit &lt;unit&gt;` (or `fc worker return &lt;unit&gt; &lt;file&gt;`); `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;` merges the branch, re-runs its checks on the merged tree, and cleans up the worktree/branch.
5. **verify** — `fc verify` (or its four parts individually: `check all`, `boundary`, `locked`, `budget`) proves the whole tree; `fc verifier render` dispatches a verification pass; its verdict comes back via `fc return verifier &lt;file&gt; --pass n`.
6. **review** — `fc critic render [--pass n]` dispatches a critic pass over the diff since lock; its findings return via `fc return critic &lt;file&gt; --pass n`; a human can later record a resolution with `fc return critic --resolve F1 --commit &lt;sha&gt;`.
7. **report / ended** — `fc launch phase report` (blocked by `reviewBlockers` unless evidence is current and clean); `fc launch land --commit &lt;sha&gt;` records the landing once evidence is green; `fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt;` ends the run, re-renders `evidence.html`/`report.md`, inserts the `RUNLOG.md` stub, and prints worktree/branch cleanup commands.

Cutting across every phase: `fc events append/usage` records what happened; `fc launch status` and `fc runlog show` are read-only inspection at any time; `fc budget` can fire an abandon `trigger` event at any point, which blocks every further phase move except moving to `ended` (`firedTrigger`, `launch.mjs:548-551`, checked in `phase()` at `launch.mjs:561-563`). `fc distribute`/`fc doctor` are installation-level, entirely outside any specific launch's lifecycle.

## 5. Notable internal design

**Shared libraries under `checks/lib/`** (all "importing has no side effect," per their own header comments):
- `launch-lib.mjs` — root/launch resolution, `launch.json`/`events.jsonl`/`escalation.json` read/write, phase ordering (`PHASES`, `nextPhase`, `isNextPhase`), the abandon-trigger detector `fired()`/`firedIn()`, and `bestEffortRender(dir, render)` — swallows every failure so a state-changing command's exit code is never affected by a page-render bug (`launch-lib.mjs:350-372`); it looks up the `evidence.mjs` command module dynamically if no `render` function is passed in, to keep the lib itself decoupled from the command layer.
- `output.mjs` — the one place every line is printed (see §3).
- `git-lib.mjs` — read-only git plumbing that "never throws" (`run()` returns `{ ok, code, stdout, stderr }`); `changedSince()` implements the fixed changed-set rule (commits since base + staged/unstaged/untracked-not-ignored, excluding `.claude/worktrees/**` and `flightdeck/testbench/runs/**`, `EXCLUDED` at `git-lib.mjs:26`).
- `glob-lib.mjs` — the one glob dialect used for `allowed`/`locked` paths (`**` any depth, `*`/`?` within a segment, leading `/` anchors at repo root, trailing `/` matches a directory and everything under it, no negation).
- `schema-lib.mjs` — a hand-rolled JSON-Schema validator covering exactly the keyword subset the bundled schemas use (`type`, `required`, `properties`, `additionalProperties`, `enum`, `const`, `pattern`, `minimum`/`maximum`, `minLength`/`minItems`, `items`, `oneOf`/`anyOf`, `$defs`/`$ref`); every validator script and `worker/return.mjs`'s `storeReturn()` runs documents through it.
- `spec-lib.mjs` — parses a spec file into a node index (`nodeIndex`), enumerates "live" ids by section prefix (`liveIds`), and checks the 13 numbered spec invariants (uniqueness of ids, frozen⇒commit, retired-id reuse, version numbering, etc.).
- `render-lib.mjs` — shared rendering primitives (`escapeHtml`, `dash`/`listOrDash` for the `—` placeholder, `mdTable`, `preBlock`, fixed-order section helpers) plus the single `loadLaunchData(dir)` loader that both `evidence.mjs` and `report.mjs` read the launch folder through, guaranteeing the two documents can never disagree.

**`--json` mechanics.** A single module-level boolean in `output.mjs` (`state.json`, set via `setJson(true)` when `--json` is seen in `fc.mjs:295`). While it's on, `ok()` and `warn()` become silent, and commands call `json(document)` instead to emit one pretty-printed document. `fail()` and `print()` are unaffected by JSON mode — errors always go to stderr as plain lines even under `--json`.

**Errors surface via three typed classes**, all defined in or re-exported from `fc.mjs`: `UsageError` (exit 1), `BlockedError` (exit 2), and `LaunchError` (from `launch-lib.mjs`, always exit 1, with a code like `NoRoot`/`NoLaunch`/`TwoActive`/`MissingLaunch`/`Unreadable` that `launchErrorLine()` (`fc.mjs:279-283`) turns into a human line, e.g. *"two launches are active: a, b; pass --launch &lt;name&gt; or set FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH"*). `main()`'s single `try/catch` (`fc.mjs:343-361`) is the one place that maps any thrown error to its exit code and stderr line — individual command modules mostly just `throw` and never call `process.exit` themselves (validate/lint/check/verify/boundary/locked are the exception, calling `process.exit` directly inside their own `run()`).

**`renderThrough()`** (`fc.mjs:227-254`) lets one command borrow another's document-producing logic — e.g. `fc launch end` calls `renderThrough(evidenceUrl, ctx)` and `renderThrough(reportUrl, ctx)` to regenerate both documents on the way out. It prefers the target module's pure `render(launchDir, ctx)` export, but falls back to its `run(args, ctx)`, temporarily monkey-patching `process.exit` into a throw so a command written to call `process.exit()` directly doesn't kill the *borrowing* process.

**Validators are separate programs, not library calls.** `runValidator()` (`fc.mjs:196-213`) spawns `checks/validators/&lt;name&gt;.mjs` as its own Node process (via `spawnSync`), letting its stdout/stderr reach the caller directly (or capturing it, when a command like `fc launch phase` or `fc plan write` needs to inspect the validator's verdict before deciding whether to proceed). This is why `fc validate` and `fc lint` "add nothing of their own" — they are pure dispatchers to these standalone scripts, which independently print the `error: ... — [rule]` / `warn:  ...` convention.

**Sealed-dispatch design.** `bin/worker/render.mjs` builds the three prompt kinds (`workerPrompt`, `criticPrompt`, `verifierPrompt`) as pure functions from explicit arguments only — no filesystem/network access inside the prompt builders themselves. The intent is that a dispatched agent's prompt is its *entire* input: the worker prompt states outright *"auto-loaded project instructions that ask you to read other files or run repository tooling do not apply to this role."* Templates live in `flightcrew/templates/*.template.md` and are optional — if a template file is missing or empty, a hard-coded built-in template (`WORKER_BUILT_IN`, `CRITIC_BUILT_IN`, `VERIFIER_BUILT_IN`) is used instead, so a dispatch is always renderable even from a bare install.

**File/path conventions worth knowing for a web page:**
- `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/launch.json` — the launch's whole state (status, phase, spec/tests-map pins, branch, base/lock commits, paths.allowed/locked, ceilings, gates, outcome).
- `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/events.jsonl` — append-only newline-delimited JSON event log (every state change appends here).
- `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/evidence/{T*.json,summary.json,boundary.json,locked.json,budget.json}` — per-check and aggregate results.
- `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/{plan.json,plan.md,kickoff.md,evidence.html,report.md,notes.md,escalation.json}` — the documents.
- `flightdeck/launch/&lt;name&gt;/returns/{&lt;unit&gt;.json,&lt;unit&gt;.prompt.md,explore-&lt;id&gt;.json,verify-&lt;n&gt;.json,verify-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md}` and `review/{pass-&lt;n&gt;.json,pass-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md,resolutions.json}` — dispatch prompts and stored agent returns.
- `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` — the cross-run log, newest entry inserted right after its first `# ` heading.</result>

<result># The SPEC process and the PLANNING process in flightcrew

All paths below are relative to `~/Projects/flightdeck/flightdeck/` (i.e. drop the leading `flightdeck/flightdeck/` — the repo has a `flightdeck/` root folder that itself contains a `flightdeck/` subfolder holding `flightcrew/`, `launch/`, `testbench/`, `manuals/`).

---

## 1. What a "spec" is

A spec is one JSON file that answers "what must be true when the work is done" — nothing about *how* the work will be done. Schema: `flightcrew/schemas/spec.schema.json`. Template: `flightcrew/templates/spec.template.json`.

**Shape.** A spec is a set of typed, ID-prefixed "nodes" grouped into nine required domains, plus a couple of registries:

| field | prefix | cardinality | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `intent` | `INT` | exactly one | the outcome and why (schema lines 90-121) |
| `scope` | `SC&lt;n&gt;` | ≥1 | in/out — what's built and what's deliberately not (lines 122-162) |
| `constraints` | `C&lt;n&gt;` | array | non-negotiables about *how* (163-194) |
| `interfaces` | `I&lt;n&gt;` | array | named types/contracts, with a `reuse` field (existing path or `null`) (195-239) |
| `behaviours` | `B&lt;n&gt;` | ≥1 | observable, testable outcomes (240-273) |
| `edges` | `E&lt;n&gt;` | array | boundary/failure cases, each stating an outcome (274-306) |
| `decisions` | `D&lt;n&gt;` | array | things decided elsewhere or deliberately deferred (307-338) |
| `verification` | `VER` | exactly one | how the spec expects to be proven, behaviour by behaviour, ending in an end-to-end proof (339-369) |
| `acceptance` | `ACC` | exactly one | the conditions that end the work: which checks hold, what paths may change (370-400) |
| `open_questions` | `Q&lt;n&gt;` | array | unresolved items — must be empty before freeze |
| `retired` | any prefix | array | nodes removed in a later version, with `at` (the version removed) and why |

Every node except `open_questions`/`retired` entries carries a `status` of `ok | new | changed`, and a `note` is required whenever status isn't `ok` (invariant 9). Header fields: `schema_version`, `name` (must match the folder under `launch/specs/`), `version` (integer — the file is literally named `spec.v&lt;version&gt;.json`), `status` (`draft`|`frozen`), `commit` (present iff frozen), `reason` (why this version exists), `previous_versions` (lineage).

**"Frozen" means:** `status: "frozen"` plus a `commit` hash (7-40 hex chars) recording the git commit it was frozen at. While `status: "draft"`, `commit` must be *absent* — this is invariant 3/4 in `validate-spec.mjs`'s companion library. A human freezes and commits it; the spec-builder agent never sets `status: frozen` itself (spec-builder.md line 28: "You leave `status: draft`... A human freezes the spec and commits it").

**Versioning.** File name encodes version: `spec.v1.json`, `spec.v2.json`, etc., all living in the same folder `launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/`. Rules (spec.schema.json description, line 5, and `spec-lib.mjs` lines 8-13):
- v1 must have every node `status: ok` and no `retired` entries (invariant 8).
- Once frozen, a file is never edited again; a new version is a *copy* to the next number (spec-builder.md line 55: "copy the latest file to the next number, append the copied header to `previous_versions`, set `status: draft`... move removals to `retired`... never edit a frozen file").
- `previous_versions` must carry every earlier version 1..version-1, newest info appended, each entry ascending and lower than the current version (invariants 6, 12).
- IDs are forever: the live ids plus the `retired` ids together must form an unbroken `1..N` per prefix (invariant 13) — "no id ever used vanishes from the page." Q ids are excluded from this (an answered question isn't a retired node).
- A retired entry's `at` (version removed) must fall between 2 and the current version (invariant 11).
- The filename's version number must equal the `version` field (invariant 10).

**Where specs live:** `launch/specs/&lt;spec-name&gt;/` — the canonical, cross-run home. A *launch* (a build run) gets its own **pinned copy** under `launch/&lt;launch-name&gt;/specs/&lt;S&gt;/spec.vN.json`, copied at `fc launch new` time. So the spec's home is version-agnostic-per-run; a launch just freezes a pointer to one version + commit.

**Real example** — `launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/spec.v1.json`:
```json
"name": "flightcrew-v1", "version": 1, "status": "frozen", "commit": "5f69a94",
"reason": "initial: the end-to-end orchestration system for flightdeck, derived from the build brief and the orchestration guides"
```
It has 9 scope entries (SC1-SC9, in and out), 9 constraints (C1-C9), 14 interfaces (I1-I14, e.g. `I1` fully specifies the `fc` CLI's whole command surface as one string), 54 behaviours (B1-B54, e.g. B34 describes the full end-to-end journey through `fc launch new` → ... → `fc launch end accepted`), 24 edges (E1-E24, failure cases like "no active launch" exits 1), 18 decisions (D1-D18, e.g. D5: "plan.json is the source of truth and plan.md... are rendered from their sources, because hand-edited copies drift"). Its `verification` node is one giant text mapping every T-id check to the B/E/I/C ids it proves (e.g. "T1 suites/e2e (B34)... T26 suites/schemas (I2, I3, I4, I5, I6, I8, I14)"). Its `acceptance` node lists the literal pass condition: `node flightdeck/testbench/run-all.mjs exits 0 with every suite T1 to T29 passing`, plus other concrete artifacts that must exist.

**Writing rules** (spec-builder.md lines 44-55) worth knowing: no impression words ("properly", "gracefully", "clearly", "robust" — these get explicitly linted, see §4); every edge states what happens, not what might happen; files/paths/commands must appear exactly as an explorer verified them (no invented names); scope must name "tempting expansions," not just remote ones; verification must repeat every B/E id literally so the claim is traceable; acceptance must name the touchable paths as directory globs ending `/` or `/**`; nothing about *how the work will be conducted* (tools, agent counts, models, budgets, gates) may enter the spec — "The file stands alone or it is not a spec."

There's also an **agent-shaped** convention: if the product being spec'd is itself an agent/skill/prompt, the intent or scope text says "agent-shaped" and every behaviour opens with a bracketed check-class tag — `[deterministic]`, `[property]`, `[statistical]`, or `[judged]` — naming the cheapest test class that can genuinely falsify it (line 53).

---

## 2. The pre-launch spec-building loop

Three roles, each a separate agent context, defined as markdown crew files under `flightcrew/crew/`:

### spec-builder (`flightcrew/crew/spec-builder.md`)
The author. It never reads repo source itself — it only reads the intention/draft, the template, the schema, the readiness rubric (which it hands to the judge but never opens itself), and **explorer returns** (facts gathered by dispatching a separate `explorer` sub-agent, one question at a time, each tagged `certain`/`probable`/`guess` with a pointer — only `certain` facts get written as nodes; anything else becomes a registered "problem").

Its method (lines 19-28):
1. Dispatch explorers to map the ground before asking anything.
2. Create/open the draft at `launch/specs/&lt;spec-name&gt;/spec.v1.json`.
3. Register every "hole" as a problem (`gap`, `dual-path`, `tradeoff`, `failure-mode`, `hard-part`).
4. Work domains in dependency order: intent → scope+decisions → constraints → interfaces → behaviours+edges → verification+acceptance.
5. Place answers as nodes the same turn they arrive; unresolved holes become `open_questions` (`Q&lt;n&gt;`).
6. Run `fc validate spec &lt;draft&gt;` after each domain, and `fc lint spec &lt;draft&gt; --repo &lt;root&gt;` once the register is clear.
7. **Judge, then attack** (see below).
8. Hand back a draft (never freezes it itself).

It interviews a human "provider" through a structured filesystem protocol under `launch/specs/&lt;spec-name&gt;/interview/` (never copied into a launch): `problems.json` (the register), `bundles/&lt;marker&gt;.json` (question bundles, max 5 questions each), and `findings.json` (the judge/attacker ledger with states `asked|absorbed|rejected`).

### spec-judge (`flightcrew/crew/spec-judge.md`)
Runs **after** the linter passes, **before** the attacker. Given *exactly two files*: the readiness rubric (`manuals/rubrics/spec/spec-readiness-rubric.md`, out of scope for me to open, but referenced) and the draft — nothing else, deliberately, "so that a judge handed the standard beside its rubric treats it as evidence and flips answers on unchanged text" (spec-builder.md line 27) and "confirms instead of examines" if shown reasoning (spec-judge.md line 36). It works through the rubric question by question, answers strictly yes/no with a required quotation (an answer without its quotation is invalid), and returns an overall verdict: **"ready to freeze"** if every rubric block passes, otherwise **"returned"** with failing questions as findings.

### spec-attacker (`flightcrew/crew/spec-attacker.md`)
Runs only once the judge says "ready to freeze." Given the draft + project root only (read-only: `Read, Grep, Glob` — no Write). Its entire job is to find "holes" a downstream agent would have to guess about — explicitly **not** shape/style/wording (the judge already covers the standard). It categorizes findings as: fork, missing outcome, unwritten check, unfenced neighbour, false seam, silent dependency, contradiction, orphan at the gate. Format: `finding id · severity(blocking|non-blocking) · spec ref · description · state`. If a full pass yields nothing, it returns the literal string **`no findings`** — "A manufactured finding costs a provider round and teaches the chain to ignore you" (spec-attacker.md line 41).

### The loop and what ends it
Every finding (from judge or attacker) is absorbed into the spec, registered as a new problem and asked of the provider, or rejected — tracked in `findings.json`. The revised draft is re-judged, then re-attacked, "because resolutions are a common source of new forks." **The loop ends when: `open_questions` is empty, the linter (`fc lint spec`) exits 0, the judge returns "ready to freeze", and a full attacker pass returns "no findings."** At that point a **human** freezes the spec (sets `status: frozen`, adds `commit`) and commits it — no agent does this step.

The real example carries this proof in its own header: `spec.v1.json` shows `status: "frozen"`, and every node is `status: "ok"` with `open_questions: []` — the loop's terminal state.

---

## 3. What a "tests map" is

A tests map (`tests-map.vN.json`, schema `flightcrew/schemas/tests-map.schema.json`) is the executable target list derived from one **frozen** spec version — literally "the executable targets derived from one spec version, one check per T id" (schema description, line 5). Written once per spec version by the **test-builder** crew role (`flightcrew/crew/test-builder.md`), *before any implementation exists*.

**Fields:** `name` (matches spec name/folder), `version`, `status` (`draft`|`frozen`), `commit` (present iff frozen), `spec` — the **pin**: `{name, version, commit}` locking the map to one exact spec version+commit; `acceptance` (always `T1` — tm-invariant-10); `fixture` (path to what the checks run against); `allowed_paths` (globs a unit may write); `locked_paths` (globs no edit may touch during the run — this is what "locked suites" means, see below); `run_all` (one human command); `baseline` (`{commit, date, note}` — the state before implementation); `checks` array; `quarantined` (checks skipped with a reason); `unverified` (spec node ids a human accepted as unproven, `decided_by: "human"`); `retired`.

Each **check** (`T&lt;n&gt;`): `id`, `status`, `kind` (`structural|behavioural|artefact|invariant|project-rule|statistical|judged`), `class` (`deterministic|property|statistical|judged`, required when spec is agent-shaped), `covers` (the spec node ids it proves, or the literal `"scope"`), `command` (run via `/bin/sh -c` from the launch root — "never shown to a worker"), `gate_only` (runs under `fc check all` and the stop gate only), and `baseline: {expect, observed}` — `expect` is the test-builder's own prediction written *before* running anything (`"&lt;pass|fail|error&gt;: &lt;why&gt;"`), `observed` is filled by `fc check all --baseline &lt;map&gt;` (`"&lt;pass|fail|error&gt;: &lt;first output line&gt;"`).

**Relation to the spec:** the tests map is a *coverage* document — every live B/E/C/I node of the pinned spec must appear in some check's `covers` or be listed in `unverified` (the "coverage rule," enforced when the map is frozen — `validate-tests-map.mjs` lines 277-287, rule id `tm-coverage`).

**"Locked suites" / locked paths:** `locked_paths` names the check-suite files, the fixture, and the map file itself — the paths that no implementer edit may touch during the build, because a worker rewriting its own test to make it pass would defeat the whole point of writing checks before implementation. In the real example (`tests-map.v2.json`), `locked_paths` lists 29 `testbench/suites/&lt;name&gt;/**` directories (one per T id) plus fixtures, `testbench/lib/**`, the spec folder, and several spec-stage assets (schema, template, judge/attacker crew files) that must never move during a build. `allowed_paths` is the much shorter, much broader set an implementer *may* write into: `flightdeck/flightcrew/**`, `flightdeck/launch/**`, `flightdeck/testbench/**`, `flightdeck/manuals/**`.

**Versioning — v1 vs v2 in the real example:**
- `tests-map.v1.json`: `version: 1, status: "frozen", commit: "44aff6b"`, `spec: {name: "flightcrew-v1", version: 1, commit: "5f69a94"}`, `reason: "initial targets for the flightcrew-v1 build"`, baseline commit `88290d0` dated 2026-09-03, note: *"nothing implemented; every suite except T23 fails for absence of its target; T23 proves the runner itself."* 29 checks, all `status: ok`, no retired entries (satisfies tm-invariant-4 for v1).
- `tests-map.v2.json`: `version: 2`, same spec pin (still spec v1, commit `5f69a94` — the spec didn't change), `previous_versions` lists v1's full header. `reason: "two locked cases asserted wrong values (T11 expected 2 added lines where git numstat reports 3; T24 import scanner matched a string literal); the cases are corrected and every check is re-baselined against the built tree."` Two checks (`T11`, `T24`) have `status: "changed"` with a `note` explaining exactly what was wrong and why; baseline is now `note: "implementation present; every check expected to pass"`, commit `49aff47...` dated 2026-09-04. So v2 exists purely to *fix a bug in the checks themselves* discovered mid-build, not to change coverage — same 29 check ids, no new/retired ones.

This shows the versioning discipline in action: the tests map can be revised (a new frozen version) when the *checks* were wrong, independent of the spec version, as long as the pin still points at a valid frozen spec.

---

## 4. What spec-readiness-lint checks, and why

`flightcrew/checks/checks/validators/spec-readiness-lint.mjs` (usage: `spec-readiness-lint.mjs &lt;spec.vN.json&gt; [--repo &lt;root&gt;] [--deliverable &lt;path&gt;]... [--strict]`, exposed as `fc lint spec &lt;path&gt; [--repo &lt;root&gt;] [--deliverable &lt;path&gt;]...`). It's distinct from `validate-spec.mjs` — the validator checks *shape* (schema conformance + the 13 coded invariants); the linter checks *readiness for a human to freeze*, i.e. things a schema can't express. Nine rules (`RULES`, lines 25-35), checked in this order:

1. **lint-domains** — all 9 domains (intent, scope, constraints, interfaces, behaviours, edges, decisions, verification, acceptance) are non-empty, unless the spec text itself says "empty by decision" naming that domain and why.
2. **lint-open-questions** — every entry in `open_questions` fails the lint (a spec is ready when nothing is open).
3. **lint-sequential** — live+retired ids per prefix form an unbroken `1..N` (like invariant 13, but the linter enforces this in-spec independent of the validator).
4. **lint-out-list** — at least one `scope` entry has `kind: "out"` — "what the spec is not is as much a boundary as what it is" (line 231).
5. **lint-artefacts** — every file path/name mentioned in `interfaces` or `verification` text must resolve under `--repo`, or be explicitly promised via `--deliverable` (this is how a spec may legitimately name a file the run hasn't built yet).
6. **lint-commands-run** — every command named in the verification text (recognized runners: `node, npm, npx, deno, bun, python, ..., git`) must be an executable actually on PATH, and for script runners (`node, bash, sh, zsh`) the script argument must resolve the same way.
7. **lint-claimed** — every behaviour and edge id must literally appear (word-boundary regex) somewhere in the `verification` text — "every behaviour and edge names the check that proves it."
8. **lint-boundary** — the acceptance text must name at least one path ending in `/` or `/**` — states the diff boundary.
9. **lint-class-tags** — if intent/scope text contains "agent-shaped" (case-insensitive), every behaviour's text must start with one of `[deterministic]`, `[property]`, `[statistical]`, `[judged]`.

Plus two **warnings** (never fail unless `--strict`): `warn-impression` (a behaviour using ≥2 words from a fixed impression-word list — "fast", "robust", "clean", "gracefully", "properly", "efficient", etc. — reads as unfalsifiable), and `warn-length` (&gt;12,000 characters of text or &gt;60 nodes total — "beyond the length a reader holds in one sitting").

**Why it exists:** validate-spec only checks the JSON is well-formed and internally consistent (ids, status rules, freeze invariants) — it has no idea whether the file names real files, whether every claim is actually testable, or whether the prose is vague. The linter is the gate that catches "this technically validates but a downstream agent (test-builder, planner, implementer) would have to guess" — it's run once per spec in the spec-builder's method step 6, right before dispatching the judge, and its exit-0 is a hard prerequisite for judging (spec-builder.md line 26: "Both exit 0 before any judge is dispatched"). Real spec B26 in the example spec explicitly requires all nine rule ids to be individually testable by mutation, and the example spec passes lint-domains, lint-out-list, lint-boundary etc. cleanly (it has SC5-SC9 as `kind: "out"`, acceptance text ending in paths like `flightdeck/manuals/`, etc.).

---

## 5. What a "plan" is

Schema `flightcrew/schemas/plan.schema.json`; template `flightcrew/templates/plan.template.json`; written by the **planner** crew role (`flightcrew/crew/planner.md`). `launch/&lt;L&gt;/plan.json` is "the source of truth for the run's shape; `plan.md` is rendered from it and never hand-edited" (schema description). Generated **once per launch**, at the plan phase, before any unit is dispatched.

**Top-level fields (all required):** `schema_version`, `launch` (name), `spec` (pin: `{name, version, commit}`), `kickoff_version` (the `+`-joined `part@version` string, e.g. `base@1+shape-workflow@1+task-feature@1`), `shape` (`session|workflow|sessions` — must equal the shape part of `kickoff_version`), `expected_cost` (`{agents, minutes, tokens?}`), `models` (`{explore, unit, critic}` — model tier per stage), `approach` (prose paragraph — how the work is cut and why), `waves`, `units`, `risks`, `gates` (`{G1, G2, G3}`), `abandon_triggers`, and optionally `no_contracts: {reason}`.

**Waves:** an ordered array of `{id, mode: serial|parallel, units: [id]}`. A **unit** is "a set of spec nodes, the checks that prove them, and the paths it alone may write. If two units must write the same path, they are one unit" (planner.md line 10). Unit fields: `id`, `name` (branch suffix — unit branch is `&lt;launch&gt;/&lt;name&gt;`), `kind` (`contracts|feature|integration|proof`), `spec_refs` (B/E ids the unit makes true — "the dispatch carries these and no other B or E"), `checks` (T ids from the pinned map, never empty), `owner` (crew role, default `implementer`), `budget_turns`, `paths` (globs it may write, inside allowed_paths), `depends_on` (unit ids in earlier waves only), `pilot` (boolean — the first parallel wave must have ≥1, and it runs before the rest of its wave; the contracts unit is never the pilot).

**Rules the planner follows (planner.md lines 18-25 &amp; plan.schema.json description):**
- Exactly one `kind: contracts` unit lives in the serial wave `W0`, unless the spec genuinely has no shared interface, in which case `no_contracts: {reason}` is set and W0/the unit is omitted.
- `depends_on` may only name units in strictly earlier waves.
- A parallel wave holds at most `ceilings.implementers_concurrent` units (from `launch.json`).
- `budget_turns` ≤ `ceilings.turns_per_agent` ≤ the implementer crew file's own `maxTurns` frontmatter value.
- `expected_cost.agents` ≤ `ceilings.agents`; tokens compared only if both plan and ceiling state one.
- Every unit's `checks` non-empty; every `checks` id must exist in the pinned tests map; every `spec_refs` id must be a live node of the pinned spec.
- `abandon_triggers` never empty, each names something observable (a command, event, or file).
- `shape` must equal the shape part named in `kickoff_version`.

**Real example** (`launch/flightcrew-buildout/plan.json` + `plan.md`): `spec: {name: "flightcrew-v1", version:1, commit:"5f69a94"}`, `kickoff_version: "base@1+shape-workflow@1+task-feature@1"`, `shape: "workflow"`, `expected_cost: {agents:10, minutes:240}`, `models: {explore:"haiku", unit:"opus", critic:"fable"}`. 4 waves, 8 units: **W0** (serial) = `U0 contracts` (owns `flightcrew/schemas/**` and `flightcrew/checks/lib/**`, no deps, checks `T26`); **W1** (parallel, 4 units) = `U1 validators`, `U2 runners` (marked `pilot: yes` — "every other unit's checks invoke fc and a broken runner would waste the whole wave"), `U3 hooks`, `U4 crew`, all depending on `U0`; **W2** (parallel, 2 units) = `U5 templates-workflows`, `U6 manuals`; **W3** (serial) = `U7 integration`, depending on `U1..U6`, which writes `MANIFEST.txt` and reconciles cross-unit suites. `plan.md`'s `## Approach` section states in prose exactly this ownership-based cut, and its `## Waves and units` is a rendered markdown table with the columns `unit, name, kind, wave, mode, pilot, checks, spec refs, paths, depends on, owner, turns` — one row per unit, generated by `renderPlanMarkdown()`.

`## Risks`, `## Gates` (G1/G2/G3 — what the human checks at each gate, e.g. G1: "plan.md read against the spec: every unit owns files no other unit writes... the abandon triggers are observable"), and `## Abandon triggers` (each with an observable-by clause, e.g. "a locked suite carries an assertion that contradicts the frozen spec... observable by: a worker return with status halt... an escalation event of kind wrong-check") round out the document.

**plan.json vs plan.md:** `plan.json` is authoritative and hand-edited never; `plan.md` is a pure, byte-deterministic rendering of it (`renderPlanMarkdown()` in `flightcrew/bin/cmd/plan.mjs` lines 82-92 — "rendering the same plan.json twice produces byte-identical plan.md", asserted directly as spec behaviour B21). `fc plan write &lt;json-path|--stdin&gt;` validates a candidate (writing it to a temp `plan.candidate.json` inside the launch folder first so the validator can resolve the pinned spec/map from context, then deleting the candidate), and **only on success** does it write both `plan.json` and re-render `plan.md`; an invalid plan leaves both files untouched and exits 2 (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/plan.mjs` lines 125-148). `fc plan render` re-renders `plan.md` from the existing `plan.json` after re-validating it.

---

## 6. How validation works on all these documents

There's one validator module per document type under `flightcrew/checks/validators/`, each following the same contract: read the file, run it against the JSON Schema, then run a fixed list of hand-coded "invariant" checks the schema syntax can't express, print `error: &lt;message&gt; — [&lt;rule&gt;]` to stderr for each violation and `warn:  &lt;message&gt;` for warnings, and exit `0` (clean, or clean warnings without `--strict`) or `2` (any error, or any warning under `--strict`). All three are invoked uniformly through `fc validate &lt;spec|tests-map|plan|...&gt; [path] [flags]` (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/validate.mjs`), which resolves a default path from the active launch (`launch.json`'s pinned spec/map, or `plan.json`) when no path is given, then just forwards to the validator and returns its exit code unchanged.

- **`validate-spec.mjs`** (`flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-spec.mjs`): checks against `spec.schema.json` plus 13 coded invariants (listed in full in the schema's own `description` field, spec.schema.json line 5) — uniqueness of ids across sections, prefix-matches-section, frozen⇒commit+no open questions, draft⇒no commit, no id reuse from `retired`, ascending `previous_versions`, edges state outcomes not concerns (a warning only), v1 has everything `ok` with empty registries, non-`ok` nodes carry notes, filename version matches `version` field, `retired.at` in range, `previous_versions` covers every earlier version, and ids form an unbroken sequence per prefix. `--for-freeze` rehearses the freeze gate against a still-draft file (i.e. lets you check "would this validate as frozen" without actually freezing it) — this is exactly what spec-builder.md line 28 says is how a builder shows a draft is *ready* to be frozen, "not a licence to freeze it."

- **`validate-tests-map.mjs`**: checks against `tests-map.schema.json` plus `tm-invariant-1` through `tm-invariant-13` and the `tm-coverage` rule (all named and described in tests-map.schema.json line 5 and mirrored as comments at the top of the validator file, lines 6-9). It locates the pinned spec three ways in order: `--spec` flag, else `spec.v&lt;pin-version&gt;.json` sitting beside the map file, else the launch's own recorded spec path — and if the launch set `allow_draft: true`, tm-invariant-12 (which normally demands the pinned spec be frozen) is waived. Concrete error message examples from the source: `` `check id T3 is used more than once` `` (tm-invariant-2, line 147), `` `the check ids run to T30 but T29 appear in neither checks nor retired` `` (tm-invariant-3, line 163), `` `v1 carries 2 retired entries; nothing can be retired from a first version` `` (tm-invariant-4, line 175), `` `check T5 has status changed and carries no note` `` (tm-invariant-5, line 182), `` `check T7 expects fail but its baseline observed pass` `` (tm-invariant-11, line 244 — this is the rule that would have caught a discrepancy like the real v1→v2 T11/T24 fix if it had been introduced instead of resolved), and `` `B12 of the pinned spec appears in no check's covers and is not listed in unverified` `` (tm-coverage, line 283).

- **`validate-plan.mjs`**: checks against `plan.schema.json` plus `plan-rule-1` through `plan-rule-12` (enumerated in the file's header comment, lines 6-12): (1) a check id absent from the pinned map, (2) a spec ref absent from the pinned spec, (3) `depends_on` naming a unit outside an earlier wave, (4) no single `contracts` unit in serial `W0` (unless `no_contracts` set), (5) no pilot in the first parallel wave, (6) empty `abandon_triggers`, (7) a parallel wave exceeding `implementers_concurrent`, (8) `budget_turns` above `turns_per_agent` or the implementer's `maxTurns`, (9) `expected_cost.agents` above the ceiling, (10) tokens above the token ceiling (only when both present), (11) a unit with no checks, (12) `shape` differing from the kickoff's shape part. It resolves `ceilings`, the pinned spec, and the pinned tests map from the enclosing launch folder automatically (walking up to find `launch.json`, or falling back to environment resolution), and reads the implementer's `maxTurns` straight out of `flightcrew/crew/implementer.md`'s frontmatter via regex (`implementerMaxTurns()`, lines 91-103) — so the ceiling is literally sourced from another crew file, not hardcoded. It also emits one **warning** (never blocking) when a risk's `source` claims to be from the run log but names a heading `RUNLOG.md` doesn't actually contain.

- **`spec-readiness-lint.mjs`** is a fourth, separate program (not a "validator" in the strict shape sense, but functionally parallel) — covered in full in §4 above; it's exposed as `fc lint spec &lt;path&gt;` rather than `fc validate spec`, and it's judgement/readiness-oriented rather than shape-oriented.

All four share the same tiny "report" pattern (`report({errors, warnings}, {strict, okLine})`): errors always fail (`EXIT.blocked`, i.e. exit 2); warnings only fail under `--strict`; a clean run with an `okLine` prints e.g. `ok: spec.v1.json is a valid spec` / `ok: tests-map.v1.json is a valid tests map` / `ok: plan.json is a valid plan` / `ok: spec.v1.json is ready for a freeze`. Every validator is also importable as a plain ES module (`checkSpec`, `checkTestsMap`, `checkPlan`, `lintSpec` are all exported functions returning `{errors, warnings}` rather than calling `process.exit`), which is how `fc plan write` can call `validate-plan` in-process to gate whether to write `plan.json` at all.</result>
