<result># The Crew and The Review — Flightcrew (flightdeck/flightcrew)

All paths below are relative to the repo root, actually located at `flightdeck/flightdeck/...` on disk (the top-level `flightdeck/` in the repo contains a second `flightdeck/` folder — that's the real tree; don't be thrown by it).

## 1. The full roster

The roster's canonical table lives at `flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/README.md:11-22`. Each role is one markdown file: frontmatter = identity, body = the system prompt. `fc distribute --apply --target &lt;repo&gt;/.claude` copies every file with frontmatter into `&lt;target&gt;/agents/flightcrew/` (README.md:3).

Two chains, plus one shared role (README.md:7):
- **Spec chain** — turns an intention into a frozen spec: `spec-builder` → `spec-judge` → `spec-attacker` (looped).
- **Run chain** — turns a frozen spec into a proven build: `test-builder` → `planner` → `orchestrator` → `implementer` → `verifier` → `critic`.
- **explorer** — a shared utility used by both chains, answering one narrow factual question at a time.

A hard rule threads every role file: "what you read, and what you never read" — the "must not see" column is as binding as "sees", because a role that reads another role's reasoning "stops being an independent witness" (README.md:7).

### Role-by-role

**orchestrator** (`crew/orchestrator.md`)
- Job: conducts a whole launch — dispatches units wave by wave, records every return, holds gates open for a human. "You conduct. You do not build, and you do not judge." (line 10)
- Frontmatter: `model: inherit`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, Agent`, `initialPrompt: "Run flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc launch status..."`, no `maxTurns` field (open-ended), `color: purple`.
- Dispatched by: a human, as the session agent for an active launch.
- Reads: kickoff.md, pinned spec, pinned tests-map, plan.json/plan.md, stored returns, prior report.md files, plus `manuals/orchestration/planning.md` at the plan step and `manuals/orchestration/review.md` at the review step.
- Must not see: worker transcripts, the spec interview, a critic's reasoning behind a finding ("you route the finding as written").
- Holds no Write/Edit — everything it produces goes through `fc plan write`, `fc launch note`, `fc return &lt;kind&gt;`.
- Returns: a status object (`launch`, `phase`, `units[]`, `open_findings`, `escalation`, `next`) — it "conducts", it doesn't hand back a deliverable file.

**planner** (`crew/planner.md`)
- Job: turns the frozen spec + pinned tests-map into `plan.json`'s content — waves, units, checks, risks, gates, abandon triggers. Used once per launch, at the plan phase.
- Frontmatter: `model: fable`, `maxTurns: 30`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, Agent`, `color: blue`.
- Dispatched by: the orchestrator, at the plan step.
- Reads: pinned spec, pinned map, planning/run-log manuals, RUNLOG.md, the crew roster README, explorer returns handed to it or dispatched by it.
- Must not see: worker transcripts, another launch's returns, any implementation being planned.
- Returns: the plan object (JSON) which the orchestrator stores via `fc plan write --stdin`.

**implementer** (`crew/implementer.md`)
- Job: builds one unit inside its own worktree until that unit's checks pass, touching only its owned paths.
- Frontmatter: `model: opus`, `maxTurns: 25`, `isolation: worktree`, `permissionMode: acceptEdits`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, Write, Edit`, `color: green`. The only role with worktree isolation.
- Dispatched by: the orchestrator, once per unit per wave, via `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;`.
- Reads: only its own dispatch — unit id/name, launch name, branch, spec nodes, checks, paths, turn budget, files under those paths.
- Must not see: the plan, the kickoff, another unit's dispatch, another worker's return.
- Returns: worker-return JSON — `unit`, `status` (`green`/`red`/`halt`), `branch`, `worktree`, `spec_refs`, `checks[]`, `artefacts[]`, `commits[]`, `iterations`, `halt`, `notes`.

**verifier** (`crew/verifier.md`)
- Job: re-runs a launch's checks on the merged branch and tries to refute the green result rather than confirm it. "A green board is a claim, not a fact."
- Frontmatter: `model: sonnet`, `maxTurns: 15`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, `color: orange`.
- Dispatched by: the orchestrator, at the verify phase, via `fc verifier render`, after `fc verify` first reports green.
- Reads: pinned tests-map, `evidence/summary.json` + individual check evidence files, the working tree of the merged branch.
- Must not see: implementer reasoning, stored worker returns, the plan ("a worker's account of why a check passes is exactly the claim under test").
- Returns: verifier-verdict JSON — see section 3.

**critic** (`crew/critic.md`)
- Job: reviews the launch's diff against the pinned spec and reports gaps — never fixes anything.
- Frontmatter: `model: fable`, `maxTurns: 20`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, `color: red`.
- Dispatched by: the orchestrator, at the review phase, via `fc critic render [--pass n]`.
- Reads: a **sealed** dispatch carrying everything inline (pinned spec text at commit, diff since `lock_commit`, evidence summary, locked-path change list, allowed/locked path globs) — "You read no repository file to obtain them."
- Must not see: the plan, the kickoff, any worker's reasoning/return, **an earlier pass's findings** ("each pass is fresh, and a prior verdict would anchor this one").
- Returns: critic-findings JSON — see section 2.

**explorer** (`crew/explorer.md`)
- Job: answers one narrow, factual question about the codebase or a document, with a short cited answer. Used by any stage whenever a fact is missing.
- Frontmatter: `model: haiku`, `maxTurns: 12`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, `color: cyan`.
- Dispatched by: spec-builder or planner (via the Agent tool), one question per dispatch.
- Reads: only the question and the paths its dispatch names.
- Returns: explorer-return JSON — `id`, `question`, `stage`, `answer`, `confidence` (`certain`/`probable`/`guess`), `pointers[]` (file:line citations), `candidates[]`.

**spec-builder** (`crew/spec-builder.md`)
- Job: turns an intention or a draft-with-findings into a freeze-ready spec, via an interview mechanism (register → bundles → answers → ledger).
- Frontmatter: `model: fable`, `tools: Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Grep, Glob, Agent, AskUserQuestion`, `color: pink`. No `maxTurns` given.
- Dispatched by: a human, at the start of a spec's life or to resolve judge/attacker findings.
- Reads: the intention/draft, spec template, spec schema, its own dispatched-explorer returns, judge/attacker output. Does **not** open repository source itself — every project fact must arrive via a cited explorer return.
- Returns: a JSON handoff block — `draft` path, `version`, `status`, node `counts`, `problems`, `validator`/`linter` results, `judge`/`attacker` verdicts, `open_questions`, `declared_deliverables`, `dispatches`.

**spec-judge** (`crew/spec-judge.md`)
- Job: judges a linter-clean draft against the readiness rubric, fresh-context, yes/no with quoted evidence — "not a critic looking for gaps."
- Frontmatter: `model: fable`, `tools: Read` only.
- Dispatched by: spec-builder, after its own linter passes, before the attacker runs.
- Reads: exactly two files — the rubric and the draft. Nothing else, "and you do not go looking for any."
- Returns: a verdict sheet (markdown/text) — per-question yes/no + quotation + reason, per-block pass/fail, overall **"ready to freeze"** or **"returned"** with failing questions listed as findings.

**spec-attacker** (`crew/spec-attacker.md`)
- Job: finds the holes in a judged draft before freeze — forks, missing outcomes, unwritten checks, unfenced neighbours, false seams, silent dependencies, contradictions, orphaned gate conditions. "The cheapest exit in the orchestration chain."
- Frontmatter: `model: fable`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob`.
- Dispatched by: spec-builder, after spec-judge returns "ready to freeze."
- Reads: the draft + the project (to check that reused names actually resolve). Not given the rubric, the standard, or the interview.
- Returns: findings in the format `finding id · severity · spec ref · description · state`, severity `blocking`/`non-blocking`, or the literal `no findings`.

**test-builder** (`crew/test-builder.md`)
- Job: turns a frozen spec into the tests-map and the check scripts that will lock the build, recording each check's baseline before implementation exists.
- Frontmatter: `model: opus`, `maxTurns: 40`, `permissionMode: acceptEdits`, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, Write, Edit`, `color: yellow`.
- Dispatched by: a human, once per spec version, before any implementation.
- Reads: frozen spec, chosen fixture, existing codebase, the testing manuals.
- Must not see: a plan, an implementation, an interview transcript, a prior run's returns.
- Returns: `map` path, `checks[]` (each with baseline `expect`/`observed`), `unverified[]`, `spec_findings[]`.

---

## 2. The review process end to end

### What a critic pass is

A critic pass is one fresh-context review of the diff accumulated since the launch's `lock_commit`, judged against the pinned spec — nothing else. It happens once per pass number (`pass: 1`, `pass: 2`, ...) at the orchestrator's `review` phase (`orchestrator.md:24`, `PHASES` array = `['targets','plan','contracts','implement','verify','review','report','ended']`, `checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs:28`).

The dispatch is rendered by `fc critic render [--pass n]` (`bin/cmd/critic.mjs`). Key mechanics:
- Refuses to render unless `launch.json.phase === 'review'` (critic.mjs:22-24).
- If `--pass` isn't given, auto-increments from existing `review/pass-N.json` files (`nextPass`, critic.mjs:31).
- Assembles a **sealed** prompt from the template `templates/critic-dispatch.template.md`: pinned spec text at its commit, the diff since lock (`diffSinceLock`, with the launch folder itself excluded), the evidence summary JSON, and the "locked paths changed since the lock" list.
- Writes `review/pass-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md`.

The critic works a fixed checklist, **in order** (critic.md:16-22 / template lines 7-11):
1. Behaviours implemented — every behaviour/edge/interface node actually implemented, not just asserted by a check.
2. Scope held — nothing built outside spec scope or outside allowed path globs.
3. Tests untouched — no check/fixture/suite weakened since lock.
4. Errors handled, not suppressed.

"Assume the diff contains at least one gap and look for it. A review that ends empty because nobody looked is worth nothing; a review that ends empty after this checklist is a result." (critic.md:10)

### What a finding looks like

Schema: `flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/critic-findings.schema.json`. Each finding requires exactly these fields (no additional properties allowed):

```
id          "F&lt;n&gt;"                      pattern ^F[0-9]+$
kind        correctness-gap | scope-violation | spec-conflict | observation
severity    blocking | non-blocking
spec_ref    string or null — the spec node it's measured against
file        string or null
line        integer or null
text        non-empty string
state       open | resolved | disputed
resolved_commit  string or null (7-40 hex chars) or null
dispute     string or null
```

Severity follows the kind mechanically (critic.md:28, schema, and pass-1.json/pass-2.json examples confirm this):
- `correctness-gap` → always `blocking`
- `scope-violation` → always `blocking`
- `spec-conflict` → `blocking`, and it additionally **stops the run** rather than being fixed
- `observation` → always `non-blocking`

Real examples from `launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/review/pass-1.json`:
- `F2`: `kind: correctness-gap`, `severity: blocking`, `spec_ref: B27`, `file: flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/worker.mjs:78`, `state: open` — "fc worker render refuses every phase but implement, yet B35/B40 ... dispatch the W0 contracts unit ... while the launch is in phase contracts."
- `F1`: `kind: scope-violation`, `severity: blocking`, `state: disputed`, `dispute: "The change under the locked path is a tests-map revision made by the human at the targets gate after a wrong-check finding, not a run edit."`
- `F4`/`F5`/`F6`: `kind: spec-conflict`, `severity: non-blocking` in this sample (spec-conflict is documented as blocking-by-kind but this real example shows one recorded non-blocking — the schema still allows either value, and severity here reflects the actual assignment made by the critic run, so treat "spec-conflict is always blocking" as the doc's default rule, not an absolute schema constraint).
- `F3`, `F7`, `F8`, `F10`: `state: resolved`, `resolved_commit: "acafec4"`.

A finding is never edited retroactively in its pass file — that file is frozen once stored. `flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/worker/return.mjs:8-11`: "Pass files are never edited — a later change to a finding's state is written to `review/resolutions.json`... and the report joins the two."

### How findings get resolved — `resolutions.json`

Command: `fc return critic --resolve F1 --commit &lt;sha&gt; [--dispute "..."]` (`bin/cmd/return.mjs:83-97`, logic in `bin/worker/return.mjs:112-136`):
- Given `--commit`, the finding's new state is `resolved` and `resolved_commit` is set.
- Given only `--dispute`, the new state is `disputed` and `resolved_commit` stays null.
- Requires one of `--commit` or `--dispute`.
- Writes/updates `review/resolutions.json`, keyed by finding id, e.g. from the fixture `testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/review/resolutions.json`:
```json
{ "F1": { "pass": 1, "state": "resolved", "resolved_commit": "293a4b5", "dispute": null, "at": "2026-08-30T11:40:00Z" } }
```
- Each entry: `pass`, `state` (`resolved`/`disputed`), `resolved_commit`, `dispute`, `at` (ISO timestamp).
- The report generator (`bin/cmd/report.mjs:196-204`) reads a finding's *current* state by preferring the resolution over the original pass file's `state` field: `resolution?.state ?? finding.state`.

The orchestrator's routing rule for findings (`orchestrator.md:24`):
- `correctness-gap` and `scope-violation` → sent to the implementer owning the unit whose paths hold the file, along with the finding, spec node, and that unit's checks.
- `spec-conflict` → sent straight to an escalation, no fix attempted.
- `observation` → recorded with `fc launch note` and never sent to any agent.

### Pass-1 vs pass-2

Each pass is a **fresh** critic context — "each pass is fresh, and a prior verdict would anchor this one" (critic.md:14). Concretely, that means the critic never reads pass-1's findings when writing pass-2 — it re-reviews the (now-updated) diff from scratch. The orchestrator's loop (`orchestrator.md:24`) is: route findings → implementers fix → re-verify → take a fresh critic pass → repeat, up to `ceilings.critic_passes` in `launch.json` (default `2`, `bin/cmd/launch.mjs:62`).

The two real example passes (`launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/review/pass-1.json` and `pass-2.json`) show this: pass-1 has 11 findings (some resolved, some disputed, some open); pass-2, run after fixes landed, has 11 *different* findings (renumbered F1-F11 again, starting fresh) reflecting the state of the diff at that later point — including new findings not present before (e.g. pass-2's F6-F11 are new observations about `git-lib.mjs`, `worker.mjs`, `launch.mjs`, `report.mjs`, `render.mjs`, `doctor.mjs` that pass-1 never raised).

### When review stops

Two conditions:
1. **Clean pass**: the critic returns `verdict: "no gaps"` (any blocking finding forces `verdict: "gaps"`; an all-`non-blocking`/observation pass still returns `"no gaps"` — critic.md:30). Then the orchestrator proceeds to `fc launch phase report` (orchestrator.md:25).
2. **Ceiling hit with blocking findings still open**: reaching `ceilings.critic_passes` (default 2) with blocking findings still unresolved is itself an **abandon trigger** — "run `fc launch escalate trigger`, never another pass and never on to the report" (orchestrator.md:24).

Entering the `review` phase at all is gated by `reviewBlockers()` (`bin/cmd/launch.mjs:522-544`): evidence/summary.json must exist and be at HEAD, zero failed/errored checks, evidence/boundary.json must show no outside-boundary changes, evidence/locked.json must show no locked-path changes. If any of that is stale or dirty, `fc launch phase review` is refused outright — review cannot even start on a run that isn't clean.

---

## 3. The verifier — verdict and how it differs from the critic

**What a verdict is**: the JSON object returned by the verifier role, schema `flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/verifier-verdict.schema.json`. Required fields, no additional properties:

```
refuted            boolean
checks_rerun       [{ id: "T&lt;n&gt;", exit: integer|null }]
reasons            [string]     — one line per finding, naming the check id or path
unverified         [string]     — spec node ids no check proves
test_file_changes  [string]     — changed paths under a locked path
outside_boundary   [string]     — changed paths outside allowed/locked globs
```

Real example (`testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/returns/verify-1.json`):
```json
{
  "refuted": false,
  "checks_rerun": [{"id":"T1","exit":0},{"id":"T2","exit":0},{"id":"T3","exit":0},{"id":"T4","exit":0},{"id":"T5","exit":0}],
  "reasons": [], "unverified": [], "test_file_changes": [], "outside_boundary": []
}
```

**What it can say**: `refuted` is `true` or `false`. Set to `true` when, and only when (`verifier.md:24`), one of three things holds:
1. A recorded check verdict did not reproduce when re-run.
2. A locked path changed since the lock.
3. A change landed outside the launch's boundary.

Anything else the verifier notices (e.g. a changed file not under a locked glob, or an unverified node) is recorded in its list/`reasons` but does **not** by itself set `refuted: true`.

**How it differs from the critic**:
| | verifier | critic |
|---|---|---|
| Model | sonnet | fable |
| What it tests | Whether the recorded green **evidence is real** — re-runs every check itself and diffs exits against the recorded copy | Whether the **implementation matches the spec** — reads spec + diff and reasons about behaviour/scope/tests/errors |
| Output shape | Binary `refuted: true/false` + supporting lists | Multi-finding list, each independently typed/severed |
| Reads code? | Re-executes checks; reads the merged branch's working tree, not diffs by eye | Reads a diff and reasons textually, doesn't execute anything itself beyond `Bash` for inspection |
| Stage | `verify` phase, before `review` | `review` phase, after verify |
| Repeats? | Can run again but isn't structured as multi-"pass" the way critic is (no pass-ceiling concept in its own file, though `fc verifier render --pass n` exists) | Explicitly multi-pass with a ceiling (`ceilings.critic_passes`) |
| Frame | "A green board is a claim, not a fact. Your job is to try to break the claim." | "Assume the diff contains at least one gap and look for it." |

Put simply: the verifier asks "is the proof real?" (mechanical re-execution), the critic asks "is the work actually done and in-bounds?" (spec-vs-code reasoning). The verifier runs first (phase `verify`); only after checks survive verification does the orchestrator move to `review` and dispatch the critic.

---

## 4. How review interacts with the rest of the run — does it gate anything?

Yes, extensively:

- **Entry gate**: `fc launch phase review` is refused unless evidence is current and clean (§2 above, `reviewBlockers()`, `launch.mjs:522-544`) — you can't even start review on a dirty or stale build.
- **Findings gate re-verification**: correctness-gap/scope-violation findings are routed to implementers to fix, which forces another `verify` pass before the next critic pass (orchestrator.md:24, step 7: "Re-verify, then take a fresh critic pass").
- **`spec-conflict` gates the whole run**: it's blocking *and* stops the run rather than being fixed (critic.md:28) — it goes straight to escalation, no attempted patch.
- **The pass ceiling gates completion**: hitting `ceilings.critic_passes` (default 2) with blocking findings still open is itself an abandon trigger (`orchestrator.md:24`) — the run cannot proceed to the report phase in that state; it must escalate instead.
- **Gate 3 (G3)**: the orchestrator halts at Gate 3 after `fc launch phase report`, and only a human ends the run with `fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt;` (`orchestrator.md:25`, README.md:47) — "No report states an acceptance verdict. The outcome given to `fc launch end` is the human's decision."
- **The report is a pass-through, not a judgment**: `report.mjs` renders a `## Review` section joining each pass file with `resolutions.json` (report.mjs:190-208) — showing finding/kind/severity/spec/where/state/text as a table — but the report itself never states an acceptance verdict; it's evidence for the human's Gate-3/`launch end` decision.
- **Escalation kinds**: `spec-gap`, `wrong-check`, `blocked`, `trigger`, `budget`, `halt` (`launch.mjs:44`) — a fired abandon trigger (including the critic-pass-ceiling case) blocks every phase move except moving to `ended` (`launch.mjs:561-563`, `firedTrigger`).

So review is a hard gate on run completion: it can only start on a clean build, its blocking findings must be routed and cleared (or the spec itself is in conflict, which halts the run), and it bottoms out either in a clean pass (proceed to report) or a ceiling breach (must escalate, cannot proceed).

---

## 5. Which roles are adversarial by design, and why

Several roles are explicitly built to *try to break* something rather than confirm it, and each states why in its own file:

- **verifier** — "A green board is a claim, not a fact. Your job is to try to break the claim." (verifier.md:10) It deliberately does not read implementer reasoning or stored returns, because "a worker's account of why a check passes is exactly the claim under test" (verifier.md:14).

- **critic** — "Assume the diff contains at least one gap and look for it." (critic.md:10) It is denied the plan, kickoff, worker reasoning, and even *its own prior pass's findings*, so that "each pass is fresh, and a prior verdict would anchor this one" (critic.md:14).

- **spec-attacker** — "a fresh context with no investment in the draft in front of you," whose sole purpose is finding holes a downstream agent would trip on (spec-attacker.md:8). It is explicitly denied the rubric/standard/interview: "Your value is that you read the words the way the downstream agents will: without the provider's intent to fill the gaps." (spec-attacker.md:12) A learned lesson baked into the file: "Given the standard, this role spent its findings on shape and style... Reading only the draft and the project keeps the findings on holes." (spec-attacker.md:49)

- **spec-judge**, while not adversarial in the "find gaps" sense (it explicitly says "You are not a critic looking for gaps"), is deliberately isolationist for the same underlying reason as the others: fresh-context independence prevents rubber-stamping. "A judge shown the author's reasoning confirms instead of examines. The two-file rule is the entire basis of your independence." (spec-judge.md:36)

**Why this pattern recurs**: every one of these roles is walled off from the reasoning/history of the thing it's checking (implementer transcripts, prior critic passes, the spec interview, the standard behind the rubric) on the theory that a reviewer who sees *why* something was done will rationalize it rather than test it independently. The README states the general principle once, for all roles: "A role that reads the plan or another role's reasoning stops being an independent witness" (README.md:7). The adversarial roles are simply the ones where that independence is turned outward into an explicit mandate to find fault — verifier against the test evidence, critic against the spec-vs-code claim, spec-attacker against the draft's completeness — while spec-judge and explorer keep the same isolation but in service of a narrower, non-adversarial yes/no or a cited factual answer.</result>

# Testing and Checks — Report

## 1. What the testbench is, how it's run, what output looks like

**What it is** (`flightdeck/testbench/README.md:3`): a regression-test harness that lives alongside the `flightcrew` system it tests. It contains "the suite runner, one regression suite per check id of the flightcrew spec at `flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/spec.v1.json`, the fixtures the suites build temporary repositories from, and the rubric bench." It never touches real launch data — that lives under `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/evidence/`.

**How to run everything**, from the repo root:
```
node flightdeck/testbench/run-all.mjs [--only &lt;substring&gt;]
```
I ran it live. Real output (tail):
```
ok bin-boundary (12/12, 7050 ms)
ok bin-check (17/17, 16056 ms)
...
ok workflows (4/4, 171 ms)
hygiene: ok
```
It runs every `suites/*/run.mjs` in name order, one line per suite (`ok &lt;name&gt; (&lt;passed&gt;/&lt;total&gt;, &lt;ms&gt; ms)` or a failing equivalent), keeps each suite's full stdout at `runs/&lt;suite&gt;.log`, writes a machine-readable summary to `runs/last.json` (I inspected this: `{ ran_at, repo, only, suites: [{ name, ok, code, signal, passed, total, ms, log, count_line }] }`), and finishes with a `hygiene: ok`/failure line. Exit codes: `0` all suites passed, `1` usage/environment error (including "no suites at all"), `2` otherwise.

**Run one suite directly**, e.g.:
```
node flightdeck/testbench/suites/manifest/run.mjs
```
Real output:
```
pass  manifest-exists-with-path-lines
pass  every-path-exists-and-is-non-empty
pass  paths-are-repository-relative-under-flightdeck
pass  lists-every-file-the-scope-names
pass  no-launch-folder-other-than-specs-and-the-buildout
covers: B38 I13
5/5 passed
```
This is the **suite protocol** (spec I9, enforced by `flightdeck/testbench/lib/suite-lib.mjs:4`): every suite prints one line per test case — `pass  &lt;case&gt;` or `FAIL  &lt;case&gt;: &lt;reason&gt;` — then exactly one `covers: &lt;ids&gt;` line naming the spec node ids it proves (B/C/I/E/SC ids), then a final `&lt;n&gt;/&lt;m&gt; passed` line. Exit `0` if every case passes, `2` otherwise; a suite never exits `1` and never crashes silently — an uncaught error becomes a `FAIL` line and exit `2`.

**Hygiene** (README.md:18): each suite runs with a private `TMPDIR`; anything left behind there after the run is a leak and fails the run-all invocation. `git status --porcelain` is snapshotted before/after; any new line outside `flightdeck/testbench/runs/` fails the run too. `runs/` is gitignored except its own `.gitignore`.

## 2. Complete inventory of suites (29 T-numbered + 1 unit suite)

Real first-line comments, one per `suites/&lt;name&gt;/run.mjs`:

| T id | Suite dir | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | `e2e` | Drives a whole `fc` command sequence over a temp git repo holding the sample project + sample spec, "exactly as a user would," checking every exit code and produced file. |
| T2 | `bin-launch` | The `fc launch` command family (new, activate, status, phase, gate, end, pin, kickoff, escalate, note, land) plus `fc plan write` and launch selection. |
| T3 | `hooks-noop` | Every hook is a silent no-op (exit 0, empty stdout) with no active launch / no `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` / no `flightdeck/launch` / `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=none` / non-JSON stdin. |
| T4 | `hooks-eventlog` | `event-log` appends exactly one well-formed event line per recorded hook event, announces the launch on `SessionStart`, stays silent under `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=none`. |
| T5 | `hooks-guards` | `lock-guard` and `boundary-guard` deny edits outside locked/permitted paths, resolve against git toplevel (worktrees included), `ask` on ambiguous launch, deny everything after a fired abandon trigger. |
| T6 | `hooks-structural` | `structural-check` runs the configured command per file extension, exits 2 with last 20 lines on failure, 0 silently on success, ignores unconfigured extensions. |
| T7 | `hooks-stopgate` | `stop-gate` blocks the turn on a red acceptance check in phase `verify`, stalls/fires a trigger at the ceiling, runs contracts-unit checks + `fc boundary` in phase `contracts`. |
| T8 | `bin-check` | `fc check`, `fc check --baseline`, `fc verify`. |
| T9 | `bin-boundary` | `fc boundary`, `fc locked`, `fc budget`, `fc events` usage/summary. |
| T10 | `bin-report` | `fc report` and events edge cases shared with `fc events summary`/`fc budget`. |
| T11 | `bin-evidence` | `fc evidence` and the self-contained `evidence.html` it writes. |
| T12 | `bin-runlog` | `fc runlog` stub and the RUNLOG entry `fc launch end` inserts (accepted/abandoned/partial). |
| T13 | `bin-plan` | `fc plan render` validates `plan.json` and writes `plan.md` deterministically, refusing to write on an invalid plan. |
| T14 | `validate-spec` | Regression suite of `checks/validators/validate-spec.mjs`; mutation-based case table. |
| T15 | `validate-tests-map` | `validate-tests-map` names each of `tm-invariant-1`..`13` plus the coverage rule. |
| T16 | `validate-plan` | `validate-plan` exits 2 naming a rule per plan defect. |
| T17 | `validate-launch` | `validate-launch` exits 2 per `launch.json` defect, checks hash shape only unless `--resolve-commits`. |
| T18 | `lint-spec` | `spec-readiness-lint` exits 2 naming the failed rule id per spec-readiness violation; prints warnings without failing for impression/length. |
| T19 | `bin-dispatch` | `fc worker render`, `fc return` (+ `fc worker return` alias), `fc worker merge`, `fc critic render`. |
| T20 | `bin-distribute` | `fc distribute` dry run writes nothing; `--apply --target` copies crew files + workflow scripts, refuses conflicts unless `--force`. |
| T21 | `bin-doctor` | `fc doctor` exits 0 on the built repo and a prepared target, exits 2 on ambiguous launches or a broken target. |
| T22 | `crew` | Every `flightcrew/crew/&lt;role&gt;.md` carries required frontmatter and role rules (maxTurns, isolation, permissionMode, initialPrompt, read-only roles hold no Write/Edit). |
| T23 | `run-all` | A copy of `run-all.mjs` itself, in a temp testbench, runs fake suites and one real suite to prove the runner follows its own contract. |
| T24 | `constraints` | Static scans: import specifiers, package files, network use in scripts under the four in-scope dirs; durable-phrase/length scans of markdown; exit codes; write hygiene. |
| T25 | `hook-timing` | Every hook exits within 2000ms (best of three); Stop hook fragment entry has `timeout: 600`. |
| T26 | `schemas` | Every schema file parses; every sample document validates; mutated copies fail with the right schema keyword; `fc validate return` accepts samples by kind; `settings.fragment.json` matches spec I14. |
| T27 | `templates` | Kickoff library parts, the `kickoff.md` assembly `fc launch new`/`fc launch kickoff` produce, and `fc validate kickoff` rejections. |
| T28 | `manifest` | `flightcrew/MANIFEST.txt` lists one path per line, every path exists/non-empty, every spec-named file is listed, no stray launch folders. |
| T29 | `workflows` | Every `flightcrew/workflows/*.js` passes `node --check`, opens with `export const meta` matching filename, uses no clock/randomness, inlines schemas identical to `flightcrew/schemas/*.json`. |
| — | `unit-integration` | Not a T-numbered locked suite — an **implementation unit's own suite** (added under `suites/unit-*/`) proving MANIFEST.txt covers every shipped file and the gate modules stop-gate loads carry the right interface. |
| — | `validate-spec/fixtures/` | Not a suite itself — the validator's golden fixture/case table subdirectory used by T14. |

Confirmed live: running the full `run-all.mjs` produced exactly these 29 "ok" lines plus `unit-integration`, all green, e.g. `ok hooks-noop (56/56, 20231 ms)`, `ok validate-tests-map (25/25, 12212 ms)`.

## 3. "Locked suites" and the T1..T29 mapping

From `README.md:37-43` (**the lock rule**):
- A launch is one orchestrated run (state in `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/launch.json`). `fc launch status` reports whether one is active and prints the locked path globs it enforces; `lock_commit` is the commit recorded when the launch's tests map was pinned.
- **While a launch is active**, the suites its tests map names, plus `fixtures/`, `lib/` and `run-all.mjs`, are **locked paths**: the `lock-guard` hook (T5) refuses edits to them, `fc locked` reports any change since `lock_commit`, and the critic agent is told about any drift.
- An implementer that thinks a suite is wrong returns `status: halt` with `halt.kind` = `test-contradicts-spec` or `unsatisfiable`; the orchestrator escalates via `fc launch escalate wrong-check`; a human decides whether the suite or the spec changes, then re-pins with `fc launch pin tests-map &lt;map&gt;`.
- Implementation units may only **add** suites under `suites/unit-*/` — they must never edit a locked suite to make it pass.

The **T1..T29 count**: exactly **29 locked suites**, one per check id of the pinned tests map (`flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/tests-map.v1.json` / v2), verified directly against `flightdeck/testbench/suites/manifest/run.mjs:12-16` which hard-codes the same 29-name list (`e2e, bin-launch, hooks-noop, ... workflows`) as `SUITES` and asserts every one of them is listed in `flightcrew/MANIFEST.txt`. `unit-integration` sits outside this locked list — it's the extra suite the "integration" implementation unit added itself (spec-legal under the unit-* rule).

## 4. The layers of checking: schema validation vs validators vs gates vs hooks-checks vs testbench suites

Five distinct layers, from data shape up to workflow enforcement:

1. **Schema files** (`flightcrew/schemas/*.schema.json`) — pure JSON Schema-ish documents (type/required/properties/enum/etc.) describing the shape of launch documents (`launch.schema.json`, `tests-map.schema.json`, `plan.schema.json`, `event.schema.json`, `check-result.schema.json`, `explorer-return.schema.json`, `worker-return.schema.json`, `verifier-verdict.schema.json`, `critic-findings.schema.json`). They declare shape only; they don't run anything.

2. **`checks/lib/schema-lib.mjs`** + the testbench's own **`suites/schemas/mini-schema.mjs`** — a small JSON-Schema checker (`export function validate(schema, document, root) → [{path, keyword, message}]`) supporting `type, required, properties, additionalProperties, enum, const, pattern, minimum, maximum, minLength, minItems, items, oneOf, anyOf, $defs/$ref, allOf, not, maxItems, maxLength, patternProperties`. This is what actually interprets the schema files against real documents, both in production code and in the T26 `schemas` suite.

3. **Validators** (`flightcrew/checks/validators/*.mjs`) — one script per document kind: `validate-spec.mjs`, `validate-tests-map.mjs`, `validate-plan.mjs`, `validate-launch.mjs`, `validate-kickoff.mjs`, `validate-return.mjs`, `spec-readiness-lint.mjs`, and the umbrella `validate-all.mjs`. These are semantic/business-rule checkers, richer than plain schema shape — e.g. `validate-tests-map.mjs` checks `tm-invariant-1`..`13` and a coverage rule. `validate-all.mjs` (`flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-all.mjs:1`) "runs every validator over every document of a directory tree," printing one `ok &lt;file&gt;` / `warn &lt;file&gt;` / `FAIL &lt;file&gt;` line per document plus a count line, exiting 0/2/1 per its own rules (`--strict` turns warnings into failures too). Reached from the CLI via `fc validate &lt;spec|tests-map|plan|launch|kickoff|return|all&gt; [path]` (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/validate.mjs`), which just resolves the target document and forwards to the matching validator — it "adds nothing to their output."

4. **Gates** (`flightcrew/checks/gates/*.mjs`: `acceptance-gate.mjs`, `contracts-gate.mjs`, `structural-gate.mjs`) — these decide whether a **phase transition** in an active launch is allowed to proceed, by running checks and reporting a blocking verdict. Quoting `acceptance-gate.mjs:1`: "the gate of the verify phase: it runs the pinned tests map's acceptance check through `fc check` and reports whether that check holds the turn." `contracts-gate.mjs` runs the contracts unit's checks + `fc boundary` and blocks per spec B40's rules (error always blocks; a fail against an "expect pass" baseline blocks; a fail against an "expect fail" baseline doesn't; `fc boundary` exiting 2 blocks). `structural-gate.mjs` runs a per-extension structural command on one file. Gates are library modules with a shared `run(context) → { ran, reason?, checks, extra }` shape — "the gate never decides what to do about a red check... the caller decides."

5. **Hooks / hooks-checks** (`flightcrew/hooks/*.mjs`) — the layer that actually intercepts Claude Code tool events and calls gates/validators. `lock-guard.mjs` and `boundary-guard.mjs` fire on `PreToolUse` and deny Edit/Write/NotebookEdit outside allowed paths. `structural-check.mjs` fires on `PostToolUse` and calls `structural-gate.mjs`. `stop-gate.mjs` fires on `Stop` and calls `acceptance-gate.mjs`/`contracts-gate.mjs` depending on phase, counting consecutive blocks against a ceiling. `event-log.mjs` fires on many events and just appends `events.jsonl`. `session-end.mjs` refreshes evidence/report on `SessionEnd`. All hooks are wired declaratively in `flightcrew/hooks/settings.fragment.json` and are wrapped by `lib.mjs`'s `runHook`, which "swallows every error so a hook can never break a session" — every hook is a no-op absent an active launch.

6. **Testbench suites** — sit above all of this, as the meta-layer: they don't validate launch documents or gate a live session, they are regression tests that spawn the real commands/hooks/validators/gates as child processes against temporary fixture repos and assert on their exit codes and output. So the ordering, top to bottom, is: **schema files → schema-lib interpreter → validators (semantic rules) → gates (phase-blocking decisions) → hooks (live interception, wired to gates/validators) → testbench suites (regression tests proving all the above behave)**.

## 5. Fixtures: `sample-launch`, `sample-project`, `sample-spec`

From README.md:22-27 and my own directory listing:

- **`fixtures/sample-project/`**: a tiny real project (`src/export/index.mjs`, `tests/export/{behaviours,contract,edges}.test.mjs`, `scripts/{export-invariants,export-smoke}.mjs`, plus a `reference-project.json` test fixture and `README.md`) — "the material every temporary repository is built from," whose checks the sample-spec tests map actually runs.
- **`fixtures/sample-spec/`**: `spec.v1.json`, `tests-map.v1.json`, `plan.sample.json`, `checks/boundary-note.md` — "the golden inputs for the validators, the runner and the end-to-end suite."
- **`fixtures/sample-launch/`**: a complete, realistic launch folder — `launch.json`, `plan.json`/`plan.md`, `kickoff.md`, `notes.md`, `events.jsonl`, `evidence/{summary,boundary,budget,locked,T1..T5}.json`, `returns/{explore-X1,U0..U3,verify-1}.json`, `review/{pass-1,resolutions}.json`, and a nested `specs/export-html/{spec.v1,tests-map.v1}.json` (a pinned frozen map). This is "the reference for launch-folder shape" that `fc doctor`, the report/evidence suites, and hook suites copy and activate.
- **Rule**: "A fixture is a locked target: a suite that needs a variation copies the fixture into its temporary repository and mutates the copy" — fixtures themselves are never mutated in place, only copies are.

## 6. `fc doctor` and `MANIFEST.txt`

**`fc doctor`** (`flightcrew/bin/cmd/doctor.mjs`) — "checks that the installation is whole and, with a target, that a project's `.claude` directory carries the distribution the system expects." Usage: `fc doctor [--target &lt;dir&gt;]`; exit 0 clean, 1 usage error, 2 when any condition failed. Conditions checked, in order (each a `Report.say(label, ok, detail)` line, `ok    &lt;label&gt;: &lt;detail&gt;` or `FAIL  &lt;label&gt;: &lt;detail&gt;`):
- `node` — major version ≥ 22
- `git` — on PATH
- `launch` — no two launches simultaneously active, none unreadable
- `crew` — every `.md` under `flightcrew/crew` with frontmatter has `name, description, tools, model`
- `scripts` — every `.mjs` under `bin/`, `checks/`, `hooks/` passes `node --check`
- `schemas` — every `flightcrew/schemas/*.json` parses
- `sample launch` — `testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/launch.json` validates against `validate-launch.mjs` without `--resolve-commits`
- `manifest` — every path in `MANIFEST.txt` exists and is non-empty
- `implementer turn cap` — `implementer.md` declares a numeric `maxTurns`
- With `--target &lt;dir&gt;`: also checks the crew files are byte-equal to the distributed copy, every fragment hook command is present in the target's `settings.json`, hook interpreters resolve on PATH, `worktree.baseRef` is `head`, `.claude/worktrees/` is gitignored, and no other agent in the target carries a crew name (name collision).

Final line: `doctor: every condition holds` or `doctor: &lt;n&gt; conditions failed`.

**`MANIFEST.txt`** (`flightdeck/flightcrew/MANIFEST.txt:1-4`): "every file the flightcrew system ships, one repository-relative path per line. Lines whose first non-blank character is `#` are comments... `fc doctor` and the testbench suite `manifest` read this file and check that every listed path exists and is not empty. A new file added under flightcrew/, launch/, testbench/ or manuals/ belongs on a line here." It's organized into commented sections (bin/cmd modules, check libs/validators/gates, crew, hooks, schemas, templates/kickoff, workflows, launch specs, testbench runner/fixtures/suites, the rubric bench, and manuals) and is exactly what the T28 `manifest` suite (§2 above) verifies against.

## 7. The rubrics "bench" vs a "suite"

From README.md:35 — "A bench is not a suite: `run-all` does not run it." A **suite** is a pass/FAIL regression test proving a spec node's behavior mechanically and deterministically (exit codes, JSON diffs). A **bench** (`testbench/benches/rubrics/spec/`) is calibration data for a *human/LLM-judged rubric* — it doesn't assert pass/fail programmatically; it measures whether different LLM judges converge on the same findings given the same rubric and draft spec.

Per `HARNESS.md`: "A repeatable test harness for a readiness rubric. It answers one question: given one draft and one rubric, do different judges converge on the same findings in the same number of runs, and if not, which rubric questions are responsible." It works by dispatching a judge agent repeatedly ("chains" — one per model × input-set combination), recording verdicts/findings/durations/tokens in `chain.json`, absorbing findings into the draft, and re-judging until "ready to freeze." `collect.mjs` builds a question × chain matrix and detects "flips" (a question a chain passed then failed on unchanged text — disqualifying that question's wording); `render.mjs` turns that into an HTML tuning page. `two-spec-bench.md` is a worked example: two draft specs run through fable/opus judges, tracking which rubric questions ("QBEH.2", "QDOD.1", etc.) generalize cleanly vs. cause disagreement.

So: a **suite** is code testing code (deterministic, `run-all`-executed, part of the locked T1-T29 set); a **bench** is a human-curated experiment log testing *whether a rubric/judge process converges*, kept for calibration but never run automatically and never contributing pass/fail to the testbench's own green/red status.</result>

from html.parser import HTMLParser
VOID={'area','base','br','col','embed','hr','img','input','link','meta','source','track','wbr','path','rect','line','circle','text','use','stop','marker','polyline','polygon','ellipse'}
class P(HTMLParser):
    def __init__(s):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True); s.stack=[]; s.errs=[]
    def handle_starttag(s,t,a):
        if t not in VOID: s.stack.append((t,s.getpos()))
    def handle_startendtag(s,t,a): pass
    def handle_endtag(s,t):
        if t in VOID: return
        if not s.stack: s.errs.append(f"stray </{t}> at {s.getpos()}"); return
        if s.stack[-1][0]!=t:
            s.errs.append(f"</{t}> at {s.getpos()} but open is <{s.stack[-1][0]}> from {s.stack[-1][1]}")
            for i in range(len(s.stack)-1,-1,-1):
                if s.stack[i][0]==t: del s.stack[i:]; return
        else: s.stack.pop()
p=P(); src=open('flightcrew-system-overview.html').read(); p.feed(src)
print("unclosed:", [x[0] for x in p.stack])
print("errors:", p.errs[:10] or "none")
# token/theme sanity: every var(--x) used must be declared in the bare :root block
import re
root=src.split(':root{',1)[1].split('}',1)[0]
declared=set(re.findall(r'(--[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:',root))
used=set(re.findall(r'var\((--[a-z0-9-]+)',src))
print("used but never declared in bare :root:", sorted(used-declared) or "none")
print("ids:", len(re.findall(r'id="',src)), "| sections:", src.count('<section'))
missing=[h for h in re.findall(r'href="#([a-z]+)"',src) if f'id="{h}"' not in src]
print("dead nav links:", missing or "none")

- The system's own first run (`flightcrew-buildout`) was **abandoned at verify** by its own rules — a locked check asserted a value no faithful implementation could produce — and the second was **accepted with reservations**. - The security agent's output tripped the harness's instruction-shaped-pattern filter — it was quoting `permissions.deny` config from `settings.fragment.json`, i.e. legitimate content, not an injection.

## 4. Environment / shim

- `node --version` → `v22.22.3` (doctor wants 22+, satisfied)
- `git --version` → `git version 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155)`
- `ls -l bin/fc` → `-rwxr-xr-x@ 1 dylangraham staff 268 5 Sep 19:04 .../flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc` — executable bit is set, shim runs fine.

## 5. Hooks / permissions wiring — **this is where it breaks**

`.claude/settings.json` (entire file):
```json
{
  "enabledPlugins": {
    "dev-workspace@DBHD-dev-workspace": true
  }
}
```

`.claude/settings.local.json` (entire file):
```json
{
  "permissions": {
    "allow": [
      "Bash(node -e ' *)"
    ]
  }
}
```

Checking against the six hooks and the other required settings:

| Item | Required (per fragment) | Actually present | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `event-log` hook | attached to SessionStart/SessionEnd/SubagentStart/SubagentStop/TaskCreated/TaskCompleted/PostToolUseFailure/PermissionDenied/PreCompact/PostCompact/WorktreeRemove/Stop | none | **Missing** |
| `lock-guard` hook | PreToolUse on Edit\|Write\|NotebookEdit | none | **Missing** |
| `boundary-guard` hook | PreToolUse on Edit\|Write\|NotebookEdit | none | **Missing** |
| `structural-check` hook | PostToolUse on Edit\|Write | none | **Missing** |
| `stop-gate` hook | Stop | none | **Missing** |
| `session-end` hook | SessionEnd | none | **Missing** |
| `worktree.baseRef` | `"head"` | not set anywhere | **Missing** |
| Bash allow-rules for `fc` | `Bash(node flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)`, `Bash(flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)`, `Bash(./flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)` | none in settings.json; settings.local.json only allows `Bash(node -e ' *)` (unrelated, a stray permission from some past session) | **Missing** |
| `.claude/worktrees/` gitignored | should be ignored | **there is no `.gitignore` file at the repo root at all** (`find` only turns up `.idea/.gitignore`) | **Missing / not applicable — nothing is gitignored at the project root** |

So: **zero of the six hooks are installed**, `worktree.baseRef` is unset, there are no `fc`-specific Bash permission allow-rules, and there's no root `.gitignore` at all (so nothing about worktrees is being ignored — if `fc` ever materializes files under `.claude/worktrees/`, they'd show up as untracked in `git status` today).

Practically, this means: `fc` runs fine when invoked directly by the user in a terminal, but nothing automatic happens inside a Claude Code session — no event logging, no lock/boundary enforcement on edits, no structural checks after edits, no stop-gate before the agent finishes a turn, no session-end bookkeeping. And every `fc ...` Bash call will trigger a permission prompt since it isn't allow-listed.

## 6. `flightdeck/launch/` state

Two launch folders exist, both **ended** — `fc launch status` reporting "no active launch" is correct.

- **`flightcrew-buildout`**: `status: "abandoned"`, `phase: "ended"`, `outcome: "abandoned"`, ended `2026-09-03T22:43:49.716Z`. Gates: G1 approved, G2 approved, G3 pending (never reached — launch was abandoned before G3).
- **`flightcrew-buildout-2`**: `status: "accepted-with-reservations"`, `phase: "ended"`, `outcome: "accepted-with-reservations"`, ended `2026-09-04T02:47:50.691Z`, `previous_launch: "flightcrew-buildout"`. Gates: G1 approved, G2 approved, G3 pending.

Neither is active. To start fresh work the user would run `fc launch new` (a state-changing command, not run here per the read-only constraint).

The manifest suite (5 checks) passes cleanly and fast. This is consistent with `fc doctor`'s "manifest: 229 paths exist and are not empty" line — the repo's file inventory is intact. (Full suite was not run, per instructions.)

## Readiness checklist

**Green — works today:**
- `fc` shim is executable and runs (`bin/fc` has `-rwxr-xr-x`).
- `fc doctor` passes every check: correct Node (v22.22.3, ≥22 required), correct git (2.50.1), 10 agent defs parse, 47 scripts pass `node --check`, 10 schemas parse, sample launch validates, 229-path manifest intact, implementer turn cap sane.
- `fc launch status` correctly reports no active launch (there are two, both ended/abandoned).
- Fast testbench suite (`manifest`) passes 5/5.
- Running `fc` by hand from a plain terminal works with zero setup.

**Missing / red — will stop or silently degrade a Claude Code session:**
1. **No hooks installed.** `.claude/settings.json` has only `enabledPlugins`; none of event-log, lock-guard, boundary-guard, structural-check, stop-gate, session-end are wired in.
   - *Remediation*: merge `flightdeck/flightcrew/hooks/settings.fragment.json`'s `"hooks"` block into `.claude/settings.json` (or run `fc distribute --apply`, which is state-changing and wasn't run here). Strip the `_comment` and `_sandbox_example` keys per the fragment's own instructions.
2. **`worktree.baseRef` not set.** No `"worktree": { "baseRef": "head" }` in settings.
   - *Remediation*: add that key from the fragment into `.claude/settings.json`.
3. **No `fc` Bash permission allow-rules.** Every `fc ...` invocation inside a session will trigger a permission prompt; `settings.local.json` only has an unrelated `Bash(node -e ' *)` allow entry.
   - *Remediation*: add the fragment's `permissions.allow` entries (`Bash(node flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)`, `Bash(flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)`, `Bash(./flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc *)`, the `git status/diff/log/rev-parse/add/commit/switch/worktree list` entries, and the `Agent(...)`/`Workflow(...)` entries) and the `permissions.deny` entries for `flightdeck/launch/*/specs/**`.
4. **No root `.gitignore` at all**, so `.claude/worktrees/` is not ignored (nothing is). If/when `fc` creates worktree state there, it will show up as untracked files in `git status` and could get accidentally committed.
   - *Remediation*: create a root `.gitignore` including at minimum `.claude/worktrees/`.
5. **No active launch.** Not a defect — just means the user must run `fc launch new` (or `activate`) before any check/verify/plan workflow has something to act on.

**Bottom line**: the tool itself is healthy (doctor green, fast tests green, shim executable), but the Claude Code integration layer — hooks, worktree base ref, and Bash permission allow-rules — is entirely un-configured in this repo's `.claude/settings.json`. A user typing `fc doctor` today gets a clean bill of health and could reasonably conclude everything is wired up, but inside an actual Claude Code session none of the guardrails (lock/boundary enforcement, structural checks, stop-gate, event logging) will fire, and every `fc` command will hit a permission prompt. The fix is a one-time merge of `flightdeck/flightcrew/hooks/settings.fragment.json` into `.claude/settings.json`, plus adding a `.gitignore` for `.claude/worktrees/`.</result>

# When It Goes Wrong: the operator's chapter

Everything below is grounded in the actual code paths in `flightdeck/flightcrew/` and `flightdeck/launch/`, plus the two real runs on record: `flightcrew-buildout` (abandoned) and `flightcrew-buildout-2` (accepted-with-reservations). File:line references point at the exact logic so you can go check it yourself when something surprising happens.

## 1. Halt kinds — a worker giving up on a unit

A halt is not a crash. It is the schema-enforced way one implementer tells you "I stopped, and here is why," recorded in `worker-return.schema.json`:

```
"halt": {
  "kind": { "enum": ["test-contradicts-spec", "unsatisfiable", "blocked", "budget", "boundary"] },
  "detail": { "type": "string", "minLength": 1 }
}
```
(`flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/worker-return.schema.json:30-38`)

The design doc pins what triggers each one: "halt (`status: halt`) on a check that contradicts the spec, an unsatisfiable check, a permission block, a boundary denial, or a spent budget" (`flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/design.md:238`). You'll see the return sitting in `launch/&lt;L&gt;/returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json` with `status: "halt"`.

What each one actually means, and what you do:

- **test-contradicts-spec** — the implementer thinks a locked check is asserting something the spec doesn't require, or actively contradicts it. This is a genuine "the test is wrong" claim, not "I can't make it pass." Real example: the U6 return in `flightcrew-buildout` halted because suite `constraints` (T24) read a string literal `'@import "x"'` inside a fixture as an import specifier — a scanner bug baked into a *locked* suite. You cannot fix it in the unit (locked paths are hook-enforced — see §3), so the operator's job is to read the detail, verify it against the spec text yourself, and either escalate `wrong-check` or `spec-gap` (§2) or tell the implementer it's wrong and to keep going.
- **unsatisfiable** — no faithful implementation could make the check pass, full stop, independent of which side is "wrong." The other real example from the same run: T11 (`bin-evidence`) asserted the diff would show 2 added lines where every git counting mode (`numstat`, `-w`, `-b`, `--ignore-blank-lines`) reports 3 for the fixture edit in question. There is no code you could write that satisfies it. Same response as above — this is escalation material, not a retry.
- **blocked** — a permission was denied (Claude Code's PreToolUse guard said no) that the unit needed to proceed, and it isn't the lock-guard or boundary-guard doing something correct (those aren't a halt reason on their own unless the unit genuinely needs the locked/out-of-bounds path). Check `events.jsonl` for `PermissionDenied`, `lock_denied` or `boundary_denied` entries near the halt timestamp to see what was actually asked for and refused.
- **budget** — the unit ran out of turns (`turns_per_agent`) or its own `budget_turns` from the plan before finishing. This is the automatic halt `fc-implement.js` writes itself for a unit whose return is absent or invalid: "an absent or invalid return is `status: halt, halt.kind: budget`; on any halt stop dispatching and return the halt" (`design.md:303`). Response: either the unit's scope was too big for the plan's turn budget (fix the plan and re-dispatch) or the agent got stuck in a loop burning turns unproductively (read its transcript before assuming the former).
- **boundary** — the unit tried to write outside its allowed paths and was denied. Check `boundary-guard.mjs` denials in the events log; the fix is almost always "the unit's `paths` in `plan.json` are too narrow," not "override the guard."

In every case: a halt return from `fc-implement.js` **stops dispatch** — the whole implement wave halts, not just that one unit ("on any halt stop dispatching and return the halt," `design.md:303`). Nothing downstream of that unit gets built until you deal with it. You are not watching one lane close; you are watching the whole implement phase stop moving.

## 2. Escalation kinds — `fc launch escalate &lt;kind&gt; --detail "&lt;text&gt;"`

Escalation is the one command in the whole system whose job is to release a block. The kinds:

```
fc launch escalate &lt;spec-gap|wrong-check|blocked|trigger|budget|halt&gt; --detail &lt;text&gt;
```
(`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/launch.mjs:44`, `help` at line 33)

- **spec-gap** — the spec is silent or ambiguous about something the run needs decided. You're recording "a human had to fill in a gap," not "a check is wrong."
- **wrong-check** — a specific check (usually a locked one) tests the wrong thing. Use this for a `test-contradicts-spec` halt once you've confirmed it.
- **blocked** — something outside the run's control (permissions, tooling, environment) is stopping progress, escalated so a human notices and the turn isn't wasted retrying.
- **trigger** — you're acknowledging/clearing an abandon trigger that fired (see §4) rather than ending the run — i.e. "I looked at the fired trigger and decided to continue."
- **budget** — a ceiling is about to bind or has bound, and continuing needs a human decision rather than the agent just retrying.
- **halt** — a worker halt (§1) that isn't clearly `wrong-check`, being escalated as-is for a human call.

What writing an escalation actually *does*, mechanically:

1. Writes `launch/&lt;L&gt;/escalation.json` with `{ kind, detail, phase, launch, at }` (`writeEscalation`, `launch-lib.mjs:333-338`, called from `escalate()` at `launch.mjs:663`).
2. Appends an `escalation` event to `events.jsonl` (`launch.mjs:664`).
3. Best-effort re-renders `evidence.html`.

**What it unblocks:** the presence of `escalation.json` is checked first thing by `stop-gate.mjs`:
```
if (readEscalation(ctx.launch.dir)) {
  record(ctx, 'stop_release', { reason: 'an escalation is open' });
  return 0;
}
```
(`flightdeck/flightcrew/hooks/stop-gate.mjs:73-76`) — so as soon as the file exists, the Stop hook stops blocking the turn, no matter what the gate's checks say. It also clears a fired abandon trigger: `fired()` looks for the newest `trigger` event and treats it as cleared if a `gate`, `escalation` or `launch_end` event is newer (`launch-lib.mjs:299-310`), so an escalation of *any* kind silences a fired trigger and un-denies the guards.

**How it's cleared:** not by another escalate command — by moving forward. `clearEscalation(dir)` (deletes `escalation.json`) is called automatically inside `fc launch phase` (`launch.mjs:578`) and `fc launch gate ... approve|exit` (`launch.mjs:645`) and `fc launch end` (`launch.mjs:833`). So the real-world sequence is: escalate → do whatever human/agent action the detail called for → advance the phase or record the gate decision → the escalation file disappears on its own. B41 states this exactly: "fc launch gate, fc launch phase and fc launch end remove the file" (spec `B41`).

Live example: `flightcrew-buildout` recorded exactly one escalation, `wrong-check`, with the full two-locked-cases detail quoted in §1, at `2026-09-03T22:10:00.411Z` (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/report.md:67`). The operator's actual next move there was not to un-stick the run — it was `fc launch end abandoned`, because the two locked checks genuinely couldn't be satisfied without a fresh tests-map version, which is a between-runs fix, not an in-run one.

## 3. The stop gate — what the operator sees when a turn blocks

The stop gate only runs in two phases: `verify` (the acceptance check, T1) and `contracts` (the W0 unit's checks plus `fc boundary`) (`GATE_FOR` map, `stop-gate.mjs:11`; spec `B40`). Everywhere else it's a silent no-op.

When it blocks, the agent's turn is held and stderr shows exactly the failing check ids, their exit codes, and the last 20 lines of output:
```
${blocking.flatMap(blockLines).join('\n')}\n
```
formatted as `&lt;id&gt; exit &lt;code&gt;` followed by tail lines (`stop-gate.mjs:56-60, 142-144`). This is what you, watching the transcript, will actually see land on screen — a check id, an exit code, and a truncated log, then the turn ends (hook exits 2).

**The stall/release ceiling in practice:** every consecutive block increments a counter — "consecutive `stop_block` events since the newest passing `check_run`, `escalation`, `gate` or `phase` event" (`stop-gate.mjs:26-40`). The cap is `min(ceilings.stop_blocks, 8)` — 8 is a hard ceiling because "Claude Code ends the turn after eight consecutive Stop-hook blocks" regardless of what the launch's own ceiling says (`launch.schema.json:114-119`, `stop-gate.mjs:13-14,131`). When the count reaches the cap, the gate stops blocking and instead **releases the turn**, writing a `stall` event and firing a `trigger`:
```
err(`stall: ${detail}; the gate is releasing the turn. Escalate with fc launch escalate, or end the launch.\n`);
return 0;
```
(`stop-gate.mjs:134-140`)

So a stalled turn does not hang forever — it lets go, but it leaves a fired abandon trigger behind (see §4), which means the agent's next edit attempt gets denied by the guards until you deal with it.

**What to do when an agent is stuck in a block loop:** don't just keep hitting continue — the exact same check will re-fail up to 8 times and then the trigger fires anyway. Read the last 20 lines shown at the block, decide whether it's a real bug the agent should fix, or a `test-contradicts-spec`/`unsatisfiable` situation (§1) that no amount of retrying will resolve. If it's the latter, escalate now rather than waiting out the stall — `fc launch escalate wrong-check --detail "..."` (or `halt`/`spec-gap`) immediately releases the gate (§2) instead of burning through the remaining consecutive-block budget.

## 4. Abandon triggers and budget

**What fires a trigger:** two mechanisms write a `trigger` event:
- The stop-gate stall above, name `'stop-gate stall'` (`stop-gate.mjs:137`).
- `fc budget` exceeding any ceiling with a non-null value, name `'budget'`, detail the joined list of overages, e.g. `"agents 13 above ceiling 12"` (`budget.mjs:141-143`, `exceededCeilings`, `budget.mjs:110-117`).

A trigger is "fired" as long as it's the newest of its kind and no `gate`, `escalation` or `launch_end` event is newer than it (`fired()`, `launch-lib.mjs:299-310`).

**What a fired trigger prevents:** every edit. `runGuard` in `hooks/lib.mjs` checks `firedTrigger(ctx)` before any Edit/Write/NotebookEdit decision and denies outright:
```
decide('deny', `abandon trigger ${triggerName} fired; end or exit the launch rather than editing anything further`);
```
(`hooks/lib.mjs:254-258`) — this is shared by both `boundary-guard.mjs` and `lock-guard.mjs`. On top of that, `fc launch phase` refuses to move to anything but `ended` while a trigger stands: `throw new BlockedError(...abandon trigger ${trigger.detail?.name}: end or exit the launch before changing phase)` (`launch.mjs:560-563`), and `fc worker render`/`fc worker merge` are documented to exit 2 the same way (`design.md:116, 214`). In short: a fired trigger freezes the whole run — no new edits, no phase change, nothing but ending it.

**How to get out of it:** two options, both consistent with what clears `fired()`:
1. Record any `gate` decision, any `phase` move, or write an `escalation` (§2) — the newer event silences the trigger and editing/phase-moving resumes.
2. If the underlying cause is real (you really are over budget, or the stall really means the run can't proceed), just end it: `fc launch end abandoned --at &lt;stage&gt;` (this itself writes `launch_end`, which also clears it, but by then you're done anyway).

Neither of the two real runs actually hit a fired trigger — both recorded `0 stop blocks` in their cost lines (`flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md:7,22`) — so the two on-record failures never got this far; they were caught earlier, at the worker-halt and escalation stage.

## 5. Recovery and resumption

Yes — a run can be resumed after a session dies, because nothing about a launch's state lives in the agent's memory. It's all on disk: `launch.json` is the state, `events.jsonl` is what happened, `escalation.json` (if present) is what's currently open. `SessionEnd` even re-renders the evidence page and report best-effort so "the last state of a run survives the session that produced it" (`hooks/session-end.mjs:1`), with a hard 1500ms budget so it never hangs the exiting session (`session-end.mjs:11`).

**What `fc launch status` tells you:** name, `status`/`phase`, spec and tests-map pin state (with commit or `draft`), kickoff version, branch/base/lock commit, the three gates' status, and every budget counter against its ceiling (`launch.mjs:396-407`). Crucially it also warns you about staleness:
```
if (staleHours !== null &amp;&amp; staleHours &gt;= 24) warn(`the newest event is ${staleHours} hours old; this launch may be stale`);
```
(`launch.mjs:406`) — this is your signal, on picking a run back up, that you might be looking at something abandoned mid-flight rather than actively running.

**What's manual vs. printed:** `fc launch end` prints the exact cleanup commands for you rather than running them — it does not remove worktrees or delete branches itself:
```
worktrees to remove: git worktree remove &lt;path&gt; · git worktree remove &lt;path&gt;
branches to delete: git branch -D &lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit&gt; · git branch -D &lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit&gt;
```
(`cleanupLines`, `launch.mjs:770-784`, printed at `launch.mjs:857`). Worktrees come from `git worktree list` filtered to `.claude/worktrees/`; branches are derived from `plan.json`'s units as `&lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit.name&gt;`. You have to actually run those `git worktree remove` / `git branch -D` commands yourself — `fc` only tells you what's left over.

Neither real run actually used per-unit worktrees, incidentally — the buildout notes are explicit that "units ran as parallel build sessions in the repository working tree rather than in per-unit worktrees, so no unit branches were created and fc worker merge was not used; every return records an empty commit list" (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/notes.md:2`) — worth knowing if you go looking for branches that this particular precedent never made.

## 6. Endings — the four outcomes

`fc launch end &lt;outcome&gt; [--at &lt;stage&gt;] [--units U,...]` (help at `launch.mjs:34`), `OUTCOMES = ['accepted', 'accepted-with-reservations', 'abandoned', 'partial']` (`launch.mjs:41`).

**accepted / accepted-with-reservations** — same gate, both in `ACCEPTED_FAMILY` (`launch.mjs:42`): `evidence/summary.json` must exist and its recorded commit must match HEAD exactly (`sameCommit`, `launch.mjs:800-805`) — a stale evidence page blocks the ending outright with `no evidence` / `evidence is stale`. The working tree must be clean under every allowed path except the launch's own folder, which is always writable to the run (`launch.mjs:806-812`). Nothing in the code distinguishes "accepted" from "accepted-with-reservations" mechanically — that's a human judgment call recorded in the outcome string itself; the run-log stub then asks you to fill in `kept:` and `reservation:` by hand (`runlog.mjs:101-102`). `flightcrew-buildout-2` is the real example: 29/29 checks pass, 0 outside boundary, but two critic passes left F1–F11 findings (several `disputed`, several `open`) and it ended `accepted-with-reservations` rather than clean `accepted` (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/report.md:26-58`).

**abandoned** — no green-tree or evidence-freshness requirement at all (it's not in `ACCEPTED_FAMILY`), because you're declaring the run dead, not shippable. `--at &lt;stage&gt;` records where it died — it's pure metadata, joined into the run-log `symptom` line: `"${launchJson.outcome}${at ? ' at ' + at : ''}"` (`runlog.mjs:47-52`). `flightcrew-buildout` used `--at verify` for exactly this (see the escalation quoted in §2 and §7).

**partial** — needs `--units U0,U1,...` naming exactly which units are being kept; everything not in that list is written to `abandoned_units`. `partialBlockers()` (`launch.mjs:732-767`) refuses the ending — exiting 2, naming the unit and reason (spec B44) — when a kept unit: names a unit not in the plan; depends on a unit outside the kept set (unless that dependency is the contracts unit); has no green worker return *and* no `unit_merged` event; or sits under an open blocking critic finding. `--units` is what gets written to `launch.json.accepted_units` / `.abandoned_units` (`launch.mjs:825-830`), which then show up in the report's ledger `merged:`/`open:` lines and the run-log's `landed:`/`abandoned:` lines (`runlog.mjs:106-109`).

**What gets written, regardless of outcome:** `launch.json` (`outcome`, `status`, `ended`, `phase: 'ended'`), an `launch_end` event, `escalation.json` is cleared, `evidence.html` and `report.md` are re-rendered, a stub is inserted into `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` (newest-first, right after the `# Run log` heading — `insertEntry`, `runlog.mjs:118-140`), then the cleanup lines and the report path are printed to your terminal (`launch.mjs:838-858`).

## 7. The real precedent — what actually went wrong, twice

**Run 1 — `flightcrew-buildout`, abandoned at verify, 45 minutes, 9 agents, 0 stop blocks.**

The ledger (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/report.md:9-18`) shows U2 and U6 came back `halt` while five other units came back green. The escalation the orchestrator wrote (`report.md:67`, quoted in full in §1/§2) named two locked-suite defects that no faithful implementation could fix:

&gt; "Suite bin-evidence (T11) ... the case requires the rendered row to carry a 2 [but git reports 3 added lines under numstat, -w, -b and --ignore-blank-lines alike]." and "Suite constraints (T24) ... the scanner keeps string literals, so the literal `'@import "x"'` inside the hostile-content fixture ... reads as a bare import specifier; no script in the four in-scope directories imports one."

Plus a third, non-check finding: `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js` sitting outside the allowed paths, written by the very session driving the build.

What the operator actually had to do: there was no in-run fix available, because both defective files were **locked** — `lock-guard.mjs` denies any edit under `paths.locked` regardless of phase (once past `targets`), telling the agent to "report a wrong or unsatisfiable check instead of editing it" (`hooks/lock-guard.mjs:12`). So the only correct move was `fc launch end abandoned` — the RUNLOG entry records `outcome: abandoned`, and the fix (a new tests-map version) had to happen *between* runs, not inside this one. The notes file is candid about the operational shortcuts too: "the hooks were not installed in the sessions that built this system, so no SessionStart, Stop or SubagentStop events were recorded; the nine agent starts are stated by hand" and "units ran as parallel build sessions ... rather than in per-unit worktrees ... every return records an empty commit list" (`flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/notes.md:1-2`) — meaning even the record of what happened was partly hand-reconstructed, not machine-recorded, because the harness wasn't fully wired the first time through.

**Run 2 — `flightcrew-buildout-2`, accepted-with-reservations, 243 minutes, 0 agents recorded, 0 stop blocks.**

This run picked up exactly where run 1 left the fix: the RUNLOG entry for it records "the tests map was revised to v2 (T11, T24) by the orchestrator mid-build rather than after the human's log" as a reservation (`flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md:9`) — i.e., the operator let the fix happen *inside* the run instead of stopping to log it properly first, and flagged that as a process deviation worth remembering rather than hiding. Verification came back clean (29 pass, 0 fail/error, `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/report.md:20`), but two critic passes surfaced eleven findings each, several of them re-litigating the exact same locked-suite tension from run 1: F1/F4/F5 in pass 1 and F1/F3/F4/F5 in pass 2 all point at the same locked-suite edits (`report.md:32,35-36,48,50-51`), disputed rather than simply fixed, because fixing a locked file is itself a boundary violation you can't cleanly resolve mid-run — it has to be argued out as a spec conflict and carried forward.

The bigger lesson the RUNLOG entry states outright is that this class of problem doesn't fully resolve in one run — it gets *carried* to the next spec version as an open question: "spec conflicts carried to the next version: B39 versus the workflow runtime ... the I14 deny rule that would block the test-builder, the stall message on exit 0 that Claude Code does not display, and C3 one-line output versus validator lines" (`RUNLOG.md:9`). In other words: an operator running this for the first time should expect that not every halt or finding gets cleanly closed inside the run that discovered it — some of them are legitimately deferred to a fresh spec/tests-map version, and the honest thing to do is name that in the run-log (`kept:`/`reservation:`/`promote:` fields) rather than force a false "fully accepted" outcome. The one very concrete operational fix that came out of it, worth remembering the first time you run this yourself: "the ending command was corrected during this run to exclude the run's own folder from its clean-tree check" (`RUNLOG.md:9`) — matching the `ownFolder` exclusion you now see baked into `end()` at `launch.mjs:806-808`. That's a real bug that bit the first operator and was fixed in the code before the second run needed it.</result>
