<result># flightcrew operator's primer — command surface and refusal reference

Source root for everything below: `~/Projects/flightdeck/flightdeck/flightcrew/` (note: nested one level deeper than `flightdeck/flightcrew/` — the repo has an outer `flightdeck/` folder containing another `flightdeck/`). All line numbers refer to files under that path.

---

## 1. The literal command sequence, frozen spec → `fc launch end`

Global flags on every `fc` invocation: `--launch &lt;name&gt;` and `--json`, pulled out wherever they appear (`bin/fc.mjs:256-276`). Exit codes throughout: **0** ok, **1** usage/environment error, **2** blocked/failed check (`checks/lib/output.mjs:15`).

### Pre-launch (no launch resolved yet — `lint` and `validate` are in `WITHOUT_LAUNCH`, `bin/fc.mjs:30`)

1. **`fc lint spec &lt;path&gt; [--repo &lt;root&gt;] [--deliverable &lt;path&gt;]...`**
   `bin/cmd/lint.mjs:16-27`. Runs `spec-readiness-lint.mjs`. Prints the linter's own `error: &lt;message&gt; — [&lt;rule&gt;]` / `warn:  &lt;message&gt;` lines. Exit 0 clean, 2 a rule failed, 1 usage.

2. **`fc validate spec &lt;path&gt; --for-freeze`**
   `bin/cmd/validate.mjs:48-58` dispatches to `validate-spec.mjs`. `--for-freeze` "rehearses the freeze gate against a draft" (`checks/validators/validate-spec.mjs:8`). Exit 0 clean, 2 on any error (or any warning if you also pass `--strict`).

3. **`fc validate tests-map &lt;path&gt;`**
   Same dispatcher → `validate-tests-map.mjs`. No `--for-freeze` flag exists for this validator (only `--spec &lt;file&gt;` and `--strict`, `checks/validators/validate-tests-map.mjs:2`).

At this point you still have nothing under `flightdeck/launch/`.

### Phase `targets` — creating and activating the run

4. **`fc launch new &lt;spec-path&gt; [--name N] [--kickoff base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;] [--branch B] [--allow &lt;glob&gt;]...`**
   `bin/cmd/launch.mjs:256-312`. Creates the launch folder as `status: draft`, `phase: targets`, copies the spec, renders `kickoff.md`, writes `launch.json`, `events.jsonl`, `evidence/`, `returns/`, `review/`. On success prints exactly:
   ```
   export FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;
   ```
   (`launch.mjs:310`) — literally the line you're meant to `eval`/export next.

5. **`export FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;`** — take the printed line verbatim.

6. **`fc launch pin tests-map &lt;path&gt; [--allow-draft]`**
   `launch.mjs:412-457`. Copies the map into the launch's `specs/&lt;spec&gt;/` folder, sets `paths.allowed`/`paths.locked`, sets `enforce_boundary: true`, sets `lock_commit = HEAD`. Prints:
   ```
   tests map pinned: &lt;path&gt; @ &lt;commit|draft&gt;; lock &lt;short-lock&gt;
   allow these commands so the checks can run:
     Bash(&lt;command&gt;)
   ```
   (lines 453-455) — the `Bash(...)` lines are what you paste into your permission allowlist.

7. **`fc launch activate [&lt;name&gt;] [--allow-draft]`**
   `launch.mjs:316-355`. Moves `status: draft → active`. Refuses if another launch is already active. On success: `launch &lt;name&gt; is active in phase &lt;phase&gt;`.

8. **(optional) `fc launch kickoff [--parts base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;]`**
   `launch.mjs:461-468`. Re-renders `kickoff.md`. Only needed if you want a different shape/task part combination than `new` used, or to restore `kickoff.md` byte-for-byte after a hand edit (the header comment even says: "re-rendering an unchanged launch restores exactly the bytes the last render wrote", `launch.mjs:157-159`).

### Phase `targets → plan`

9. **`fc launch phase plan`**
   `launch.mjs:553-582`. Must be in `targets` (or pass `--force`). Runs `planBlockers()` (see §2) before writing anything. On success: `phase targets → plan`.

10. **`fc plan write &lt;json-path&gt;` (or `fc plan write --stdin`)**
    `bin/cmd/plan.mjs:125-148`. Stages the candidate, runs `validate-plan` against it, and **only if it validates** writes `plan.json` and renders `plan.md`. Success: `plan written: &lt;path&gt;/plan.md`. A failing plan leaves nothing written (exit 2).

11. **`fc launch gate G1 approve [--note &lt;text&gt;]`**
    `launch.mjs:613-653`. Must currently be in `contracts`'s predecessor phase `plan` (i.e. `source==='plan'` for G1) unless `--force`. No blocker function runs for G1 itself. Approving **moves `plan → contracts` automatically**. Success: `gate G1 approved; phase plan → contracts`.

### Phase `contracts` (wave 0)

Build/run the contracts unit's checks here (`fc check &lt;T-ids&gt;` — see §pre-existing check machinery) so gate G2's blockers are satisfied.

12. **`fc launch gate G2 approve`**
    Runs `gate2Blockers()` first (§2) — every check named by the plan's `contracts` unit (or the launch's `acceptance` id as fallback) must have fresh, non-error evidence at/after the lock commit. On success, **moves `contracts → implement`**: `gate G2 approved; phase contracts → implement`.

### Phase `implement` — per unit, repeat:

13. **`fc worker render &lt;unit-id&gt;`**
    `bin/cmd/worker.mjs:73-103`. Must be in phase `implement`; refuses if an abandon trigger has fired. Writes `returns/&lt;unit&gt;.prompt.md` and prints the sealed dispatch prompt (dispatch this to the implementer subagent).

14. **(the implementer works, then) `fc return worker &lt;file&gt; --unit &lt;U&gt;`** (equivalently `fc worker return &lt;unit&gt; &lt;file&gt;`)
    `bin/cmd/return.mjs:74-114` / `worker.mjs:224-227`. Validates the return against `worker-return` schema; only if valid, stores `returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json` and appends a `return` event. Success: `return: worker &lt;unit&gt; stored at returns/&lt;unit&gt;.json`.

15. **`fc check &lt;check-ids&gt;`** (per-unit checks, run in the worker's own worktree, or by you to confirm)
    `bin/cmd/check.mjs:358-409`.

16. **`fc worker merge &lt;unit-id&gt;`**
    `worker.mjs:134-213`. Requires: a stored return with `status: "green"`; every dependency already merged (a `unit_merged` event exists for each); repo checked out on the launch's own `branch`; the unit branch exists; merge doesn't conflict; the unit's checks pass on the merged tree. On success it also removes the worktree and deletes the unit branch, then prints `merged: &lt;unit&gt; from &lt;branch&gt; at &lt;commit&gt;`.

Repeat 13-16 for every unit in the plan.

### Phase `implement → verify`

17. **`fc launch phase verify`**
    No dedicated blocker function fires for this target phase (only `plan` and `review` have one, `launch.mjs:568`) — just the "is this the very next phase" check.

18. **`fc verifier render [--pass n]`**
    `bin/cmd/verifier.mjs`. Must be phase `verify`; requires `evidence/summary.json` fresh at HEAD (else `DispatchError`, see §2). Writes `returns/verify-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md`.

19. **`fc return verifier &lt;file&gt; --pass &lt;n&gt;`**
    Stores `returns/verify-&lt;n&gt;.json` after schema validation.

20. **`fc verify`**
    `bin/cmd/verify.mjs`. Runs, in order: `check all`, `boundary`, `locked`, `budget` (re-invoked as a subprocess so the budget counter has one implementation). Success line: `verify: N pass, N fail, N error, N skipped; boundary clean; no locked change; within budget`. This is the command whose output `evidence/summary.json`, `evidence/boundary.json`, `evidence/locked.json` you need fresh before the next phase move.

### Phase `verify → review`

21. **`fc launch phase review`**
    Runs `reviewBlockers()` (§2) — needs a fresh `summary.json` at HEAD with 0 fail/0 error, a `boundary.json` with nothing outside, and a `locked.json` with nothing locked-and-changed.

22. **`fc critic render [--pass n]`**
    `bin/cmd/critic.mjs`. Must be phase `review`. Writes `review/pass-&lt;n&gt;.prompt.md` with the diff since lock, evidence, locked-change list.

23. **`fc return critic &lt;file&gt; --pass &lt;n&gt;`**
    Stores `review/pass-&lt;n&gt;.json` after schema validation.

24. **(if a finding needs a state change) `fc return critic --resolve F&lt;n&gt; --commit &lt;sha&gt; [--dispute "&lt;reason&gt;"]`**
    `bin/cmd/return.mjs:83-97` → `worker/return.mjs:116-136`. Writes `review/resolutions.json` (never edits the pass file itself). `state` becomes `resolved` (with `--commit`) or `disputed` (with `--dispute`).

25. **`fc launch gate G3 approve [--note &lt;text&gt;]`** (or `exit`)
    G3 has no phase-move side effect (`source` is `null` for G3, `launch.mjs:628-629`) and no blocker function — it is purely a recorded decision. `exit` prints: `gate G3 exited; now run: fc launch end abandoned --at G3`.

### Phase `review → report → ended`

26. **`fc launch phase report`** — next-phase move, no dedicated blockers.

27. **(optional manual re-renders — normally done automatically) `fc evidence`**, **`fc report`**, **`fc runlog stub`** — see §5, "idempotent".

28. **`fc launch end &lt;accepted|accepted-with-reservations|abandoned|partial&gt; [--at &lt;stage&gt;] [--units U0,U1,...]`**
    `launch.mjs:786-861`. For any accepted-family outcome: needs `evidence/summary.json` fresh at HEAD, and the working tree clean under every allowed path except the launch's own folder. For `partial`: needs `--units` and passes `partialBlockers()` (§2). On success it **automatically** re-renders `evidence.html`, re-renders `report.md`, and inserts the `RUNLOG.md` stub (`launch.mjs:836-855`), then prints the git worktree/branch cleanup lines and the path to `report.md`.

### After merge to main

29. **`fc launch land --commit &lt;sha&gt; [--pr &lt;url&gt;] [--evidence-commit &lt;sha&gt;]`**
    `launch.mjs:681-706`. Requires `evidence/summary.json` to match the (evidence-)commit and record 0 fail/0 error. Writes `launchJson.landed`. Success: `landed at &lt;short-sha&gt; · &lt;pr-url&gt;`.

---

## 2. Every precondition that refuses — "why did it just refuse me"

### `planBlockers(ctx)` — `launch.mjs:499-520` (gates `fc launch phase plan`)
Triggered by, each producing one line, joined under `BlockedError(['phase plan refused:', ...])` → **exit 2**:
- spec still a draft, no `--allow-draft`: `'the pinned spec is a draft; activate or pin with --allow-draft'`
- no tests map pinned at all: `'no tests map is pinned; run fc launch pin tests-map'`
- tests map draft, no `--allow-draft`: `'the pinned tests map is a draft; pin it with --allow-draft'`
- a check's baseline `observed` word disagrees with (or is absent from) its `expect` word: `` `${id}: no baseline was observed` `` or `` `${id}: observed ${observed} where the map expects ${expect}` `` (`baselineDisagreements`, lines 473-485)
- `launch.json` fails `validate-launch`: `'launch.json does not validate'` + the validator's own lines
- `kickoff.md` fails `validate-kickoff`: `'kickoff.md does not validate'` + the validator's own lines

### `reviewBlockers(ctx)` — `launch.mjs:523-544` (gates `fc launch phase review`)
- `evidence/summary.json` missing: `'evidence/summary.json is absent; run fc verify'`
- summary commit ≠ HEAD: `` `evidence/summary.json is at ${short}, HEAD is ${short}` ``
- any fail/error in summary counts: `` `evidence records ${fail} failed and ${error} errored checks` ``
- `evidence/boundary.json` missing: `'evidence/boundary.json is absent; run fc boundary'`
- boundary has outside paths: `` `${n} changed paths are outside the boundary` ``
- `evidence/locked.json` missing: `'evidence/locked.json is absent; run fc locked'`
- locked-path changes present: `` `${n} changed paths match a locked glob` ``
All joined under `BlockedError(['phase review refused:', ...])` → **exit 2**.

### `gate2Blockers(ctx)` — `launch.mjs:596-611` (gates `fc launch gate G2 approve`)
For every check id named by the plan's `contracts` unit (fallback: the launch's `acceptance` id):
- no evidence file at all: `` `${id} has no evidence; run fc check ${id}` ``
- verdict `error`: `` `${id} has verdict error` ``
- evidence ran before the lock commit: `` `${id} last ran at ${short}, before the lock commit ${short}` ``
Joined under `BlockedError(['gate G2 refused:', ...])` → **exit 2**.

### `firedTrigger(dir)` / `fired()` — `launch-lib.mjs:299-315`, checked in `phase()` (`launch.mjs:560-563`) and in `worker render`/`merge`
An abandon trigger (`event: 'trigger'`, e.g. the one `fc budget` writes when a ceiling is exceeded, `budget.mjs:142`) is "fired" when it's newer than the most recent `gate`, `escalation` or `launch_end` event. While fired:
- `fc launch phase &lt;anything but ended&gt;` → `` `abandon trigger ${name}: end or exit the launch before changing phase` `` — **exit 2** (`BlockedError`)
- `fc worker render` / `fc worker merge` → `` `abandon trigger fired: ${name}; end or exit the launch before dispatching` `` — **exit 2**
Clearing it: `fc launch gate ... `, `fc launch escalate ...`, or `fc launch phase ended` via `fc launch end` (any of these appends a clearing event; `clearEscalation`/phase-move code also runs).

### `partialBlockers(dir, plan, keep)` — `launch.mjs:732-767` (gates `fc launch end partial --units ...`)
- a named unit isn't in the plan: `` `${id} is not a unit of this plan` ``
- a kept unit depends on one not being kept: `` `${id} depends on ${dep}, which is not among the units kept` ``
- a kept unit has no green return and no `unit_merged` event: `` `${id} has no green worker return and no unit_merged event` ``
- a kept unit's paths overlap an **open, blocking** critic finding: `` `${id} has the open blocking finding ${finding.id} under its paths (${finding.file})` ``
Joined under `BlockedError(['partial ending refused:', ...])` → **exit 2**.

### Phase/gate structural guards (`launch.mjs:phase`, `gate`)
- no phase named: `` `fc launch phase needs one of ${PHASES.join(', ')}` `` — exit 1
- unknown phase name: `` `unknown phase ${to}; expected one of ...` `` — exit 1
- phase skipped (not the immediate next phase) without `--force`: `` `illegal phase change: ${from} → ${to}; the next phase is ${next}` `` — exit 1
- gate id/decision malformed: `` `fc launch gate needs one of G1, G2, G3` `` / `'fc launch gate needs approve or exit'` — exit 1
- re-recording an already-decided gate without `--force`: `` `gate ${id} is already ${recorded}; pass --force to record it again` `` — exit 1
- approving G1/G2 from the wrong phase without `--force`: `` `gate ${id} approve moves ${source} → ${moveTo}; the launch is in phase ${from}` `` — exit 1

### `fc launch new` — `launch.mjs:259-312`
- no spec path given: `'fc launch new needs the path of the spec to run'`
- file not found: `` `no spec at ${given}` ``
- spec has no `name` field: `` `${given} carries no spec name` ``
- launch folder already exists: `` `launch exists: ${path}` ``
- kickoff assembly problems (before anything is written): `'a kickoff needs parts: base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;'`, `` `a kickoff starts with the base part: ${names}` ``, `` `kickoff part not found: ${path}` ``, `` `kickoff part has no version comment: ${path}` ``, `` `a kickoff names one shape-&lt;s&gt; and one task-&lt;t&gt; part; got ${partsSpec}` ``
All exit 1 (`UsageError`).

### `fc launch activate` — `launch.mjs:316-355`
- no name given and not exactly one draft: `'fc launch activate needs the name of the launch to activate'`
- target not a draft: `` `launch ${name} has status ${status}; only a draft launch can be activated` ``
- another launch already active: `` `launch ${names} is active; end it before activating ${name}` ``
- spec still draft, no `--allow-draft`: `` `spec not frozen: ${path} carries no commit; pass --allow-draft` ``
- tests map still draft, no `--allow-draft`: `` `tests map not frozen: ${path} carries no commit; pass --allow-draft` ``
- pinned map names a different spec/version: `` `pin mismatch: the pinned tests map names spec ${n} v${v}, the launch names ${n2} v${v2}` ``
All exit 1.

### `fc launch pin tests-map` — `launch.mjs:412-457`
- wrong sub: `'fc launch pin: the only pin is tests-map'`
- no path given: `'fc launch pin tests-map needs the path of the map to pin'`
- file not found: `` `no tests map at ${given}` ``
- map draft, no `--allow-draft`: `` `tests map not frozen: ${given} is a draft; pass --allow-draft to pin it anyway` ``
- spec name mismatch: `` `pin mismatch: the map names spec ${m}, the launch names ${s}` ``
All exit 1.

### `fc launch escalate` / `note` / `land`
- `` `fc launch escalate needs one of ${ESCALATION_KINDS.join(', ')}` `` (kinds: `spec-gap, wrong-check, blocked, trigger, budget, halt`) — exit 1
- `'fc launch escalate needs --detail &lt;text&gt; naming the finding'` — exit 1
- `'fc launch note needs the text to record'` — exit 1
- `'fc launch land needs --commit &lt;sha&gt;'` — exit 1
- land, no matching evidence: `` `no evidence at ${short}: evidence/summary.json records ${short}` `` — **exit 2**
- land, evidence shows failures: `` `evidence at ${short} records ${fail} failed and ${error} errored checks` `` — **exit 2**

### `fc launch end`
- bad outcome word: `` `fc launch end needs one of accepted, accepted-with-reservations, abandoned, partial` `` — exit 1
- `partial` without `--units`: `'fc launch end partial needs --units &lt;U0,U1,...&gt;'` — exit 1
- accepted-family, no evidence: `` `no evidence: evidence/summary.json is absent, HEAD is ${short}; run fc verify` `` — **exit 2**
- accepted-family, stale evidence: `` `evidence is stale: evidence/summary.json is at ${short}, HEAD is ${short}` `` — **exit 2**
- accepted-family, dirty tree outside the launch's own folder: `` `working tree not clean under allowed paths: ${dirty.join(', ')}` `` — **exit 2**
- `partial`, no `plan.json`: `'a partial ending needs plan.json to know the units'` — **exit 2**

### `fc worker render` / `merge` (`worker.mjs`)
- wrong phase for render: `` `fc worker render: the launch is in phase ${phase}, and a unit is dispatched in phase implement` `` — exit 1
- no `plan.json`: `'no plan.json in this launch: run fc plan write before dispatching a unit'` — exit 1
- unknown unit id: `` `unknown unit ${id}: the plan names ${ids}` `` — exit 1
- merge: no stored return: `` `${id}: no return stored at returns/${id}.json` `` — **exit 2**
- merge: return not green: `` `${id}: the stored return is ${status}, and only a green return is merged` `` — **exit 2**
- merge: unmet dependencies: `` `${id}: ${deps} not merged yet; merge every dependency first` `` — **exit 2**
- merge: wrong branch checked out: `` `${id}: the repository is on ${on} and the run branch is ${runBranch}` `` — **exit 2**
- merge: unit branch doesn't exist: `` `${id}: branch ${branch} does not exist` `` — **exit 2**
- merge conflicts (aborted automatically): `` `${id}: the merge of ${branch} conflicts in ${where}; the merge was aborted` `` — **exit 2**
- merge: unit checks red on merged tree (aborted): `` `${id}: ${...} on the merged tree; the merge was aborted` `` — **exit 2**

### `fc critic render` / `fc verifier render` (`critic.mjs`, `verifier.mjs`, both via `worker/render.mjs`)
- wrong phase: `` `fc critic render: the launch is in phase ${phase}, and a review pass is dispatched in phase review` `` (analogous for verifier/`verify`)
- `currentEvidence` stale/missing (`worker/render.mjs:344-357`): `'evidence/summary.json has not been written: run fc check all first'`, or `` `evidence/summary.json was recorded at ${commit} and HEAD is ${head}: run fc check all again` ``
- no pinned spec: `'the launch pins no spec'` / `` `the pinned spec ${path} is not in the launch folder` ``

### `fc return &lt;kind&gt; &lt;file&gt;` (`return.mjs`, `worker/return.mjs`)
- unknown kind: `` `fc return: expected a kind, one of worker, explorer, verifier, critic` `` — exit 1
- `--resolve` on non-critic kind: `'fc return --resolve applies to a critic return'` — exit 1
- resolve with no id: `'fc return critic --resolve needs a finding id'`; with id but no `--commit`/`--dispute`: `` `fc return critic --resolve ${id} needs --commit &lt;sha&gt; or --dispute "&lt;reason&gt;"` `` — exit 1
- return file unreadable/not JSON: `` `${file} could not be read: ...` `` (exit 1) or `` `${file} is not valid JSON: ...` `` (**blocking, exit 2**)
- return fails its kind's schema: `` `${kind} return does not validate: ${errors}` `` — **exit 2** (nothing written)
- can't determine identifier: `` `fc return ${kind} needs --${flag} &lt;value&gt;: the file does not name one` `` — exit 1

### `fc check` (`check.mjs`)
- no tests map pinned: `'no tests map pinned'` — exit 1 (`CheckError`, default usage code)
- pin mismatch vs `launch.json.spec`: `` `pin mismatch: the pinned tests map names a different spec (...)` `` — exit 1
- unknown check id requested: `` `unknown check ${ids}: the pinned tests map has no such id` `` — exit 1
- any check ends `fail`/`error`: reported as `` red: &lt;id&gt; &lt;verdict&gt;, ... `` — **exit 2**
- `--baseline` mode, a command couldn't even run: `` `${n} command(s) could not be run` `` — **exit 2**

### `fc boundary` / `fc locked`
- `--base &lt;commit&gt;` doesn't resolve: `` `fc boundary: --base ${base} does not resolve to a commit` `` (and analogous for `locked`) — exit 1
- outside/locked paths found: exit 2 with the offending list printed

### `fc budget`
- any counter exceeds its ceiling: writes an abandon `trigger` event and `` `budget exceeded: ${...}` `` — **exit 2**. Ceilings come from `launch.json.ceilings`; a `null` ceiling is reported but never enforced (`budget.mjs:110-117`).

### `fc verify`
- aggregates checks/boundary/locked/budget; exit 2 if any is *blocked*, exit 1 if any errored for a non-blocking reason (e.g. runner missing), else 0.

### Launch-resolution errors (`checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs`, surfaced by `bin/fc.mjs:278-283, 333-341`) — all exit **1**
- `NoRoot`: `'no flightdeck root'` — no source in `FLIGHTCREW_ROOT → CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR → cwd → script` names a directory containing `flightdeck/launch/`.
- `NoLaunch`: `'no active launch'` — no `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH` set and no (or zero) launch with `status: active`; also what you get if you explicitly set `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=none`.
- `TwoActive`: printed specially as `` two launches are active: X, Y; pass --launch &lt;name&gt; or set FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH `` (`fc.mjs:280`).
- `MissingLaunch`: printed specially as `` no launch folder named ${name} `` (`fc.mjs:281`) — `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH` names a folder that doesn't exist.
- `Unreadable`: `` `${file} could not be read/parsed: ${message}` `` — a launch's `launch.json` is corrupt.

---

## 3. Every flag that's easy to miss

| flag | where | what it does / when you need it |
|---|---|---|
| `--allow-draft` | `launch activate`, `launch pin tests-map` | Waives the "spec/tests-map must be frozen (carry a `commit`)" check. Needed while iterating before a real freeze; sets `launch.json.allow_draft: true`, which is then echoed in every report/kickoff and waives `tm-invariant-12` in the map validator. |
| `--force` | `launch phase`, `launch gate` | `phase`: skips both the "next phase only" check and the `planBlockers`/`reviewBlockers` gate — use only when you know exactly why the run should jump. `gate`: lets you re-record an already-decided gate, or approve G1/G2 from a phase other than their expected source phase (records the decision but does **not** move the phase in that case). |
| `--name` | `launch new` | Overrides the default `&lt;spec-name&gt;-&lt;n&gt;` folder name — needed when running the same spec twice concurrently isn't possible (only one active launch allowed) but you still want a memorable folder name, or to avoid a name collision. |
| `--kickoff` | `launch new` | `base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;` — picks which library kickoff parts get assembled. Default is `base+shape-session+task-feature` (`DEFAULT_PARTS`, `launch.mjs:45`). Needed the moment you're not doing a plain session/feature build. |
| `--branch` | `launch new` | Sets `launch.json.branch` (default `run/&lt;name&gt;`). Matters because `fc worker merge` refuses if the repo isn't checked out on exactly this branch, and `fc launch end` warns if it differs from the checked-out branch. |
| `--allow` | `launch new` (array, repeatable) | Seeds `paths.allowed` before a tests map is pinned. Once you `pin tests-map`, `paths.allowed` is overwritten from the map's `allowed_paths` anyway (`launch.mjs:439-447`), so this only matters for the brief window before pinning. |
| `--at` | `launch end` | Free-text "stage" recorded on the `launch_end` event's detail (`launch.mjs:832`) — purely descriptive, printed by `gate ... exit` as a suggested value (`fc launch end abandoned --at G3`). |
| `--units` | `launch end partial` | Required for `partial` outcome; comma-separated list of unit ids to keep — everything else is recorded as `abandoned_units`. Must pass `partialBlockers` (§2). |
| `--base` | `boundary`, `locked` | Measures the change set from an explicit commit instead of `lock_commit`/`base_commit`. Use when re-checking a range that isn't "since the pin". |
| `--baseline` | `check` | Runs a **draft** map's checks without any launch/pin and records `observed` lines + `baseline.commit`/`baseline.date` into the map file itself, writing **no evidence**. This is how you first populate a tests map's baselines before you ever pin it (`needsLaunch` is false for this mode, `check.mjs:29`). |
| `--stdin` | `plan write` | Reads the candidate plan JSON from stdin instead of a file path — useful when an agent pipes its plan straight in. |
| `--json` | global | Turns on machine-readable output: `ok()`/`warn()` become silent, commands print one JSON document via `json()` instead (`checks/lib/output.mjs:20-28`). Needed for scripting around `fc launch status`, `fc events summary`, etc. |
| `--launch &lt;name&gt;` | global | Overrides `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH` for one invocation without exporting it — handy when you're poking at a launch other than your exported active one. |
| `--target` | `doctor`, `distribute` | `doctor --target &lt;dir&gt;`: additionally checks a project's already-distributed `.claude` directory against this installation (agents byte-equal, hooks present, interpreter resolvable, `.gitignore` has `.claude/worktrees/`). `distribute --target &lt;dir&gt;`: where to copy crew/workflows; **required** if you also pass `--apply` (`distribute.mjs:104-109`). |
| `--apply` | `distribute` | Without it, `distribute` only lists what *would* be copied (dry run). With it, actually copies crew `.md` files and workflow `.js` files, then prints the settings-fragment JSON and constitution text you must paste in by hand. |
| `--resolve-commits` | `validate launch` (forwarded flag on `validate-launch.mjs`) | Checks that every recorded commit hash actually resolves in the repository, not just that it has the right shape. Needed for a real freeze-time check; skip it for fast structural-only validation. |
| `--strict` | `validate spec/tests-map/launch`, `lint spec` (forwarded) | Turns validator *warnings* into failures (exit 2 instead of 0). Use right before freezing, not during drafting. |
| `--for-freeze` | `validate spec` | "Rehearses the freeze gate against a draft" spec (`validate-spec.mjs:8`) — the step named explicitly in the task's pre-launch sequence. |
| `--only` | *(not found anywhere in `bin/cmd/*.mjs` or `bin/fc.mjs`)* | No `--only` flag exists in this command surface — check the validators/checks scripts under `checks/` if the primer needs it; it is not part of `fc`'s own argument parsing. |

---

## 4. Environment variables

Resolution order for the launch **root** (`checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs:31, 100-112`): `FLIGHTCREW_ROOT → CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR → cwd (git toplevel) → script's own repo`. **The first source that names a value is final** — if that candidate doesn't contain `flightdeck/launch/`, resolution fails as `NoRoot` rather than falling through to the next source.

- **`FLIGHTCREW_ROOT`** — Set this explicitly when you're invoking `fc` from outside the flightdeck repo (e.g. a script, a CI job, or a hook that has no reliable cwd) and want to be certain which repository it acts on, overriding everything else including `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`.
- **`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`** — Set (or rely on Claude Code to set) this when running inside a Claude Code session; it's the second fallback and is what hooks use exclusively (`launch-lib.mjs:14-15`: "Hooks pass `sources: ['CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR']`, because a hook with `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` unset is a silent no-op"). You need it set for hooks to do anything at all.
- **`FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH`** — Names which launch folder `fc` acts on, overriding the "unique active launch" auto-detection. Set it:
  - after `fc launch new` prints `export FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;name&gt;` (step 5 above) — this is the normal first-time use;
  - whenever two launches could be active/ambiguous (`TwoActive` error tells you to);
  - to the literal value **`none`** to deliberately force "no active launch" (`launch-lib.mjs:174-175`) — e.g. to run `fc doctor` (`checkSampleLaunch` in `doctor.mjs:185` explicitly sets `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH: 'none'` when validating the sample launch fixture) or any other launch-independent check without accidentally picking up whatever launch happens to be active.

---

## 5. Idempotent / safe-to-re-run vs. state-changing

**Safe to run freely, any time, to look around (read-only or self-overwriting with no side effects on the repo):**
- `fc launch status`, `fc launch status --json`
- `fc doctor`, `fc doctor --target &lt;dir&gt;`
- `fc distribute` (no `--apply` — dry-run listing only)
- `fc validate &lt;kind&gt; [path]`, `fc lint spec &lt;path&gt;` — pure checks, write nothing
- `fc events summary`
- `fc runlog show [--spec S]`
- `fc evidence`, `fc report`, `fc plan render` — each is a **pure re-render** from stored JSON (`plan.json`, `launch.json`, `events.jsonl`, `evidence/*.json`); re-running produces byte-identical output when nothing underlying changed (explicitly asserted for kickoff at `launch.mjs:157-159`, for plan at `plan.mjs:6-7` "spec B21", and evidence/report simply reload `loadLaunchData`).
- `fc boundary [--base]`, `fc locked [--base]`, `fc budget`, `fc check &lt;ids&gt;` — these do write evidence files (`evidence/boundary.json`, `evidence/locked.json`, `evidence/budget.json`, `evidence/&lt;T&gt;.json`) and rebuild `summary.json`, but re-running them just overwrites those files with the current truth — safe to repeat as often as you like, and in fact this is exactly how you clear a "phase refused" blocker. `fc budget` is the one exception with a real side effect: exceeding a ceiling **appends an irreversible `trigger` event** to `events.jsonl` each time it's exceeded, which is not itself destructive but does start blocking phase/dispatch commands until cleared.
- `fc worker render`, `fc critic render`, `fc verifier render` — write a `.prompt.md` file but don't change any state; re-rendering the same pass number overwrites the prompt file harmlessly (though re-running `render` with no `--pass` picks the *next* pass number each time, so it's not literally idempotent output-wise, just non-destructive).

**Changes state, and some of it is not reversible by any `fc` command:**
- `fc launch new` — creates the launch folder; re-running with the same `--name`/default name refuses (`launch exists`), so it's naturally guarded against accidental duplication, but there's no `fc launch delete`.
- `fc launch activate`, `fc launch pin tests-map`, `fc launch kickoff`, `fc launch phase &lt;p&gt;`, `fc launch gate &lt;G&gt; &lt;decision&gt;`, `fc launch escalate`, `fc launch note`, `fc launch land`, `fc launch end` — every one of these appends to `events.jsonl` (append-only, no undo) and/or rewrites `launch.json`. Phase and gate moves are meant to be one-directional (`isNextPhase`); `--force` lets you defeat that, but there's no "go back a phase" command — the log is the only record of what happened.
- `fc worker merge` — **destructive in the repository, not just the launch folder**: it runs a real `git merge --no-ff --commit`, deletes the unit's git worktree (`git worktree remove --force`) and deletes the unit branch (`git branch -D`) on success. A failed merge is cleanly aborted (`git merge --abort`) and nothing is left dirty, but a *successful* merge is a real commit on your run branch that only `git reset`/`git revert` (outside `fc`) can undo.
- `fc return &lt;kind&gt; &lt;file&gt;` / `fc return critic --resolve` — overwrites the fixed-path return/resolutions file for that identifier; re-running with a different file for the same unit/pass silently replaces the stored return (there's no versioning of returns beyond the events log).
- `fc distribute --apply [--force]` — copies files into another project's `.claude/` directory; refuses on conflicting files unless `--force`, which then overwrites them.
- `fc launch end` outcome is final for that launch (sets `status`/`outcome`/`ended`, moves phase to `ended`) and triggers the one-way cleanup instructions (worktree removal / branch deletion) it prints for you to run yourself.</result>

from html.parser import HTMLParser
import re
VOID={'area','base','br','col','embed','hr','img','input','link','meta','source','track','wbr'}
class P(HTMLParser):
    def __init__(s):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True); s.stack=[]; s.errs=[]
    def handle_starttag(s,t,a):
        if t not in VOID: s.stack.append((t,s.getpos()))
    def handle_endtag(s,t):
        if t in VOID: return
        if not s.stack: s.errs.append(f"stray </{t}> {s.getpos()}"); return
        if s.stack[-1][0]!=t:
            s.errs.append(f"</{t}> at {s.getpos()} but open is <{s.stack[-1][0]}> from {s.stack[-1][1]}")
            for i in range(len(s.stack)-1,-1,-1):
                if s.stack[i][0]==t: del s.stack[i:]; return
        else: s.stack.pop()
f='flightcrew-operators-manual.html'
src=open(f).read(); p=P(); p.feed(src)
print("unclosed:",[x[0] for x in p.stack] or "none")
print("errors:",p.errs[:8] or "none")

declared=set(re.findall(r'(--[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:',root))
used=set(re.findall(r'var\((--[a-z0-9-]+)',src))
print("undeclared tokens:",sorted(used-declared) or "none")
dead=[h for h in re.findall(r'href="#([a-z0-9]+)"',src) if f'id="{h}"' not in src]
print("dead links:",dead or "none")

## 1. Where the tree fragment comes from

It's real, quoted verbatim from **`flightdeck/STRUCTURE.md`** (current `main` branch), lines 35–38:

```
├── launch/                            runs — orchestrated build runs, self contained
│   └── example-launch/
│       ├── checks/
│       └── specs/
├── manuals/                            documentation — system manuals the crew reads regularly
```

I checked the other candidate sources and none of them contain it:
- `flightdeck/launch/README.md` — does not exist on `main`. It exists on the `flightcrew-buildout` / `flightcrew-buildout-v1` branches (commit `9131265`), where it describes something different (below).
- Root `README.md` — 4 lines, unrelated.
- `flightdeck/flightcrew/README.md` — doesn't exist (only a top-level `./README.md`).
- `flightdeck/manuals/launch/launch-anatomy.md` — does not exist on `main` either; also only present on the buildout branches.

So on `main`, `STRUCTURE.md`'s `example-launch/` is a purely illustrative placeholder — `flightdeck/launch/` on `main` currently holds no folder called `example-launch` at all (real contents are `flightcrew-buildout/` and `flightcrew-buildout-2/`, per `flightdeck/launch/runs.keep`).

## 2. Is `checks/` real inside a launch run folder?

**No** — not as a child of the launch run folder itself. Concretely:

- `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/` (real launch on `main`) contains: `escalation.json`, `events.jsonl`, `evidence.html`, `evidence/`, `kickoff.md`, `launch.json`, `notes.md`, `plan.json`, `plan.md`, `report.md`, `returns/`, `specs/flightcrew-v1/`. **No `checks/`.**
- `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/` — only `returns/`. No `checks/`.
- `flightdeck/testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/` (on the buildout branch, `git show 9131265:flightdeck/testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/`) contains `events.jsonl`, `evidence/`, `kickoff.md`, `launch.json`, `notes.md`, `plan.json`, `plan.md`, `returns/`, `review/`, `specs/export-html/{spec.v1.json,tests-map.v1.json}`. **No `checks/`.**
- Code check: `fc launch new` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/launch.mjs:269-273`, on the buildout branch) only ever creates `evidence/`, `returns/`, `review/`, and `specs/&lt;name&gt;/` inside a launch dir:
```js
const specCopy = path.join(dir, 'specs', specName, path.basename(specFile));
fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(specCopy), { recursive: true });
fs.copyFileSync(specFile, specCopy);
for (const child of ['evidence', 'returns', 'review']) fs.mkdirSync(path.join(dir, child), { recursive: true });
```
Nothing writes to `&lt;launch&gt;/checks/`. Grepping `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin` and `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks` for any write to a `checks/` path inside a launch turns up nothing — every `checks/lib/...` hit is an *import path* (code living in the global `flightcrew/checks/lib/`), not a launch-local `checks/` directory.

The `checks/` that *is* real lives one level up: `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/` — the universal, cross-launch check harnesses (`validators/`, `gates/`, `lib/`), described by `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/checks.keep`: "Universal check harnesses, infrastructure for every launch."

There is one place `checks/` is documented as belonging to a launch-adjacent folder, but it's **`flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/checks/`**, not `flightdeck/launch/&lt;launch&gt;/checks/`. From `flightdeck/launch/README.md` at commit `9131265` (buildout branch):

&gt; `specs/&lt;name&gt;/`: the canonical, cross-run home of one spec: `spec.v*.json`, `tests-map.v*.json`, `checks/` for check scripts with no natural project home, and `interview/`, which is never copied anywhere.

So `STRUCTURE.md`'s tree fragment appears to have conflated "a launch folder has a `checks/` and a `specs/`" with the real rule "the **spec's own folder** (`launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/`) may hold a `checks/` and an `interview/`, and a **launch run** holds a `specs/` copy." The doc is stale/aspirational at best, and on `main` there is no working code, README, or example that puts `checks/` directly under a launch run folder. I'd call this a documentation bug in `STRUCTURE.md`.

## 3. Canonical home of a spec — yes, confirmed, with more detail

`flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/`, sibling to the launch run folders (not inside any of them), is exactly right — confirmed both by the (buildout-branch) `launch/README.md` and by `launch-anatomy.md`:

&gt; "The canonical spec home is `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;S&gt;/` (`spec.v*.json`, `tests-map.v*.json`, `checks/`, `interview/`); a launch holds copies, never the originals." — `flightdeck/manuals/launch/launch-anatomy.md` (buildout branch)

Real example on `main`: `flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/` contains `design.md`, `spec.v1.json`, `tests-map.v1.json`, `tests-map.v2.json` (confirmed via `git ls-tree`).

What `fc launch new` actually copies out of that folder, per `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/launch.mjs`:
- **`newLaunch()`** (line ~269): copies only `spec.v*.json` (`path.basename(specFile)`) into `&lt;launch&gt;/specs/&lt;specName&gt;/`. `design.md`, `tests-map.*`, `checks/`, `interview/` are **not** touched.
- **`pin()` / `fc launch pin tests-map`** (line ~424-430): later copies only `tests-map.v*.json` into the same `&lt;launch&gt;/specs/&lt;specName&gt;/` folder, and locks both the launch's own copy (`flightdeck/launch/${name}/specs/**`) and the canonical original (`flightdeck/launch/specs/${specName}/**`) once pinned.
- **`design.md` stays behind** in `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/` — it is never copied into a launch.
- **`interview/`** — the README explicitly says it "is never copied anywhere." It never leaves the canonical spec folder.
- **`checks/`** in the canonical spec folder — also never copied into a launch run folder by this code path (nor referenced in `newLaunch`/`pin` at all).

## 4. The pinned copy vs the canonical original

- They are **byte-identical at copy time** — `fs.copyFileSync(specFile, specCopy)` / `fs.copyFileSync(mapFile, copy)` are exact byte copies, no transformation.
- After that, they can diverge only if the canonical original is edited later, which the design tries to prevent: once `fc launch pin tests-map` runs, `launchJson.paths.locked` includes both `flightdeck/launch/${name}/specs/**` (the pinned copy) *and* `flightdeck/launch/specs/${specName}/**` (the canonical original), so the boundary/hook machinery locks both paths for the run's duration.
- **Which one do the validators and the critic read? The pinned copy inside the launch, not the canonical original.** Concretely:
  - `fc validate spec` / `fc validate tests-map` with no path given resolves via `defaultTarget()` in `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/validate.mjs`: `path.join(dir, json.spec.path)` / `path.join(dir, json.tests_map.path)` — `dir` is the **launch** dir, and `json.spec.path` / `json.tests_map.path` are the relative paths written by `newLaunch`/`pin`, i.e. `specs/&lt;name&gt;/spec.v1.json` *relative to the launch folder*.
  - `fc critic render` reads via `readPinnedSpecPath(launch)` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/worker/render.mjs:327-333`), which does `path.resolve(launch.dir, launch.json.spec.path)` — again the launch's own copy.
  - `fc check` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/cmd/check.mjs`, `pinnedMap()`) resolves `path.resolve(launchDir, pin.path)` the same way.
  - The canonical original under `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;S&gt;/` is only read directly when a path is given explicitly — e.g. before a launch exists, for linting/freezing (`fc lint spec flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;S&gt;/spec.v1.json`), or the one-time copy at `new`/`pin` time.

## 5. Before any launch exists

Nothing in the code creates `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/` for you. `fc launch new` only ever *reads* from an existing spec path (it takes `&lt;spec-path&gt;` as an argument and errors with `no spec at ${given}` if it isn't there — `launch.mjs`, `newLaunch()`) and *writes* into the new launch dir. There is no `fc spec new` / `fc spec init` command in the codebase I found.

So a brand-new spec draft's very first physical home is wherever the author (or the spec-interviewer/spec-builder crew) chooses to create it by hand — and by convention, and by every real example in the repo (`flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/`, `flightdeck/testbench/fixtures/sample-launch/specs/export-html/`), that means the author manually creates `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/` and drops `spec.v1.json` (and, per the README, an `interview/` folder for interview transcripts) there — before any launch folder exists, before it's frozen, and long before `fc launch new` ever runs. Only after the spec is frozen and someone runs `fc launch new flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/spec.v1.json` does a launch folder get created and a copy taken.

## Bottom line on the user's quoted fragment

- The `example-launch/{checks/, specs/}` fragment is verbatim from `flightdeck/STRUCTURE.md:35-38` on `main`.
- `specs/` under a launch is real and accurate (pinned copies).
- `checks/` under a launch run folder is **not real** anywhere in the codebase or examples — it doesn't exist in either real launch on `main`, isn't created by `fc launch new`/`pin`, and isn't mentioned by `launch-anatomy.md`'s actual folder-layout listing for `&lt;launch&gt;/`. The one legitimate `checks/` in this whole area belongs to `flightdeck/launch/specs/&lt;name&gt;/checks/` (the canonical spec folder, per the buildout-branch `launch/README.md`), which `STRUCTURE.md` appears to have telescoped incorrectly into the launch run folder itself. Treat `STRUCTURE.md`'s tree as stale/aspirational on this one point and correct it to show `checks/` (if kept at all) under `specs/&lt;name&gt;/`, not under `example-launch/` directly.
- The "canonical home is `flightdeck/launch/specs/`" claim is correct, sibling to the launch folders, and richer than just spec+tests-map: it can also hold `design.md`, an `interview/` folder (never copied out), and a `checks/` folder for check scripts with no natural project home (also never referenced by the copy code, so effectively unused/aspirational today).</result>

<result>## Preliminary note on location

None of the paths named in the question exist in the currently checked-out tree (`main`, HEAD `5300f0d`). They exist only on the `flightcrew-buildout` / `flightcrew-buildout-v1` branches, at commit `75b2b426` (`git show 75b2b426:flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/README.md` etc. — confirmed via `git branch --all --contains 75b2b426` → `flightcrew-buildout`, `remotes/origin/flightcrew-buildout`). ## 1. The complete part inventory

Eight files total: `base.md` + 3 shape parts + 4 task parts (plus the non-rendered `README.md`).

- `base.md` — **mandatory, always included, never optional.** `loadParts` (launch.mjs:131) hard-requires it first: `if (names[0] !== 'base') throw new UsageError(...)`.
- Shape (choose exactly one, by convention/doc, **not enforced** as "exactly one" in code): `shape-session.md`, `shape-workflow.md`, `shape-sessions.md`.
- Task (choose exactly one, by convention/doc, **not enforced** as "exactly one" in code): `task-feature.md`, `task-migration.md`, `task-audit.md`, `task-agent.md`.

Code reality (`renderKickoff`, launch.mjs:161‑165):
```
const shape = parts.find((part) =&gt; part.name.startsWith('shape-'));
const task = parts.find((part) =&gt; part.name.startsWith('task-'));
if (!shape || !task) throw new UsageError(`a kickoff names one shape-&lt;s&gt; and one task-&lt;t&gt; part; got ${partsSpec}`);
```
`.find()` only checks that **at least one** part matching each prefix exists — it takes the first match for the header's title/version-string derivation. Zero task parts (or zero shape parts) throws (`!shape || !task`). But **two shape parts, or two task parts, are not rejected**: `loadParts` has no dedup or count check, and `renderKickoff`'s body assembly (`parts.map((part) =&gt; part.text).join('\n\n')`) just concatenates every part that was named, including duplicates/extras. So a launch *can* be given `base+shape-session+shape-workflow+task-feature+task-migration` and it will render — the header title will just read the first shape/task found, but both bodies get glued in.

What the validator (`validate-kickoff.mjs`) actually enforces is only about the **rendered header and Roles section**, not part composition:
- Rule 1: title line matches `^Kickoff:\s*\S` and the header carries every field in `FIELDS = ['launch','spec','tests-map','kickoff version','read first','prior reports','write plan with','evidence']`.
- Rule 2/3: the `spec`/`tests-map` pointer paths resolve, and their commit is a 7–40 char hex hash or `draft` (only if `launch.json.allow_draft`).
- Rule 4: every `prior reports` path resolves.
- Rule 5: every backticked name under `## Roles` has a matching `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/&lt;name&gt;.md`.

Nothing in the validator checks which shape/task parts were used, how many, or that they came from the fixed set of 7 — it validates the *rendered output shape*, not the *part selection*.

## 2. Is there any interactive kickoff step at all?

**No.** I grepped every kickoff-related file (`README.md`, all 8 parts, `launch.mjs`, `validate-kickoff.mjs`) for `AskUserQuestion`, `&lt;fill`, `fill-in`, `placeholder`, `TODO`, `TBD`, `prompt the user`, `ask the user`, `human fills`, `interview` — zero hits anywhere.

The mechanism is purely mechanical file concatenation. Direct evidence:
- README.md:9 — "`kickoff.md` is rendered, never hand-edited. A conduct change is an edit to a part here, with that part's version bumped; the next render picks it up."
- launch.mjs:7‑8 (file header comment) — "Nothing here is hand-written by an agent: kickoff.md and the pinned copies are produced by these commands, which is why re-rendering a kickoff restores it byte for byte."
- base.md's own "Never" list bans hand-editing it: "hand-edit kickoff.md or plan.md (both are re-rendered from their sources)."

The only "human" involvement in a kickoff's *content* is choosing which pre-authored part names to pass on the `--kickoff`/`--parts` flag (or accepting the default). There is no interview, no `AskUserQuestion` call, no fill-in-blank template anywhere in this pipeline — it is `fs.readFileSync` + string concatenation (`renderKickoff`, launch.mjs:161‑183).

## 3. What IS configurable per run, and where does it live

The kickoff parts (`base.md`/`shape-*.md`/`task-*.md`) are fixed prose — not per-run parameters. All actual per-run configuration lives in `launch.json` (schema: `flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/launch.schema.json`; defaults sourced in `defaultsFor`, launch.mjs:221‑229 from `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/launch.template.json`) and in `plan.json`. Concretely, per-run knobs an operator can turn:

| Knob | Set by | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|
| which spec is run | `fc launch new &lt;spec-path&gt;` | `launch.json.spec.{name,version,commit,path}` |
| launch name | `--name` (else auto `defaultName`) | `launch.json.name` |
| kickoff part selection | `--kickoff base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;` on `new`, or `--parts` on `fc launch kickoff` | `launch.json.kickoff.version`, drives which files get concatenated |
| integration branch | `--branch B` (else `run/&lt;name&gt;`) | `launch.json.branch` |
| pre-declared writable globs | `--allow &lt;glob&gt;`... (repeatable) | `launch.json.paths.allowed` (before pin) |
| tests-map pin, which sets allowed/locked paths | `fc launch pin tests-map &lt;path&gt;` reading `allowed_paths`/`locked_paths` off the tests-map JSON | `launch.json.paths.{allowed,locked}`, `enforce_boundary` (pin.mjs:439‑447) |
| draft acceptance | `--allow-draft` on `activate`/`pin` | `launch.json.allow_draft` |
| acceptance check id | `launch.template.json` (`acceptance`, defaults to `T1`) | `launch.json.acceptance` |
| structural per-extension lint/check commands | `launch.template.json.structural`, merged over `BUILT_IN_DEFAULTS.structural` | `launch.json.structural` |
| budget ceilings: `agents`, `implementers_concurrent`, `turns_per_agent`, `gate_iterations`, `stop_blocks`, `critic_passes`, `minutes`, `tokens`, `expected_tokens` | `launch.template.json.ceilings`, merged over `BUILT_IN_DEFAULTS.ceilings` (launch.mjs:56‑67, 221‑229); the plan may *tighten* but never loosen them (base.md: "Dispatch caps are defaults the plan may tighten and may not loosen") | `launch.json.ceilings`, echoed/tightened in `plan.json.expected_cost` |
| the actual work breakdown: waves, units, unit `kind`/`checks`/`paths`/`budget_turns`/`depends_on`/`pilot`, models used per role, risks, gate criteria, abandon triggers, `no_contracts` | authored by the planner agent and validated/stored via `fc plan write` | `plan.json` / rendered `plan.md` (schema `plan.template.json`) |
| gate approvals | `fc launch gate G1|G2|G3 approve|exit [--note]` | `launch.json.gates.*` |
| run ending / outcome | `fc launch end &lt;accepted|accepted-with-reservations|abandoned|partial&gt; [--units]` | `launch.json.outcome`, `ended`, `accepted_units`/`abandoned_units` |

So the answer to "what's configurable" is: essentially everything about *scope, ceilings, paths, branch, models, unit cutting, gates* is configured through `launch.json` + `plan.json`, not through the kickoff templates. The kickoff templates only encode fixed *process prose* ("how to conduct"), which is why the README (§"Rules for a part") explicitly draws that boundary: "A rule for one run belongs in the plan," and the header block points at, rather than duplicates, all of this run-specific state.

## 4. The generated header block

`renderKickoff` (launch.mjs:161‑183) computes the entire header purely from run-time data — none of it is template text:

```js
const version = parts.map((part) =&gt; `${part.name}@${part.version}`).join('+');
const launchRel = repoPath(ctx, launchDir);
const specPath = repoPath(ctx, path.resolve(launchDir, launchJson.spec?.path ?? ''));
const specLine = `${specPath} @ ${launchJson.spec?.commit ?? 'draft'}`;
const mapLine = launchJson.tests_map
  ? `${repoPath(ctx, path.resolve(launchDir, launchJson.tests_map.path ?? ''))} @ ${launchJson.tests_map.commit ?? 'draft'}`
  : '(none)';
const priors = priorReports(ctx, launchJson);
const header = [
  `# Kickoff: ${task.name} · ${shape.name}`,
  `launch: ${launchRel}    spec: ${specLine}    tests-map: ${mapLine}`,
  `kickoff version: ${version}`,
  `read first: ${repoPath(ctx, path.join(ctx.launchDir, 'RUNLOG.md'))}    prior reports: ${priors.length &gt; 0 ? priors.join(', ') : 'none'}`,
  `write plan with: fc plan write    evidence: ${launchRel}/evidence.html`,
].join('\n');
```

What's injected and not from any template file:
- the launch folder's repo-relative path
- the spec's path **and its pinned commit** (or `draft`)
- the tests-map path and commit, or the literal `(none)` if unpinned (`launchJson.tests_map` check)
- the **assembled version string**, built by concatenating each loaded part's own `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;` line into `part@version` joined by `+` (`partVersion`, launch.mjs:118‑121, used at line 137)
- the run-log pointer (`flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md`) — a fixed "read first" pointer, computed via `repoPath`
- **prior reports**: `priorReports()` (launch.mjs:145‑154) scans every *other* launch folder under `ctx.launchDir`, keeps those whose `launchJson.spec.name` matches this one, and lists the repo-relative path of each one's `report.md` if it exists, sorted — a live cross-run query, not static content
- fixed pointers to `fc plan write` and the evidence page path

Real header, from `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/kickoff.md`:
```
# Kickoff: task-feature · shape-workflow
launch: flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout    spec: flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/specs/flightcrew-v1/spec.v1.json @ 5f69a94    tests-map: flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/specs/flightcrew-v1/tests-map.v1.json @ 44aff6b
kickoff version: base@1+shape-workflow@1+task-feature@1
read first: flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md    prior reports: none
write plan with: fc plan write    evidence: flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/evidence.html
```

## 5. How would an operator change how runs are conducted?

Confirmed and quoted (README.md:9): **"`kickoff.md` is rendered, never hand-edited. A conduct change is an edit to a part here, with that part's version bumped; the next render picks it up."**

And the versioning rule that makes this traceable (README.md:19): "The first line is `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;` and nothing else. The assembled kickoff version is the `+`-joined list of `part@version`, and it is recorded in the launch's `launch.json`, in the `kickoff_version` field of the launch's `plan.json`, and in the run-log entry."

Can an operator add a **new** shape/task part of their own? Yes, mechanically, with nothing registering the name anywhere:
- `ctx.fd.kickoff` (`flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc.mjs:69`) is just `path.join(templates, 'kickoff')` — a directory.
- `loadParts` (launch.mjs:128‑142) does a plain `path.join(ctx.fd.kickoff, \`${name}.md\`)` + `isFile` check — it does not consult any registry/enum of legal part names.
- The only rules a new part must satisfy: (a) filename gives it a `shape-` or `task-` prefix for `renderKickoff`'s `.find()` to pick it up as the shape/task; (b) first line is exactly `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;`; (c) any agent name it puts in backticks under a `## Roles` heading must have a matching `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/&lt;name&gt;.md` (rule 5 in `validate-kickoff.mjs`) — but `task-*` files in this library don't even carry `## Roles` sections (only `base.md` does), so this constraint mostly wouldn't apply to a new task part.
- No code lists `['task-feature','task-migration','task-audit','task-agent']` or `['shape-session','shape-workflow','shape-sessions']` as an enum anywhere (`grep -n "shape-\|task-" launch.mjs` turns up only the `.startsWith()` prefix checks and the doc/help strings, no enumerations).

So the "closed set of 7" is a closed set only in the sense that the README documents 7 and only 7 exist as files today — it is enforced by *nothing* in code except the `shape-`/`task-`/`base` filename convention and the version-comment format.

## 6. Are the parts composable in other ways — could an operator write `task-refactor.md` and use `--kickoff base+shape-session+task-refactor` with no other change?

**Yes, this would work with no other code change.** Walking the code path for `fc launch new ... --kickoff base+shape-session+task-refactor`:
1. `newLaunch` (launch.mjs:301) passes `flags.kickoff` straight to `renderKickoff`.
2. `loadParts` splits on `+` → `['base','shape-session','task-refactor']`, checks `names[0] === 'base'` ✓, then for each name does `path.join(ctx.fd.kickoff, 'task-refactor.md')`, `isFile` ✓ (once the operator drops the file there), reads it, requires its first line to be `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;` — that's the only structural requirement.
3. `renderKickoff` finds `shape = shape-session`, `task = task-refactor` via `.startsWith()` — both truthy, so it proceeds; the title becomes `# Kickoff: task-refactor · shape-session`; the version string becomes `base@N+shape-session@N+task-refactor@N`.
4. `writeKickoff`/`fs.writeFileSync` writes the file; `validate-kickoff.mjs` only checks header fields, pointer resolution, and Roles-section crew names — none of which reference the specific part name `task-refactor`, so it passes as long as the header block is well-formed (which it is, mechanically, regardless of body content) and any backticked names under `## Roles` (only present via `base.md`, unaffected) resolve.

Nothing needs registering elsewhere — no enum, no JSON schema `enum` field for kickoff part names, no crew.json entry required (crew.json / `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/README.md` governs *agent roles*, not kickoff task/shape names). The "7 options" are a documented convention, not an enforced closed set — the kickoff library is functionally an **extensible library keyed by filename**, not a fixed menu of exactly 7 choices.</result>
<usage><subagent_tokens>80269</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>28</tool_uses><duration_ms>229557</duration_ms></usage>
</task-notification>

[ASSISTANT 💬]
The kickoff agent came back with a definitive answer on question 3 — and it's the opposite of what "7 options as flags" implies.

## On `flightcrew-buildout`: not a closed set, and no prompt anywhere

**No interactive step exists.** The agent grepped every kickoff file and the renderer for `AskUserQuestion`, `<fill`, placeholder, TODO, "ask the user", "interview" — zero hits. It is `readFileSync` plus string concatenation. The README is blunt about it: *"`kickoff.md` is rendered, never hand-edited."* Your only input is which part names you pass.

**But the library is extensible, not a menu of seven.** Nothing in the code enumerates the names. `loadParts` just does `path.join(kickoffDir, name + '.md')` and checks the file exists. Write `task-refactor.md` whose first line is `<!-- version: 1 -->`, then `--kickoff base+shape-session+task-refactor`, and it renders — no registry, no enum, no schema entry, nothing else to change. The only rules are: `base` must be first, at least one `shape-`-prefixed and one `task-`-prefixed part must be present, and every part's first line must be the version comment.

So "7 options" is a documented convention with seven files sitting in a directory, enforced by nothing but filename prefix. I said "closed set" in the manual; that was wrong.

One loose thread worth knowing: `renderKickoff` uses `.find()` for the shape and task, so **two shapes or two tasks aren't rejected** — the header names the first of each, but every part's body gets concatenated in. `base+shape-session+shape-workflow+task-feature` renders happily and gives you a kickoff carrying two contradictory dispatch doctrines.

The header block is entirely computed, not templated — and one part of it is a live query: `priorReports()` scans every other launch folder, keeps the ones running the same spec, and lists their `report.md` paths. That's how a new run gets pointed at its predecessors.

## On `main`: the concept is gone, replaced by a config file

`templates/kickoff/` doesn't exist. `liftoff.template.yaml` does the same job differently — you edit one annotated YAML before the run, uncommenting alternatives. That's a shift from *"pick a pre-authored doctrine"* to *"declare ~80 individual decisions"*, and it moves things that were hardcoded on the buildout branch (git isolation, merge policy, retry policy, which gates are even enabled, the PR-as-final-gate) into per-run configuration.

Your `missions/shape-library.md` reads like the reconciliation of the two: *"Create a library of flightcrew architecture shapes. build a shape, record it forever, reuse when ever"* with `+ spec: + liftoff: + plan:` under it — a named, reusable bundle of all three, which is the kickoff-parts idea generalised over the liftoff config.

Still waiting on the spec-process agent for question 2. And the branch question stands: which of the two do you want documented?
